The chest now holds a stack at most of the bulk things (coal, raw iron and gold, lapis, ingots; sixteen of a wool), moves only what fits, knows when it is full, grows once into a double chest (a second chest beside it with the same facing, taken back up if the game does not join them), and never holds up the portal: with nothing more fitting the trip goes on. The same chest held six blaze rods while the ladder sent the bot to the fortress for rods; eyes, rods, powder and pearls in the stash are now fetched before the portal. The hoglin: both Nether falls began two and three blocks from an edge, a toss the one-cell drop check could not see. With a hoglin, zoglin, ravager, iron golem or warden within ten blocks, a drop within three blocks along a clear line counts, the bot steps back to ground three blocks from any drop, and an escape takes no footing near an edge and no route along one. A new arena, `ledge` (nine wide, twelve above lava), with drills `hoglin_ledge` and `hoglin_ledge_pair`, measures it against the code before.

132. *Four live loops and Nether gold.* After the restart at 16:10: the iron pickaxe wore from twenty-two uses to none in eighteen seconds of the night mine, standing still while the server put the blocks back, and with seventeen ingots and a crafting table carried no spare was made (the spare rule runs only in the day's work); the bot sealed itself in with nothing to dig with. The night mine now replaces a pickaxe under twenty-four uses from the pockets (iron, else stone), mines on when it can make one, and gives up a target after four steps that do not move it. Home for the spare pickaxe, the restock opened and shut the chest once a second for twenty seconds with thirty-six slots taken; a restock now makes room with the lid shut first, and one that takes nothing rests its items. In the Nether the fortress sweep walked ninety blocks out and back every three and a half minutes, the section just patrolled pulling each leg back while in view; that section is now left behind for four minutes, and the leg follows the fortress's long axis. And Nether gold: taken by rule only in mine and tunnel steps, with no piglin check, though piglins turn on a player who breaks gold near them. It is now never broken with a piglin within sixteen blocks, and the Nether's walks (the fortress sweep, its patrols, the walk back to the blazes) stop for gold within four blocks while pearls are short. Arena: the hoglin ledge drill, before and after the toss margin, 12.5 to 6.9 median damage, the lava fall gone.

133. *Parkour, the island and the field cache.* The user noticed the bot never jumped and laid a block over every one-block crack: parkour had been off from the start. It is on now where a miss costs nothing, the pathfinder's own jumps (level, one up, one down; two-block gaps only while sprinting) over gaps with ground or water within five blocks and nothing that burns; deployed at the user's word before its terrain drills ran (`crack_jump`, `crack_over_lava`, queued). Back from the Nether the bot came out onto a sand island in the sea at dusk with one netherrack: no shelter site within thirty-two blocks, no blocks, and a staircase toward ore ran out under the water every time, so the shelter step failed every eight seconds for eight minutes. With no site and no blocks it now digs straight down, from the nearest dry column within four blocks, to a pocket walled in rock and caps it with one block (a sand wall does not count: it falls); failing that, a night mine from where it stands. A drowned chased it off the island before the fix went out. And the user's field cache: with home too far to walk the valuables back to, they go into a chest placed on the spot once the Nether preparations are done (the valuables and keepsakes, not the respawn kit), remembered in goal.caches, and emptied into the pockets when the ladder passes within forty-eight blocks with no crossing next.

134. *Creepers, bolder trips, and pearls.* Two creeper deaths on the mainland at night: one while gathering dirt a block at a time (each block stepped the bot below its planned site, which sent it back up, one dirt a trip, the creeper following for forty seconds) and dying in the half-sealed pocket; one at three health when Jev's stance chose a pillar the creeper walked under. Now: short of blocks for the room, a shaft pocket first, and a one-block dip is not being below the site; a creeper is fought the player's way (close in, hit, back out of the blast, hit again), armed and at eight health or more, a group of them together, and no pillar, pocket or bunker is offered with one within seven blocks. Arena, four runs each before and after: one creeper 4.9 median damage (16.1 worst) to 0 in every run, three hits in 3.5 seconds; a pair 11.1 to 7.3. The first dance version starved the connection (a move whose goal was already met returned without yielding, in a loop) and the arena server timed the bot out; every held-key move now yields once. Parkour jumps up a block across a gap are gone (the live run jumped at one twenty-three times), and gap jumps rest five minutes after three short walks in a minute. Nether supplies in hand no longer skip rebuilding the fighting kit. The flight recorder keeps a day of recordings (two wither-skeleton deaths at 16:07 and 16:27 were pruned by a day of restarts; ServerReplay still has them). Jev's stance is on in the live run for every encounter (the user). And the user found the run dull, walking about after endermen: side trips are now offered past the preparation ladder, with a deep dark expedition among them (stairs to y -52, sweeps for sculk, an ancient city's chests, gone at the first warden), and pearls come from the warped forest first: a remembered one is walked to and hunted in, the Nether is swept for one in eight legs of sixty-four blocks, and the Overworld night hunt is the fallback.

135. *Plan to die, the fourth audit, and animals.* Bold trips pack light (trip-kit.js): all but the trip's kit in a chest held for the trip, emptied when the bot is back by it or fetched as a side trip; trial chambers (keys, vaults, chests) and bastion raids (chests opened whoever is looking) join the deep dark. Pearls come from the warped forest first, the Nether swept for one by ground covered and a quarter of an hour, not by tries. Out of high-banked water: a block placed at the waterline, or a quick step cut into the nearest bank with jump held and the air guarded (a stone step dug from the water drowned the bot). Sixteen blocks and three logs of wood are always carried and made up when short. The fourth audit (18:51-19:11): no deaths, no water, no loops, but 56 percent waiting: a skeleton at the pocket wall kept it shut all night (a lone mob at the wall is now fought), a hillside pocket's night mine opened it three times (the pocket now sinks into rock first), a climb out by hand with no wood, and a dungeon chosen over a missing stone pickaxe (no side trip while a basic tool is next). Three hoglin knockbacks off the ledge by the Nether portal in five minutes: on a ledge with a hoglin close the bot now seals itself in. The pause after three deaths in ten minutes is gone for every run (the user). And animals: a wild wolf tamed with the bones now kept in the pockets (two at most), told to sit before a Nether or End crossing and stood up after; sheep and chickens bred in the field for wool and feathers; each a side trip Jev chooses.

136. *No cheats from here (2026-09-23 21:20 UTC).* The user set the goal plainly: normal survival to the dragon, without cheats. The kit restore on death, the one controlled-run aid left, is stopped; it had handed the kit back through the console 27 times in this world, so this world's progress up to 21:20 is assisted and not acceptance evidence. From 21:20 the run is unaided (Jev is not an operator; the console's only line is the spectator camera). The proof will be one clean run on a fresh world once the middle game holds. The first unaided minutes found two stalls. The night tunnel of the deep-dark trip came up under a cold ocean, and the oak-log search surveyed the sea floor 233 times from a cobblestone pocket on a sand bar, the taiga it had walked the day before a hundred blocks west: at sea with no ground in sight it now swims for the nearest area of land biome it has explored (home if none), until ground comes into view (`crossSea`, shore.js). And the pocket itself never counted as sealed, four cells of sand in the ring under it (sand is never shell), so `sealHere` placed nothing and said it had, fourteen times a second for eight minutes: resting sand or gravel under a pocket is floor now, and a seal that closes nothing gives the spot up for a minute.

137. *What a death costs, and the islets.* The first unaided morning at sea showed the distance rule for "land in sight" was wrong twice over: the sand bar was eleven blocks long, and it was two bars a block of water apart. Own ground is now the dry area joined to the columns under the body (flood-filled, up to an islet's six hundred columns; ice is sea), other islets are skipped, and the crossing is weighed before the survey, which would otherwise walk from one bar to the next. With the restore off a death now costs the kit, so two changes the user asked for. A corpse run (corpse-run.js): after the survival layer's nearby pickup, the kit and supplies the death dropped (armour, iron and diamond tools, rods, pearls, eyes, ore, food; not blocks or stone tools) are walked back for, first thing in the game step. Drops age only while loaded, so a death close to the bed has the game's five minutes, a far one keeps until the bot comes within 128 blocks; a Nether death waits for the next Nether trip, the End is never gone back to. Recovered armour is put back on. And stashing: at home past the ladder, valuables go in the chest before any stage, not only before the Nether (the live bot had carried sixty lapis, thirty-six raw iron and nineteen ingots all day); the keeps hold back the rods, pearls and eyes the stage spends.

138. *The first death without the restore (22:52 UTC).* The sea crossing worked at dawn (sixty blocks swum to a remembered oak, logs, an iron pickaxe, home, the chest, the portal), and the warped-forest search went into the Nether. There a walk dropped the bot twelve blocks onto a ledge (20 to 11 health, cause not recorded: too far for a filtered gap jump, and the flight's last terrain picture was from the Overworld), a piglin fifteen blocks off was the threat, and the survival layer sealed in where it stood: thirty-one cells of shell missing on a ledge over the lava sea. Placing a wall cell its body overlapped, `place` stepped back to clear it and the bot fell twenty-three blocks into the lava. The kit, the diamond sword and the eight rods burned. Walling in beside a drop (within two blocks) now places with `stay`: a cell that needs the bot to step aside, back off or walk closer is left, never moved for. A death is recorded as in lava when there is lava at the body, and a corpse run is not made for it.

139. *Rebuilding, and a bunker too slow to dig (23:16 UTC).* After the lava the stash's spare kit came out (the spare-kit chest paying off for the first time without the restore), a new iron sword was made, and the bot went down its mine for the iron set's twenty-four raw iron without armour. At y 33 two skeletons, a spider and two zombies: "too many of them out here", and the rules dug a bunker, three cells deep and two high, with the pickaxe the rebuild had. Fifteen seconds of digging under arrows (20 to 11.8), then the doorway, then dead at 23:16:48. A bunker is dug now only when the digging, walk to the wall included, takes three seconds or less with the best tool carried (`bunkerDigMs`, from the blocks' own dig times: iron on stone about 2.4 s, a stone pickaxe about 3.6 s, bare hands never). The same bound holds the stance's bunker option. Also from the clean run's death: a stance that failed against these mobs is not offered again for twenty seconds.

140. *Arrived, on farmland.* The dream run stood on its own plot for seven minutes failing to plant (108 "navigation ended before reaching the destination", persist, shake loose, again). Farmland is a block less a sixteenth: the feet floor into it, and `navigate` judged arrival with the floored position, so a walk to the next seed cell that the pathfinder had already finished was "short" every time. Arrival is now judged from the cell the bot stands in (one above a partial block it is standing on, as the pathfinder itself starts), and the home steps do not walk at all when the cell is already within reach. Slabs, soul sand and paths had the same trap.

141. *The fourth gold ingot.* Rebuilding golden boots with three ingots in the pockets, the dream run spent eight minutes looking for nether gold ore in the Overworld. The planner's cost estimate counts carried items as free, and unpacking a carried ingot into nine nuggets made nuggets cost one each: the fourth ingot was planned as nine nuggets on a crafting table, and nuggets can only be mined as nether gold ore. The estimate now leaves out unpacking recipes (one item into many of an item that packs back into it); acquiring still unpacks what is carried. The fourth ingot is gold ore and a smelt. Also: a craft that made nothing (ingredients left on the cursor, both runs every ten minutes or so) is clicked once more before the step fails.

142. *Sealing with a sword at arm's length (00:07 UTC).* The planner's nugget detour took the dream run into the Nether for nether gold, and on the fortress's edge a wither skeleton came to arm's length with two blazes behind it. No route away; the cornered rules saw shooters and sealed a nineteen-cell pocket, block after block with no swing while the sword was within reach, and the bot went from 19.4 to nothing in five seconds. Cornered with a melee mob in reach and health to fight, the tick now ends after the swing that opens every tick, so the next tick swings again; the pocket waits until nothing is in reach. The arena has a `fortress_mix` drill for it (a wither skeleton close, two blazes behind). The death is in the Nether with the rebuilt iron kit, so the corpse run's first real test is the next Nether trip. Arena, three runs each with Jev's stance on (00:16 UTC): fortress_mix no deaths, 15 damage median (the blazes stay up, as a defend drill allows); wither_skeleton_single 1; wither_skeleton_pair 0 (the bunker); cave_trio 7.9. All pass.

143. *Looking with the chunks' eyes.* The warped-forest search walked eight straight legs and looked only at blocks within forty-eight: every chunk loaded round the bot carries its biome, and the explored map already reads them. The detector now falls back to the nearest loaded column of warped forest, sampled every sixteen blocks out to 128. Also seen this hour: the dream base is a lakeside of small flooded pits dug over two days, and the bot fell into one beside its own bed and took two and a half minutes to climb out (step out, notch out, dig to shore, in turn). It got out; left as it is.

144. *The corpse run's first real test (00:49 UTC).* It worked as written and that was the problem: the bot went back into the Nether and walked to the fortress's edge for the kit it had dropped, wearing none of it, and the wither skeleton that killed it there killed it again. A corpse run now waits until the bot is fit: the kit worn for the Nether; in the Overworld the kit or daylight. A far death's drops keep while it waits.

145. *Standing by the furnace (the user: "he's not doing anything").* Twenty raw iron in the base furnace is two hundred seconds, and with no ore within arm's reach of a surface base the bot stood beside it for four minutes. While a batch has thirty seconds or more to go, the bot now walks to an ore within sixteen blocks (else a log, while fewer than sixteen are carried), digs it and comes back, up to twelve times a batch.

146. *An hour of the user's eye (01:30 to 02:30 UTC).* Watching the dream run, two people watching named what an audit would take hours to find. Each is fixed and tested:
- *Iron armour in the pockets.* The spare set came out of the stash after a death and was carried, not worn: armour went on only in the check before a crossing. The best armour carried is now worn at every game step (golden boots on the feet in the Nether), and a carried shield goes on the off-hand.
- *Logs left lying.* Pockets of 32 kinds, three stacks of them cobblestone: a stack is the cap now.
- *A diamond sword on the trees.* Nothing beats a fist on a log, so the sword from the last fight stayed in hand, two uses a log. A weapon or tool is put away for fist work, and a stone axe is made before felling trees when the pockets can make one.
- *Leaf litter as fuel* (the user's idea): measured on the arena server, a hundred ticks each, half an item. It is kept (sixteen, thirty-two before it is surplus) and burned before planks. Blaze rods came off the fuel list: a furnace with no coal could have burned the eyes' rods.
- *Lapis for enchanting* (a watcher's): sixteen are carried, lapis ore is taken in passing while short, and an enchanting table is a side trip once two diamonds and five levels are in hand, not only in the last preparations before the End, where it had never been made in two runs.
- *Wool* (the user's): shears from two ingots, sheep sheared (not killed) for the bed and as a side trip toward fifteen wool.
- *"I think I lost it" and off a bridge.* The corpse run in the Nether charged a wither skeleton four blocks up the fortress and the floor went from under it. A charge is level ground only, not from an edge, and walks without digging, towering or dropping more than two.
- *A mineshaft two blocks away that was not there.* Forty-seven blocks down: loot trips measured the map, and the walk stopped on the grass over a dungeon. Loot trips now keep to structures near the bot's own height.
- *Air.* A drowning's air reading of none outlived the body: the clean run "came up for air" on grass for minutes. A respawn refills it.
- *A skeleton in the pocket.* The clean run sealed itself in with one at 0.3 blocks and waited eighteen minutes: a mob within reach inside the pocket is fought.

147. *Beds against the dragon (the user's).* Measured on the endgame server in iron armour: a bed blown from open ground three or four blocks off killed the clicker; from a trench one block deep, sneaking, bed three off, 4.4 to 6.7 damage, the bed's near side in plain sight (a two-deep trench took none but hid the bed: a click through the ground is not a player's, so it is never used). Four beds are End supplies now, from wool; when the dragon perches the fight may lay one beside its head and blow it from such a trench (bed-bomb.js), ahead of the sword in the fallback order. The rehearsal on a fresh End (the old one moved aside) took five crystals in its first minute, then stopped: in the exit portal's basin with the dragon over it, no route counted as safe and the fight called it Blocked. Unsafe with no way out now holds and looks again whatever the danger. Netherite waits: beds uncover ancient debris in the Nether, but the upgrade needs a smithing template from a bastion's chests.

148. *Chests, blazes, and a head in gravel (02:30 UTC).* The user watched the dream run hunt blazes past five fortress chests: a chest counted only within 48 blocks of a remembered fortress, and fortresses are remembered one per 96 blocks. Now any chest seen that is not the bot's own (the stash, a field cache, one it placed) is opened and remembered as opened, and a Nether chest among nether bricks is a fortress's. A blaze three blocks off was walled against for twelve seconds under fire and called "something shooting": a mob at arm's length, shooter or not, is struck when cornered; a mob is seen if its head, middle or feet is (one ray to one point had a fortress fence hide a blaze); hit by a kind of mob with none in sight, the nearest of that kind is taken as the attacker, as the server's hurt event names it; and the dig-in names what it walls off from. The clean run suffocated in its night mine at y 35, a block over its head and every step failing for ten seconds: a head in a solid block while hurting counts as needing air, and the block is dug first. In the End rehearsal, endermen turned by the dragon's sweep killed the bot at the dragon's 190 of 200: the End's enderman defence is still open.

**Still open.**

9. *Three runs is too thin for a damage median.* `hoglin_single` measured 8.8, then 5.6, then 13.0 across iterations with no change aimed at it. Treat a single-drill damage move under about five points as noise, or raise the repeat count before believing it.
10. *Two blazes cost 31 damage where one costs 6.7.* It fights them now instead of hiding, which is the point, but the second blaze roughly quintuples the bill and only one run in three cleared. The pair is the case to work on next.
11. *A pack of two costs time instead of health.* `wither_skeleton_pair` went from 15.4 damage in 18 seconds to zero damage in 38: the bunker door is the right answer and it is twice as slow. Worth keeping, worth knowing.
12. *`hoglin_single` at 13 damage and one zombie run that never cleared* are both inside the noise band above, but neither has been explained.

**Opportunities.**

1. *The blaze drill reproduced the fortress stall exactly.* Three runs, the same error each time ("no dry combat route to blaze"), then `dig_in`: no swing, no damage taken, no rod. A blaze hovers, so there is no standing room within two blocks of it, the route check refuses the fight, and the survival layer's shooter rule wins and walls the bot in. This is why the dream run spent an hour in a fortress with nothing to show. Now the approach falls back to the ground under the target, which a sword reaches from three blocks.
2. *A pack eleven blocks off was not a crowd.* The herd drill charged the nearest hoglin and met four at three blocks, three times out of three. Crowds are now counted at twelve blocks for sealing and sixteen for vetoing a charge, so a herd seen coming is treated as one while there is still time to place blocks.
3. *A lone wither skeleton was hidden from rather than fought.* The survival layer listed them as ranged, so it sealed itself into a pocket and took hits through the doorway: 14.4 damage before the fix, 1.9 after. They carry a sword. Piglins now count as shooters only when they hold a crossbow.
4. *A drop on the floor is a drop not carried.* A cleared blaze scored nothing because the rod lay where it fell; the fight's own pickup only runs when the fight's own code did the killing. The hunt now collects its item before anything else.
5. *Two wither skeletons cost 15.4 damage where one costs 1.9.* Clearing them takes eighteen seconds. Worth a look after the failures: a doorway or a backstep between swings would change the arithmetic.

**Three ways the harness lied before it told the truth**, each of which would have been read as a combat failure:

6. *A fill into unloaded chunks builds nothing.* No player had ever stood in that corner of the Nether, so the first shell was never built, the bot was teleported into solid rock and smothered in twelve seconds. The region is force-loaded first and the bot now checks the floor and headroom before a mob is summoned.
7. *Carving a room out of the Nether does not evict the Nether.* Zombified piglins and an enderman were standing in the volume when the walls went up and did the killing; the server's own death line is what gave it away, so every run records it. The arena is swept of non-player entities at build and before each drill.
8. *The bot bricked itself in and the walls outlived the drill.* Cornered, it walls off the tunnel; later drills then started with it behind its own netherrack, with no line of sight, so nothing counted as a threat and it stood still while a mob wandered the far side. Three drills scored zero damage and zero swings and meant nothing. `scripts/threat-probe.js` found it: the same mob at the same distance was `visible: true` in an open room and `visible: false` in the corridor. Every drill now rebuilds its arena from a holding cell.

## clean · 2026-09-23 · "beat the game" from a fresh world, no aids · in progress

**The rules.** The user's goal (2026-09-23): Jev plays normal survival through to the ender dragon without any cheats. This run is the evidence. A fresh world (`clean-20260923`, Normal, random seed) on its own Fabric server with ServerReplay (port 25581), a fresh Jev with no saved state, the dream seeded as "beat the game" and nothing else. No kit restore, no skipped nights, no console command that touches the game; Jev is not an operator. A supervisor restarts the bot's process if it hangs (a reconnect, not a game aid). Started 22:41 UTC. The dream run on 25580 carries on alongside, unaided from 21:20, as the place late-game bugs show first.

**Minute 0 to 18.** From an empty inventory: logs, the block reserve, food, a bed, a furnace, the iron pickaxe at 9 minutes, a shield at 10, an iron sword at 11, a bucket at 17. No generative call.

1. *Death one: a base in a cave (23:00 UTC).* The home rung came up while the bot was smelting in its mine at y 14, and the site search anchors on where the bot stands: the nearest water was a pool at y 0 in a cave, and the base went there. Walking down to level it met four zombies in the dark; with an iron sword and shield but no armour yet, the survival layer went pillar, fight, pillar, defend, off the edge, fight, flee in seventeen seconds, and the bot died. A base is sited under open sky now (nothing solid but leaves for forty blocks over the water or the ground), and underground the anchor is the surface above the bot. The cave home was cleared from the bot's memory so it chooses again. Still open: the tactic changing every half second against a pack; one choice held for a few seconds would likely have beaten four zombies, or got out.

2. *Deaths two and three: the walk to bed, and the bed (00:48 UTC).* Iron-mining a hundred and thirty blocks from its base when the night came, the bot chose its bed (the home bed counted to 160 blocks) and walked there in the dark. The walk watched nothing: hit on the way from 20 to 3, it arrived with a skeleton, a spider and a spear-carrying zombie at the bed, could not sleep ("monsters nearby"), and died. It came back to life at that bed, beside the same mobs, with nothing in its pockets; it set off to gather dirt for a shelter and died again twenty seconds later. Three changes: once it is dark the home bed counts only within 48 blocks (the long walk is for dusk); the walk to bed stops when the bot is hurt by three or sees a mob within six, and the threat rules take it from there; and with no pickaxe the straight-down shaft still goes where every block in it digs by hand in a second (dirt and the like), capped with the dug dirt.

3. *A respawn spiral (20:48 to 20:52 server time).* After death two the bot came back to life six times in four minutes, at the bed or near the world spawn, at night, with nothing in its pockets, beside a group of zombies (several with spears), and died within twenty to sixty seconds each time, twice while "gathering blocks to dig in". The shelter path already tries a shaft before a trip for blocks, but the shaft needed a pickaxe; with the bare-handed shaft (above) it goes two blocks down into dirt and caps itself. The whole iron kit and twenty-nine raw iron were lost; the first death's drops were by the bed, inside the loaded ground, and aged out overnight.

**First-days trials (2026-09-24).** The goal changed to the first three in-game days done well (`scripts/first-days.js`: no deaths, no loops, never still or pacing for over a minute outside a shelter or bed, iron tools, iron armour worn, a shield, a bed and a home, on two fresh worlds in a row). Trial 1 (`first-days-1`) failed at minute 16 on pacing: two minutes between a stone face and food drops twenty blocks off, the mine step choosing stone thirty blocks down and thirty-six away while standing on a hillside of it, walking back to its old work position, and out again.

4. *One rule for getting stuck, instead of one per way of getting stuck.* Replaying the dream run's flight record step by step, the longest sinks were survival reflexes that no watchdog measured: the night mine (37 minutes, 41 entries), the surface return (32 minutes, 126 entries), the food search (30 minutes, 83 entries). Four watchdogs stood beside the patches, each with its own measure (twenty seconds without activity, five minutes within one box, thirty unchanged ticks, three errors), and every one excused time the survival layer had the bot. And activity was the wrong measure: pacing is moving, a sealing loop is placing, a re-dug hole is digging. The rule now (`src/stillness.js`): every action in either layer, keyed by what it is for rather than its name (mining stone, walking back to the stone and moving on from it are one piece of work), must within forty-five seconds reach new ground (over three blocks from anywhere the bot stood in the last three minutes), gain what it is for, dig or place a block where it has not, or get nearer its own target than it has been. A stall unwinds the running step through `task.check()`, is set aside, and is answered one way: done differently, then something else useful from here, then the rung left for later; a survival action that stalled is refused by the survival layer for ten minutes and the layer falls through to its next answer. Replayed through the actual rule, the dream run's worst stretches are caught (the seven-minute persist in one cell, four minutes at a furnace, laps round an oak wood, the armour pacing); in trial 1 the stone oscillation was credited as progress until the "nearer" measure kept its best per target, so a second walk back to the same face is not nearer twice. Waits worth making (asleep, a fight, a shelter at night) and emergencies are not measured. The five-minute box watchdog, the activity rule, the pocket-restart counter and the detour's idle counter are gone.

5. *Trial 3 (`first-days-3`): a loop on a walk that had no route.* Iron pickaxe and shield by minute 20, then sixteen blocks from home at dusk the same walk failed ten times: "navigation ended before reaching the destination". It had not ended at all: mineflayer-pathfinder resolves its `goto` on an empty path, whatever the search found, so "no route from here" read as a walk cut short, and each retry was charged to the last retry because the failing step never named itself. Now a no-route is said as such, a retry is charged to the step that failed, and every error records the source line past the shared primitives (`lastErrorFrom`), so the next loop says which walk it was.

6. *Trial 4 (`first-days-4`): a fall onto dripstone.* Night-mining coal at y 58 it took an ore out of the floor beside its feet; a cave ten blocks deep lay under it, and the next step went in. The dig refused only the one cell under the body's centre. The rule now is the player's: no floor block within reach of the feet is dug when there is open air under it. Later the same trial dug a one-block shaft for cobblestone and looked for a free cell for its crafting table six times at the bottom; a walled-in station now goes in a notch cut in the wall.

7. *The dream run's death in a cave: the shield turned to the arrows.* Full iron and a shield, its hand empty, it faced the skeleton's arrows every second while two zombies beside it took it from twenty to nothing in fifty seconds. The mob hitting the bot comes before the one shooting at it: no shield raised to an arrow with a mob that does not shoot within three and a half blocks.

8. *Trials 5 to 7.* Trial 5 was void: the trial-4 server never took its stop, the new one could not bind, and the bot played trial 4's world and player; a trial start now terminates an old server that will not stop and checks the new log names the new world. Trial 6 stood 99 seconds at the foot of the stone pit it had dug, walking at a sheep: surface travel forbade towers and digging, so a pit with sheer walls had no way out. Leaving where the bot stands is now always allowed (a pillar and digging within the start's box, from a place walled in at head height); a hunt gives up prey it cannot get nearer to; a sheep that gave no wool rests. Trial 7 died six times, five to skeletons.

9. *How to meet a skeleton, measured* (`scripts/shield-probe.js`, arena, one skeleton at eight blocks, twenty seconds a stance): standing 17.7 damage; raising the shield as each arrow came 16.3 (a shield takes a quarter second to come up and the arrow is quicker; this was the rule in use); the bow with shield cover 15.8 to 17.5 and no kill; the shield held up throughout 0 and no kill; walking at it behind the shield 2, dead in five seconds; sprinting at it with a stone sword and no shield 0 to 2, dead in four. A lone ground shooter within sixteen is now run at and struck by an armed bot at six health or more; the bow and the pocket are for when that cannot be done.

10. *The measure, made honest both ways.* The audit's pacing counted a minute of mining coal round a furnace that made an iron pickaxe and a shield; its flips counted a tunnel and the dig at its end trading names while the shaft advanced sixteen blocks. Both now use the stall rule's measure: pacing is walking back and forth that gains nothing worth keeping; a flip is a loop when it covers under five blocks and gains nothing. Trial 7's three flips were all progress under two names; several of its pacing minutes remain, and are real. Stone is also taken by the couple of dozen at the face now, instead of three at a time between trips to the surface.

11. *Trial 8* reached an iron pickaxe, a shield and a bed by minute 8, then failed on a real flip: one stair down, a full candidate search, a new ore thirty blocks off, the next stair, a dozen times a minute; later it cycled a coal target a second at the foot of its stairs. Stairs now go six at a time, and three candidates in a row that cannot be had send the bot moving. It was shot dead by a pair of skeletons while walking to an old pocket: charging a pair one after the other measured no damage and both dead in eight seconds, so up to three are now run at.

12. *Trial 9: a desert spawn.* Sand, sandstone and terracotta, no tree in sight and no dirt. The log search looked for oak alone for a hundred and twenty-eight legs; any wood is wood now. At dusk every shelter failed (no site, no blocks, no ground to dig, no pickaxe) and the failure was thrown every tick into five persists; a shelter that cannot be had now rests three minutes and the work goes on. Then shaking loose dug the sand over its head, the column fell in, and the loop, watching only for mobs, let it suffocate for twelve seconds; every wait now watches the air, and no block with sand or gravel over it is dug from under.

13. *The six silent hours.* The trial-7 watcher caught its event but never exited (`tail -F ... | head -1` keeps the tails alive), so no notice came and nothing happened between 06:00 and 11:36 UTC. The watcher now polls by byte offset and exits.

14. *Trial 10: the closest yet.* Clean for 47 minutes, with an iron pickaxe, an iron sword, a shield, a bed and all four pieces of iron armour worn by minute 26; only the home was missing. On a mountain at y 100 the site search wanted level ground beside water, found none in three places, and waited; with a bucket made meanwhile it went after cave water, and the climb back, one stair a call, flipped its step name with the bucket step until the audit called it a loop. The home is now sited dry after two looks without water (the bed and the chest do not need a pond; the water phase fills it later), and the climb takes six stairs a call like the descent. Also: the charge rule was tightened after the dream run ran at three skeletons from a river at ten health (dry firm footing only, fourteen health for more than one, three seconds without getting nearer ends it); progress counts anything worth keeping, so ingots cooking on the way to a helmet are not a stall; an unreachable furnace's saved batch goes after two tries, before its rung does.

15. *Trials 11 to 15.* Trial 11 (a sand island) failed on a shaft pocket retried five times: any survival action that fails now rests three minutes and the tick goes to the work, and the way up to air is an emergency under the name vitals reports (my own straight-up fallback had also kept it "surfacing" with a full air bar). Trial 12 had the home by minute 25 and failed at 28 standing 89 seconds: tilling was invisible to the stall rule (a block used with an item now counts), and a smelting wait with the named coal out of reach stood still (the stone in arm's reach is taken instead). Trial 13 had tools, shield, bed and home by minute 20 and was scored pacing at 30 while mining the iron for its armour: the flight record kept no pockets on its heartbeats, so the audit could see no gain; the pockets are now recorded every ten seconds and compared before and after a window. Trial 14 stood five minutes at the start and trial 15 thirteen minutes on the bed rung: the stone bulk rule re-ran the full forty-eight-block candidate search for every block, minutes of CPU underground, enough to time the connection out; rock is now looked for within reach. And the stall rule never fired in those thirteen minutes: its hostile-in-view minute was renewed at every look while a mob stayed in sight.

16. *Trials 16 to 19.* Trial 16 sat seven minutes in its night pocket with an iron pickaxe, every search failing for want of walkable ground: an enclosed bot now digs a doorway two high toward where it is going, and a stair one block away is walked in seconds or not at all. Trial 17 was a desert again: no detour at dusk without tools, so a stall with nothing else on offer is now a walk to new ground, and a stall turns the wood search's heading instead of dropping the search back to its first. Trial 18 lost twenty-one minutes to the expedition food gate, which held the game ladder off in a land with few animals; the ladder stocks its own food at the Nether crossing, so the gate no longer applies to it (abandoned at minute 24). Trial 19 had tools, sword, shield, bed and home by minute 30 and failed at 35 on a flip between the plot's repair and its tilling: its own staircase had gone down through the plot. The stall rule now keys ladder work by its rung, so alternating sub-steps cannot split its time, and the home base and three blocks under it are reserved from digging. Also: the home-site fit held the event loop for tens of seconds (the nearest sixty-four candidates now), rock was being searched for over forty-eight blocks once a block (within reach now), the hostile-in-view minute renewed itself, and a hurt bot in a watched pocket now eats behind the wall and stays in. The verdict had read the shield in the off-hand as none after heartbeats began carrying the pockets; it takes equipment from the latest frame that has it.
17. *Trials 20 to 23, and Jev choosing.* Trial 21 died to a skeleton at minute 35 while a hand-written charge rule, run before Jev was asked, kept retrying. Trial 22 was shot by a pillager at minute 7 after Jev chose to run: fight and run were the only stances it was offered. The user's direction then was that Jev should choose, fallbacks included, so the play decisions lost their confidence gates, the stance question is asked by default, and the charge at ground shooters became one of Jev's options (`docs/rule-audit.md` lists what still chooses in code). Trial 23 failed at minute 11 standing still 84 seconds: moving on from a patch of dirt left a step-less marker as the step, and the work loop spun on it. The marker is now taken down after the walk.
18. *Trial 24: everything but armour by minute 25.* A bed at 6, the iron pickaxe at 13, the sword and shield by 22, a home at 25, no deaths. It failed at minute 27 standing ninety-three seconds beside its own plot: one cell the hoe would not turn was tried again and again, and the stall's first answer (turn a search it was not using) left the bot where it was. A cell that will not turn twice from beside it now waits twenty minutes, and "another way" at a stall starts from fresh ground eight blocks off. Trial 24 ran the code before most of the rule audit's hand-over; trial 25 is the first with Jev choosing the stance, the stall answer, the night, the upkeep, the home site and the evening.
19. *Trial 25, stopped at minute 23.* A savanna with no sheep: the bed rung walked five hundred blocks for wool, and the bed could not wait its turn, so the iron pickaxe was never on offer. Night came, and Jev stayed in its pocket at 0.93 over the night mine, then went on staying after dawn: the question said neither that dawn had come nor that nothing was watching. Both are said now, the bed may wait like the shield, and its option says how long and how far the sheep search has gone (probed: with twelve minutes and 480 blocks said, Jev explores for a village at 0.61 instead). Stopped for trial 26, which runs the rest of the audit's hand-over too: the shelter method, full pockets, the furnace wait, and the fight cost.
20. *Trial 26: the best yet, then a drowning.* A bed at minute 6; the iron pickaxe, shield and sword by 8 (while the furnace cooked Jev chose to mine and dig stone, not wait); a home by 36; no failures for forty-nine minutes, iron armour the one milestone left. Then two faults. The plot fix from trial 24 marked a cell the hoe would not turn as blocked, and blocked cells reopen the base for repair: ten minutes of repairing four cells that could not be repaired, each stall answered "another way". And a detour to smelt for experience walked into water at y 46 and stood waiting while its air went from 20 to nothing: nothing in that wait looked at air. The cell now waits instead; detours and furnace waits check air; and the stall supervisor, on its own clock, stops any step that is under water with no rescue under way and hands it to the survival layer.
21. *Trial 27, void.* The watchdog kept beside the trials (outside the repository) judged the bot alive by its dashboard port, and the port went with the Observatory: from minute 2 it started a second Jev every two minutes, each knocking the last off the server ("logged in from another location") on the same state files. Nothing the bot did can be judged. The watchdogs now judge a bot by its flight recording, written every second.
22. *Trial 28, stopped at minute 25.* Everything but the armour by minute 18, the fastest yet, and no failure by the audit's rules. Then the home plot stalled five times: the pond had been poured from two blocks off, the bucket's aim caught the ground beside the hole, the water ran over a plot cell, and the tilling hung trying to clear it (a console test confirmed water over dirt there). The plot could not be left for later, so the ladder could not reach the armour. Now the pond is poured from beside its hole with a spill taken back up, a flooded cell waits, and the home's pond, plot and pen can wait their turn. Stopped for trial 29 with those changes.
23. *Trial 29: a zombie at y -9, minute 39.* A slow start: the first night's mine asked Jev fifteen times in five seconds for the same coal ore it could not move toward (the ore was never rested; now it is), and by dawn it had one iron and twenty-four copper. It then found seventeen iron. At minute 39, night mining at y -9, it stopped to craft a spare stone pickaxe and a zombie took it from twenty to nothing in seven seconds, standing still, with no stance asked: the crafting did not look up. The same shape as trial 26's drowning, so the same answer: hit twice in four seconds with no survival action in three, the walk, the dig and any window are stopped and the step unwinds to the survival layer.
24. *Trial 30: a skeleton at minute 46, and a reproduction.* The first trial to reach the armour rung (the bed deferred after a fruitless sheep search, as Jev chose once told of it). It died standing still for twenty-five seconds while a skeleton at three blocks shot it, the hurt watchdog firing three times to no effect. Reproduced on trial 30's own world: a stall answered with the earn-XP detour left a smelting batch saved; the next step finished that batch first, walked toward its furnace, stalled, and clicked the furnace from 7.6 blocks off. Mineflayer's window open waits twenty seconds for a window the server will never send, checking nothing, so neither the survival layer nor the watchdogs could act. A bot sent to open that furnace from where Jev died sat exactly twenty seconds. Every furnace and crafting-table window now goes through one opener that refuses a block out of reach, checks the task while it waits and gives up in seconds; the same live test now refuses at once, and opens the furnace normally from beside it. Trial 29's zombie death, in a pickaxe craft, fits the same wait.
25. *Trial 31: a zombie at minute 17, reproduced.* A bed, iron pickaxe and shield by minute 11. At 17, finishing a saved smelting batch, it went ten seconds without checking its task while a zombie killed it; the watchdog's new report said so (no window, not digging, not walking, unchecked 5 then 10.7 seconds). Reproduced on trial 31's world with every async bot call logged: the step sat in mineflayer's window sync, which clicks a window and waits up to twenty seconds for the server to send it again, and a window already closed is never sent. The sync is now bounded in the compatibility plugin: a closed window is not waited on, any sync gives up after two seconds, and a watchdog's stop reaches it at once. The same run then showed the step unwinding within a tenth of a second at each hit and Jev fighting; with the real loop's order the zombie was fought off at the cost of one hit.
26. *Trial 32: the best yet, then a beach and a held signal, both reproduced.* A night in a pocket, mined through (Jev chose the mine once told what was still needed and that the stone pickaxe mines iron); by minute 43 an iron pickaxe, iron sword, bucket and shield, no bed (no sheep seen). At 44, climbing to the surface from y 57 under a beach, it paced one block and back until the audit called it a loop. The real stair choices, run from its spot in its world by a spectator, were all "sandstone in the way": sandstone and the other natural ground of deserts, badlands and caves were not on the tunnel's list of ground it may dig. Replayed live with that fixed, the climb reached y 59 and spun five times a second one block from a landing with no safe step (sand to a lake above); a landing with no step and no retreat is now set aside after three tries, a call that did not move re-reads the routes, and the way up may pillar with dirt or stone that makes no tool. At 01:19, hit twice at four health, the goal ended and the bot stood still: the hurt watchdog held its threat signal for the survival layer, and the loop's own check threw it out of the loop, a regression from trial 30's sticky signals. A test reproduces it; a held signal now gives the survival layer its turn. Added the same evening, from the user: biomes with what they hold and travel to them, string for wool, night hunts, the stash chest before the bed and a chest anywhere before a risk, the risk and the cost of a death and of a night hidden in real minutes, and the last three minutes of positions in every choice.
27. *Trial 33: a pool with a stone lid, reproduced.* The fastest start yet: a bed by minute 7, iron pickaxe, iron sword and water bucket by 18, twenty-four iron smelting for the armour at 20. At 22 it went back into a small underground pool, came up in its one open cell with stone at the top of its head, and swam against a bank one block up for two minutes: the game lifts a swimmer onto a bank only with room over its head. ReproJev, put in the same pool with the real shore code, stopped at the same spot to the hundredth of a block after 75 seconds. The block over the head is now dug before any climb out of water, when it is quick and nothing liquid is over it; the same replay was out and on the bank in 12 seconds.
28. *Trials 34, 35 and 36, the first run side by side.* **34** (the code before the move-by-move escape) had an iron pickaxe, iron sword and shield by minute 40 and stood six minutes in an alcove on a mountainside, a cliff on the open side, every walk failing. ReproJev put in the same alcove with the move-by-move escape was out in two moves (a step south, a climb south). **35** spawned in a desert whose every way out crossed a river, a lake or the sea; the search for wood walks only on dry ground and turned at every third walk that met water, so it circled a box seventy-five blocks across for eight minutes. The heading is now Jev's (each way with its biomes, the water on the ground and whether land comes again past it, and how often the search went that way), and water on the chosen heading is swum. Replayed from the same spawn: north, across a lake of 38 blocks, and 427 blocks out in four and a half minutes. **36** had an iron pickaxe, sword and shield by minute 25 and failed levelling its plot: a plot cell by the pond was dug, the water came in, a stone went in to plug it or step out on, and the dirt met "placement obstructed" twenty times. The wrong block in a cell being filled now comes out first; a test with the real placement's refusal fails without the fix and passes with it.
29. *Trials 37 and 40.* **37** had an iron pickaxe and a shield by minute 7, the fastest yet, and failed at 8: at dusk, climbing out of its mine, it switched between the tunnel and a retreat from it seven times in eleven seconds while covering 4.2 blocks, under the audit's five. The real stair choices, run afterwards from each point of the climb in its world, all had a step up; hostiles about at dusk (the stair's cells near a hostile are refused) are the likely cause, not confirmed, and nothing was changed. **40** spawned on snowy plains and failed at 8 minutes: a workstation went only into a cell of plain air over a full block, and there every cell at the feet was a snow layer over grass, so the table had "no place" forty times in a minute. A station now goes into a cell of ground cover the placement replaces anyway; a test on a snow field fails without the change and passes with it.
30. *Trial 39: a bed it could not claim and a barrel in a wall.* An iron pickaxe, iron sword and shield by minute 25, and ninety-three raw iron carried. It placed its bed at the home and could not walk back to claim it (every route failed and the claim threw before counting an attempt); the home's bed step could not wait, so the armour never reached the ladder, and Jev took the trial chambers as a side trip instead. There the expedition walked at a barrel built into a wall of tuff bricks and copper, with no way in, until the audit failed the trial at minute 37. All the home's steps can now wait (on trial 39's saved state the ladder then offers the iron armour), and a container the expedition cannot get to rests after two tries (a test fails without the change and passes with it).
31. *Trial 38: everything but the armour, then a spin.* The first trial to reach the home: an iron pickaxe, iron sword, shield, bed and home by minute 31. At 41 it sealed itself in for the night at a shell with no missing cell, but was not sealed in it (a block in its own space); sealing returned "done" having placed nothing, and the survival layer called it again twenty times a second for 98 seconds. A pass that closes nothing and leaves the bot unsealed now says so and rests that spot a minute; a test with a cobweb in the bot's head cell fails without the change and passes with it.
32. *Trial 41.* An iron pickaxe, iron sword, shield and bed by minute 21. It failed at 25 on the seal spin fixed after trial 38 (it started before that fix) and on two minutes in a water hole beside its mine, going round the shore escapes (the shore, the dig to it, up for air, the step out), none failing long enough for the stall rule to call on anything else. After half a minute in the water in one place the way out is now worked one move at a time by Jev. Not replayed: the bot had changed the hole before it left, and ReproJev put there stood on dry ground; a test covers the hand-over.
33. *Trials 42 and 44.* **42** (started three minutes before the seal fix of trial 38) failed at 19 on the same seal, asked of Jev twice a second for three minutes. **44** died at minute 8 in a cave: a skeleton at four blocks, and the fight estimate Jev was given said it would take 2.5 seconds and 1.3 damage (the struck mob's rate cut to a third, as for a biter, and the swings as if it stood still). It took fourteen health in six seconds; Jev, told otherwise, chose to fight at nine health and at two, and was shot off a ledge. For a shooter the estimate now keeps its full rate, doubles the time (it backs off after each hit) and adds the walk to it, and a shield takes half its arrows; the arena trio, measured with a shield, still comes out as measured. Asked again with the corrected numbers, Jev retreats at two health (0.88) where it fought (0.93).
34. *Trial 46.* An iron pickaxe, iron sword, shield and bed by minute 15. At 16 it stood seventy-five seconds at its furnace: each ladder step smelts only what it needs, one ingot for the shield and two for the sword, and a batch under twenty seconds was stood out with no choice asked; with the crafting between them, three short waits made one standstill. The choice of what to do while it cooks is now asked from one item up, and a walk to an ore or tree is offered only where there and back fits in the cooking (a test covers both).
35. *Trial 43: the first clean hour.* No death, no loop and no standstill in the whole sixty minutes, the first trial to go the distance; it failed on what it lacked, the armour, the bed and the home. Two things held the armour back. The strategy offered it as "get 4 iron helmet" with nothing said of what it took, and Jev, carrying sixty-one raw iron and two hundred coal, put it at 0.28 and explored (a set is now offered by its name and pieces, and every step says what it takes from the pockets; on trial 43's pack the armour goes to 0.76). And it ended with five iron pickaxes: the dig took the fresher of two tools of a tier, so a worn one was never used up and the ladder made another each time the one in hand wore down; fifteen ingots went into pickaxes instead of armour. The more worn of a tier is now used first.
36. *Trial 45.* Its iron pickaxe wore out underground and it said it had "no pickaxe worth the name and no wood for one" with five jungle logs, five sticks, six iron ingots and two oak planks in its pack; it dug toward the surface by hand for minutes and failed at 51 on a standstill. The planner filled the table's plank slots one at a time: the two oak planks carried, then oak again by preference, so it planned an oak log to gather, and a log to gather reads as no wood underground. Slots that take the same alternatives are now filled with one that covers them all from the pockets, carried or made on the spot; on trial 45's pack both pickaxes plan from the jungle logs with nothing to gather (a test fails without the change).
37. *Trial 47 (before the worn-tool change).* An iron sword and shield, twenty-two iron ingots, and at minute 34 no pickaxe at all, twenty-five blocks down, with no log, plank or crafting table in the pack (its tables were left where they were used). It went round the bootstrap pickaxe, the pocket and the way out for 250 seconds. Both planners rightly want a log from the surface here; what went wrong is earlier: no spare pickaxe kept and no table carried. The worn-tool change keeps the spare whole; whether that is enough is for the next trials to show, and nothing else was changed.
38. *Trial 49: a zombie at minute one.* On a mountainside with a wooden pickaxe and no sword, a zombie in the shade came at it within the first minute. Jev chose to retreat (no safe route was found), then to pillar over sealing in (0.85, told a biter at arm's length hits freely while the blocks go down); the pillar did not rise under the hits, and the flee rules' last answer held the spot swinging a wooden pickaxe at the zombie until the bot died. No bug was found to fix: an unarmed start meeting a zombie with nowhere to go. Whether a sword should come earlier on the ladder is open.
39. *Trial 48.* An iron pickaxe, iron sword and shield by minute 20; failed at 22 on sixty-one seconds at a crafting table, a bucket's craft ending in a bare "Timed out waiting for world/inventory update", the same bare timeout seen at crafting in trials 43 and 46. Which wait it is could not be told from the record; a wait with no description now names its caller in the error, so the next one says where.
40. *Trial 52.* Failed at 8 on the shore above iron ore: at each call the search took whichever ore was nearest, and as the bot stepped toward one the other became the nearer, so it turned between an ore to the east and one to the west, pacing four blocks for a minute; the staircase, its target always new, never counted itself stuck. The ore chosen is now kept while it is still there to be had (a test fails without the change).
41. *Trial 51.* Its twenty-four iron for the armour went into the furnace and it mined nearby while they cooked; at forty-five seconds with nothing new in the pockets (the ingots were still in the furnace) the stall rule called the armour stalled, the answer Jev chose was to earn experience by smelting more ore, and that was called stalled three times. A batch of the step's own cooking is now a permitted wait for as long as it takes (plus twenty seconds); the audit's standstill rule still applies, and the while-cooking choices keep the bot moving. It failed at 35 as trial 47 did: its last pickaxe worn out at y 43, forty-four iron ingots and three sticks carried, and no crafting table and no wood, so the bootstrap pickaxe, the pocket and the way out went round for 119 seconds. A table the bot placed now comes back into the pack after the craft when it is the only one (it was picked up before only on an expedition), best effort, so a failed pick-up does not undo the craft. Not covered by a test: it will show in the trials.
42. *Trial 54.* Failed at 6 pacing two minutes near the surface above iron ore twenty and more blocks down: none reachable, so the mining step tunnelled toward the nearest ore it knew, and the nearest changed with every step, eight ores in two minutes. The same fix as trial 52's, in the mining step: the ore tunnelled toward is kept while it is there and not set aside. Not covered by a test (the step runs straight into the tunnelling); the trials will show it.
43. *Trial 53: the armour by minute 27.* An iron pickaxe, sword and shield by 7 and full iron armour by 27, the fastest yet. It failed at 29 at dusk: fifty seconds inside one pass of sealing a pocket, every block of the shell failing slowly and silently, until the stall rule stopped it. A pass now ends after three placements fail in a row, and the error is kept (a test fails without the change). Why the placements failed is not known; the next one will say.
44. *Trial 50: the first pass.* On 2026-09-25 from 05:07 to 06:07 UTC, on a fresh Normal world: no death, no step retried in a loop, no standstill or pacing over a minute outside a shelter, and every milestone: an iron pickaxe, iron sword, full iron armour worn, a shield, a bed and a home. Full iron armour, sword, shield and bed by minute 40; at 55 its last pickaxe wore out with no wood and it dug out by hand (Jev chose to cut logs and make a spare, each attempt failing underground), made another, and finished the home before the hour was out. The goal asks for two fresh worlds in a row; this is the first.
45. *Trial 56.* Failed at 7 pacing on a jungle canopy at y 79 above iron ore: every step down was "jungle leaves in the way" (leaves were not on the tunnel's list of ground it may dig), and under the leaves was air before the ground, where a stair found no floor. Leaves are now dug, and a stair may drop up to three onto solid, dry ground. From its spot, the real stair choices now go down through the canopy to y 69 in six steps.
46. *Trials 55 and 58.* Both failed standing still by a furnace, 92 and 116 seconds, with twenty-four raw iron for the armour cooking (four minutes). Jev chose to mine nearby while it cooked; after eleven ores in walking distance that choice had nothing left, and it was the only thing the plan allowed, so the bot stood by the furnace for the rest of the batch with stone all round it. A while-cooking choice that runs out is now asked again among what is left (dig the stone, the ore in reach, stand by), each way of spending the wait has its own budget, and a batch with more than a minute left looks for ore to thirty-two blocks instead of sixteen.
47. *Trial 57.* Died at minute 2. The server put the fresh player at y 4 in a cave, pockets empty, no wood underground, so it climbed for the surface ninety-five blocks up by hand, seven seconds a block of stone; on the twenty-second step a zombie came up the cave slope below its ledge. Jev chose to retreat (no route), then to keep working, and the bot went on digging while the zombie hit it; then it held a "defensive position" in which it never swung, and went from twenty to nothing in ten seconds. Replayed block for block on the arena server from the world's region files, the unarmed bot died in two of five runs. Four changes: a hit by a hostile mob stops a step at once unless the survival layer is answering the mobs (leaving them be, or gathering shelter blocks, is not an answer); Jev is offered *fight* with bare hands too, with the estimate for it (fists land two hits a second, not four: a mob struck is unhurt for half a second); a hold swings at whatever comes into reach for its second instead of waiting, and the route search that just failed from the same spot is not run again for five seconds; the mob being punched stays a threat for eight seconds when it steps out of sight under the ledge's lip. Replayed three times after: alive every time, three to seven hits taken.
48. *Trial 62.* Failed at minute 30 standing 68 seconds in the shallows at night, at five to ten health: a skeleton had been shooting, and for fifty-five seconds the bot held its shield up, facing the ground, "blocking a shot" every three quarters of a second with nothing but a creeper about. A projectile with no velocity yet counts as incoming when it is within six blocks, for the one fired too close for its velocity to have arrived; an arrow stuck in the ground also has none, and stays a minute. On the arena server an arrow lying beside the bot read zero velocity every second for twelve seconds and was counted every time. A still one now counts only in its first second in view. The reflex runs before any stance, so the skeleton's misses at its feet had kept Jev from being asked anything.
49. *Trial 60.* The cleanest run yet and still not a pass: sixty minutes with no death, no loop and no standstill, the iron tools, full iron armour, a shield and a diamond sword, and no bed and no home. Its bed step was offered to Jev at minute forty-seven as "searching for sheep for 45 minutes, 332 blocks from where the search began, none seen yet"; the server had fourteen sheep loaded, in four flocks a hundred to a hundred and fifty blocks from where the bot had been, and the bot sees animals to about a hundred and twenty-eight. Nothing remembered a flock once it was out of view. Sheep seen are now noted with the trail every fifteen seconds, a flock to a place, for half an hour; the sheep search offers Jev each flock out of view (how many, how long ago, how far, which way), the bed step says them in place of "none seen yet", and a flock walked back to and not found is forgotten.
50. *Trial 61.* Failed at minute 58 on 74 seconds still at a shelter (crafting, leaving and sealing it), with the bed and home still to do. Not yet traced.
51. *Trial 63.* Drowned at minute 25 in a flooded one-block column under a dripstone lid, a lake over the lid, two pointed dripstones between it and a dry cave. The unstuck moves offered Jev eleven digs that each said only "water beside it would flow in", and no air in the state; Jev dug up. Then low air took the turn from them, and the swim for air knew only water and air, found no way, and dug straight up into the lake. Replayed block for block with its water on the arena server it did not get out once in three. Now: each dig says what it opens onto (open air, water, lava, more rock), each move under water says whether the head comes out into air, the breath left is in the state, pointed dripstone is diggable, and the swim for air is the quickest way to air counting a block in the way at its dig time under water (standing on the bottom, where there is one, not afloat: five times quicker), and a step into a cave with ground within three below. Replayed three times after: out every time, air and health full.
52. *Trial 65.* Blown up at minute 14 at y 21 on its way up a staircase, iron sword and no armour, with four zombies and a creeper round it (fighting them all was estimated at forty-five to eighty-five damage from twenty). Jev had chosen to retreat twice as they came; then the stance was asked four times in five seconds (creeper dance, pillar, retreat, creeper dance), each under half confident, none carried through. The creeper had dropped out of view for one look at four blocks behind a zombie, which changed the kinds of mob about, which re-opens the stance; the pillar was chosen without the creeper in the question, and the question asked again when it came back. A mob seen within eight blocks stays in the threats for three seconds after it is last in view, as the mob being struck already did for eight.
53. *Trial 64.* Failed at minute 33 on the audit's loop rule, "tunnel <-> mine", with only the iron armour to go. It was in a small air pocket in an underground lake at y 53, every way down to the iron water; the staircase paced the pocket, started a new round from the same cells, paced again, and each round thrown was the step changing name, seven times in twenty-two seconds. After fifty seconds the stall rule turned it to another ore, the right answer, a loop too late. A staircase that paces where its round began is set aside at once: another round from the same cells would pace them again.
54. *Trial 66.* Failed at minute 34 on 159 seconds still, with only the iron armour to go (twenty-one raw iron of twenty-four). Going to loot a mineshaft it met a zombie it could not get to and that could not get to it: "No way out, so I'm going for the zombie", a charge reported twenty times a second, the way to it failing at once each time, health twenty throughout. A charge was reported done whatever happened, and a charge and a fight are holds the stall rule leaves alone. A charge that went nowhere (the way failed, the bot moved under a block, nothing in reach) now fails, and that mob is left be for twenty seconds while it lands nothing, as mobs Jev chose to leave be are; a fight stance whose charge failed that way is a stance that failed.
55. *Trial 67.* Failed at minute 23 on 97 seconds still by its furnace with the twenty-four iron for the armour cooking, the note 46 fix in place: two walks of eleven ores each used the twenty-four digs of the walking budget, stone was not offered (over sixty-four cobblestone carried), and the rest was waiting. The walks are bounded by the batch (back ten seconds before it is done), so the count is dropped; stone is dug to a hundred and twenty-eight.
56. *Trial 68.* Failed at minute 13, a loop and 121 seconds still, sealed in a night pocket at y 45 to work there: its table went down in an open cell two blocks off on the far side of the pocket's wall, and every craft after was "I can't reach the crafting table I placed", thirty-five times. A cell for a workstation has to be in sight from the eyes; walled in, the notch cut in the wall is the place.
57. *Trial 70, running.* The sheep sightings (note 49) at work: "back to the sheep I saw 100 blocks north", then 116 south, then 108 north, each flock forgotten on arrival. The bot was mining; it saw the sheep on the surface from below, and the walk back went by x and z alone, to under them, where none was within reach. A flock is walked to at the height it was seen, and arrived at only within twelve blocks of it.
58. *Trial 70.* Slain by a zombie at minute 24, underground at night without armour: a skeleton chase took it from twenty to thirteen, a retreat, then four zombies and a skeleton at twelve health, and the stance offered fight (seventy damage estimated), seal and keep working. Jev chose to seal; the pocket was half built when the zombies arrived, and it went from twelve to nothing in five seconds. The seal option now says the race: how many blocks the pocket wants and how long they take, and how soon the nearest biter can be at the bot. Asked again with that, Jev still sealed (0.45 to 0.57 against 0.6 before): three bad choices, and the one it made may have been the least bad. The run was lost earlier, and this one is left as a judgment, not a rule.
59. *Trial 73.* Failed at minute 9 on 175 seconds still, holding a fight stance at a creeper that stayed six blocks off, beside a drop and a few blocks up: no charge there, so "the fight is held here, facing it", reported 688 times. A hold for a mob that does not come a block nearer in eight seconds is a stance that failed, and the mob is left be while it lands nothing (a creeper only while it is more than five blocks off).
60. *Trial 72.* Everything but the home by minute 33. The home's steps were offered to Jev as "get home site" and nothing more, beside options that each said what they were worth; it chose the armour pieces first, then the home site at minute 28. The first four home steps now say what they are for, as the others do.
61. *Trial 72 passed.* Sixty minutes with no death, no loop and no standstill: the iron pickaxe, the iron sword, full iron armour, a shield and a bed by minute 22, the home (site, level, stash, bed claimed) by minute 53, then a pen and golden boots. Its home came last and late, the choice Jev made; the pass rests on it having been made in time.
62. *Trial 75.* Failed at minute 11 on 65 seconds still: its staircase broke into water, it came up for air, and a pocket it chose to seal on the hillside took fifty-three seconds of a block now and then, never sealed. A seal pass now ends at twenty seconds and the place is set aside for a minute. (No test: it wants a clock the test controls.)
63. *Trial 76.* Failed at minute 5 on 125 seconds still, and kicked from the server for the chat: the walk back to a flock (note 57) failed at once, the flock was kept, Jev chose it again, four times a second, "back to the sheep I saw 85 blocks north" each time. A flock the bot cannot walk to is forgotten as one not there on arrival is; and the walk's target had been named `goal`, hiding the bot's goal from the line that forgets the flock.
64. *Trial 74.* Everything by minute 48, then failed at minute 55, five short of what would have been the second pass in a row: a loop ("no route from here to the destination", partial) and four minutes of pacing between its plot and its pen. The night pocket it had sealed beside the plot at 10:10 left cobblestone, planks and dirt on the cell before the pen's gate, walling the plot off from the pen, with the water on the other side. The shell was already cleared off the bed's and the chest's cells before they were placed; now off the gate and its stand before the pen is built.
65. *Trial 78.* Failed at minute 20 on 70 seconds still at y -23, sealed in its night pocket and working there: it crafted an iron pickaxe, a shield and an iron sword in that minute while small batches smelted. The goal counts standing still only outside a shelter or bed; the audit does not know the bot is sealed in (the survival report is older than its twenty-second window). Left as it is, a question for the user, not a change made to the measure.
66. *Trial 77.* Drowned at minute 36 with only the armour to go. Working its way up to the surface from y 17 it pillared a one-block shaft of its own to y 26, dry, then dug up into diorite with water beside it: the option said "water beside it would flow in", and the water came down and filled the shaft it stood in. A dig over the head now says the water (or lava) would pour down onto the bot, and fill the one-block shaft it stands in where it does; a move ending under water says how far up the air is, or that the water runs to a ceiling. Asked again with that, Jev dug up once in three instead of three in three.
67. *Trial 81.* Slain by a spider at minute 2: spawned underground beside a spider dungeon, looted its chests (three iron ingots, a bucket, a golden apple), and was digging out by hand, no wood for a pickaxe, when a spider came; bare-handed, the retreat found no way, and it died with the golden apple in its pack. Eating one is now a stance Jev is offered in a fight below full health, with what it gives; asked again, Jev still chose to retreat (the apple at 0.07). A judgment, not a rule.
68. *Swimming, measured on the arena server (the user asked whether Jev swims fast).* Holding jump the bot rises 23 blocks of water in 7.0 s (3.4 a second), climbs a 12-block waterfall in 5.9 s, and crosses 40 blocks of water in 20.4 s (1.9 a second) with or without sprint: the physics has no sprint-swimming, only the slower stroke. The route planner, though, had no way up in water at all: the waterfall was "no path". A water column that comes out into air within twenty blocks is now a way up (twenty blocks is about six seconds, well inside a breath); the planner then took the bot up the waterfall in 7.8 s. Sprint-swimming is not added: it is only across, and the game's own numbers for it are needed first.
69. *The verdict changed (the user).* Each milestone counts once reached at any time, not only if still held at the end; all six by minute 45; standing still and pacing are reported as notes, not failures on their own, because they cost time and time is what is measured; deaths and loops over the sixty minutes still fail. Under these rules trial 72 (home at minute 53) would not have passed.
70. *Trial 79 passed,* the first under the new rules: the bed at minute 3, the iron pickaxe at 6, the shield and the iron sword at 7, the armour at 21, the home at 32; no death, no loop. Noted: 133 seconds still near the end on its way up to the surface, which the old rules failed. Its iron pickaxe wore out at minute 50 and was counted as reached.
71. *Trial 84, a false loop.* Called a loop at minute 5, "tunnel <-> collect_nearby_resource": the tunnel toward iron and the pickup of each block's cobblestone traded names every second or two while the tunnel advanced a block each time, five blocks in sixteen seconds, one short of the audit's exemption for flipping that covers ground. A loop comes back to where it was: a window that ends three blocks or more from where it began is not one. The trial, not yet over, was no longer failed and went on.
72. *Trial 83, a verdict bug.* All six by minute 41, then called "missing by minute 45: home (reached at minute 47)": the saved home keeps only its latest times, and the bed claimed again at bedtime (11:49:37) moved the home from minute 41 to 47. The verdict now keeps the earliest time each milestone was seen reached, from the verdicts before. Trial 83's record was corrected by hand from its flight (the stash step at 11:44:33 to 42, the bed placed and claimed at 11:44:50 to 51, minute 41, which the verdict at 11:48:49 had read), and says so.
73. *Trial 84.* Failed at minute 39 on a loop, "place_bed <-> village_bed": its home stood in a village, and the bed it had just placed on the home's bed cell was the nearest "village bed", taken and placed again eight times in eight seconds. The home's own bed cells are never a village bed.
74. *Trial 83 passed:* the bed at minute 7, the iron pickaxe at 13, the shield and the iron sword at 14, the armour at 34, the home at 41 (its record corrected from the flight, note 72); no death, no loop. Not two in a row with trial 79: trials 82 and 84 failed between them, by start and by finish.
75. *Trial 85.* Failed at minute 45 for want of the armour, everything else by minute 25. After the home it did the plot and the pen (not wanted by the trial), began the twenty-four iron at minute 27, lost two and a half minutes to a night pocket and more to a tunnel, and put all twenty-four in the furnace at minute 43: four minutes of smelting after the mining, where a player would have had the first eight cooking while mining the rest. Instead of teaching the planner to smelt while mining (a change to its core), a big batch now uses more furnaces, as a player does: from sixteen up, one or two more furnaces are made from the cobblestone carried, placed beside the one in use, each loaded with a share and its fuel and left cooking, recorded on the goal so a restart still collects them, and dug up after. On the arena server twenty-four raw iron came out in 88.7 seconds, against about 240 in one furnace. (The furnaces dug up were not picked up after: sixteen cobblestone a big batch.)
76. *Trials 86 and 89.* Trial 86 failed at minute 45 for its home (the iron by 26, the bed only at the end). Trial 89 froze to death in its first minute: it spawned in a drift of powder snow four deep, took the snow over its head for a roof, "dug out by hand" as from a mine, and the staircase refuses powder snow as dangerous: "no existing dry route" nine times. In powder snow the vitals now dig the way out to the nearest cell clear of it with a floor, and walk to it; on the arena server, out of a drift four deep in 4.2 seconds, health full.
77. *Trial 87 passed*, all six by minute 43 (bed 5, pickaxe 12, shield and sword 13, home 19, armour 43), and drowned twenty-four seconds after its hour. Replayed block for block on the arena server it drowned five times of five. Two causes, both found by measuring. First, the bot's own compatibility plugin widens its body to the server's float size, and a position set by the server at exactly x.3 against stone left it a hundred-millionth of a block inside the stone: every move from there was corrected back, twenty times a second, and the bot hung where it was (at x.3 it could not sink in still water, at x.31 it could; without the plugin it could). After every position the server sets, the body is now taken out of any overlap under a millionth of a block. Second, the swim for air held jump the whole way and counted a lower cell reached from above it; the way out went down a block under a low ceiling, north twice and up. The feet are now held at the next cell's own height, within a tenth. Replayed three times after: breathing in 4.3 seconds each time, the air never below 16. The overlap may have held the bot against walls elsewhere too (the navigation stalls beside walls).
78. *Copper (the user: "makes sense").* 26.1 has copper tools and armour between stone and iron, and nothing in the bot knew them. Now: copper armour in the fight estimate (2, 4, 3, 1: ten points, a zombie's three down to two), worn over nothing and under iron, the copper sword and axe as weapons above stone, and "copper armour first" offered to Jev as a side trip while nothing is worn, with what it takes (twenty-four ingots, the copper carried and in view) and what it gives.
