Each detour's option now says how many times it was chosen for this same stall in the last thirty minutes and that the work stood still again after each. Left in the notes: why a beach at sea level read as underground.
237. *A correction to notes 224 and 225: Jev's answers do not take seconds.* The flight record writes a decision frame when the step that asked it returns, after the chosen stance or work has run; the decision's own time is when it was answered. The seconds between them (a median of 1.9, up to thirty) are the answer being carried out, not Jev thinking: the service answers in a fifth of a second measured at the bot (7,886 answers this evening, none over 0.9 seconds). mid-231-a (note 224) did not stand four seconds waiting for an answer; it stood four seconds in the retreat Jev had chosen, finding a way, while the creeper came on. The backing from a creeper while a question is out does no harm and stays, but its reason was wrong. The event loop held for two to four seconds at a time (note 225) was real and is in the bot's own log. What stood the bot still was the retreat's route search, which is next.
238. *Nights, the most time before the Nether: the base's bed taken along.* The scoreboard (scripts/scoreboard.js) put the midgame trials' 56 hours before the Nether at over a third nights: mining through them, waiting in pockets and climbing out after. Sleeping was almost never on offer: a bed was carried at about 650 of 2,750 night choices, the base's bed was too far to be offered, and a second bed to carry, chosen 133 times, needed three wool of one colour and mostly stalled. A player takes their bed along. Jev is now offered that (take_home_bed), told that any night then passes in seconds wherever it comes, that sleep is refused with a monster near, and that the spawn point goes wherever it last slept and a death drops the bed. Carried, the base's bed is still the base's: the home does not ask for it back; lost, the home asks for a bed again.
239. *Iron armour may wait until after the Nether.* The scoreboard put mining, smelting, crafting and tunnelling at about a quarter of the 56 hours before the Nether, much of it toward twenty-four ingots of armour, which the ladder required before the Nether-first option was on offer at all. A player goes with an iron pickaxe, a bucket and food. The armour's pieces are now steps that may wait; the Nether-first option says what going without costs in the Nether, hit by hit through what is worn now (a blaze's fireball, a wither skeleton's blade, a piglin's), against full iron.
240. *mid-230-c, impaled twice by drowned at minute 100 and 102.* On a beach, drowned with tridents threw from thirteen to twenty blocks, eight a hit; the retreat was chosen and hit while it ran (the retreat says shooters keep shooting). Respawned without its gear, the bot walked 194 blocks back for its drops to the same beach and the same drowned, and died the same way in two minutes, with no armour on. Left in the notes: whether the way back for the drops says what killed the bot there and whether it is still about.
241. *mid-211-c, stranded in the Nether for over an hour.* In the Nether on the blaze-rod step, short of food, the bot turned back for the Overworld to eat; its portal was 250 blocks off at y 83 and it stood at y 39 by the lava sea. The legs on foot made no ground, the staircase toward it rested, and the way back was blocked: 1,546 stillness questions, each offering only "another way" and "mine nearby", and Jev chose the mining 907 times. Nothing on offer answered being stranded: food in the Nether (hoglins), a bridge over the lava, or a portal made where it stood. Left in the notes, next.
242. *mid-231-b, seven deaths in twenty-five minutes; and mid-230-c's second.* After each death the bot went back for its drops: the way back was a rule, taken whenever the kit was on or it was day, and nothing said what had killed the bot there. mid-231-b died four times in two and a half minutes near its base, and mid-230-c walked 194 blocks back, in no armour, to the drowned that had just killed it. The death now records what was about and what the bot wore, and going back is Jev's choice (corpse_run), once a death: told what is there, how far, how long it lasts, what was about when the bot died there, and what it wore then and wears now, against going on with what is carried.
243. *mid-220-c, blown up at minute 81.* At y -27 in full iron at 10.4 health, a skeleton shooting and a creeper seventeen blocks off, Jev chose to dig down three blocks, told "shut in below, none of them reaches it"; fourteen seconds later the creeper went off at the lid. A creeper at the cap goes off through it. The dig down now counts a creeper as still reaching the bot shut in below, and says how soon one about could go off beside it, as the pillar and the pocket do.
244. *mid-205-d, a loop at minute 150, twenty-two minutes into the Nether.* At y 16 in the Nether it needed planks, and the plan chose oak logs, which grow only in the Overworld: the mine step stood still forty-five seconds at a time over a hundred passes, the only detour "work free". The planner knew only which blocks are Nether-only or End-only; trees and the ores of stone are now known as Overworld-only, and an ingredient whose every source lies in another dimension (planks made only from such logs) costs a trip, so in the Nether the planks come from crimson or warped stems.
245. *mid-243-a, blown up at minute 61.* On a hill at night, a skeleton, spiders and two creepers about, the bot began walling a shelter at 11.5 health and stood on one dig of it for thirty seconds, not hit, not moving, the flight record ticking, until a creeper went off beside it. The dig had no bound: a block the server never finished breaking was waited on for ever. A dig is now given up at three times what the game says it takes with the tool held, and a margin, so the next answer comes while there is time for one.
246. *mid-211-e, fell forty blocks at minute 9, not yet understood.* Fetching water for the frame it had chosen to cast (the first cast_frame choice in a trial), walking under the pathfinder to a water source within 48 blocks, the bot went from y 67 to y 28 in two seconds at full health and died of the fall. Drops into water of any depth are off in the movements (a drop is three blocks at most), and nothing hit it. The ground there is to be looked at; a water source at the foot of a ravine, with a route read as reaching it, is the likeliest.
247. *mid-241-a, three hours and no Nether, with the Nether chosen twice.* Jev chose "Nether first" with only the arrows left, the arrows were set aside for half an hour, and the ladder brought them straight back: a set-aside rung returned "as soon as nothing else is left", so the arrows were at once the only step on offer again (stage_arrows, 108 times), and the bot hunted skeletons for three hours. Steps set aside because Jev chose the Nether first now wait for the Nether, and the ladder goes on to it.
248. *mid-227-b, into the lava sea at minute 176, seven minutes into the Nether.* On a fortress leg at y 89 with three magma cubes about, the bot's route stopped on a ledge, and in the next second it went over, fifty-five blocks into the lava sea, unhurt until it landed: a push, most likely a magma cube's. The step back from the edge (note 217) had fired at the cubes a few seconds before, and the route then walked back along the same ledge. Left in the notes: routes in the Nether that keep to ledges over the lava sea with mobs about, and the step back that does not hold.
249. *mid-230-d, burned at minute 46.* Climbing out of an underground lake at y 34, the bot slipped into a lava pit beside it; the way out of lava was steered at the nearest dry cell, a ledge two blocks above its feet, which no jump reaches, and it hopped in place for five seconds and burned. Cells a jump can reach now come before higher ones, and where the only way out is higher the bot pillars up out of the lava on the blocks it carries.
250. *mid-205-e, killed by zombies at minute 48.* Underground at y 6 among five zombies and a drowned, Jev chose the pillar three times at 0.97, and each time the bot was up the step back from the edge (a mob close and a drop beside) took it off its own pillar and back among them. The drop round a pillar is the point of it. Up on its own pillar, the step back no longer applies, but for a heavy hitter's toss.
251. *mid-215-d, a loop at minute 146, five minutes into the Nether.* The fortress search walked its legs, then stood at y 95 unable to reach its next leg's target a hundred blocks south; each forty-five-second stall offered one detour, "another way", taken without asking, and back again. With notes 241 (stranded 250 blocks from the portal) and 248 (routes along ledges over the lava sea), getting about the Nether is the wall after the crossing: legs that bridge, tunnel or turn when the way on foot ends, and detours there that mean something.
252. *mid-242-b, a loop at minute 40.* For the frame it was casting, the bot went for a remembered lava pool at y 24 thirty-three times: each walk followed a partial route some way in and ended no nearer than the first, and a walk that ended eight blocks nearer than it began counted as ground made, so the pool was never set aside. Ground made toward a landmark is now a new nearest approach, as it is for every other walk (note on navigation progress); a walk that comes no nearer than before sets the landmark aside for half an hour.
253. *mid-237-c, three hours and no Nether.* After a step's twenty minutes ran out and it was set aside, the ladder brought it back when nothing else was left, and Jev was handed it alone: the diamond sword forty-eight times, the bow eighteen, with no Nether-first option beside it, because that option was offered only when the step on offer was the first open rung. A step that may wait, back on the ladder, now has the Nether first beside it; taken, it waits for the Nether (note 247).
254. *mid-244-a, a loop at minute 7, not yet fixed.* Levelling its home site at y 56 beside water, each dig let water in and each fill went into water, the bot in it; the survival layer took it back to shore, and the levelling took it back into the water, four times in twenty-seven seconds, over and over. Left in the notes: a site that floods as it is levelled should be given up for another, with the reason said, not levelled again.
255. *Getting about the Nether, first: legs that go on when the way on foot ends (notes 241 and 251).* A fortress-search leg, or a leg of the way back to a known portal, that makes no ground on foot now goes on straight at its target at the height the bot stands, as a player would: through the netherrack where it is rock, and over open air or the lava sea on blocks laid ahead, crouched from the first to the last. The cells ahead are surveyed first, and the crossing goes as far as they allow: rock is dug only where no lava or water is behind it, lava where the body would go stops it, and the blocks carried are counted against those it needs. Ground made on a leg, on foot or by the crossing, is a new nearest approach to its end, as for every walk (note 252); portal legs counted eight blocks gained from wherever the leg began. And a fortress search that never once made ground on a leg never turned: its failures were counted from nothing, which is not a number, and never reached four. Only fixtures run so far (a gap of seventeen blocks and a wall of four at y 95; the lava sea at y 39 with the portal 250 blocks off).
256. *Getting about the Nether, second: when it is stuck there, choices that answer the Nether (note 241).* A stall in the Nether now offers, beside "another way", what meets it, each where it can help and with what it needs: the crossing straight toward where the stalled work was going (the blocks to dig and to lay against those carried, how much nearer it ends, what stops it, about how long); when food is why it is going back, a hoglin to hunt (how many are in view or were seen, two to four raw porkchops each, a one-hoglin fight estimate, whether health comes back meanwhile) and going on in the Nether without the Overworld for twenty minutes (what is carried to eat, what starving does at the difficulty, and that no fight is started meanwhile); and on the way back, a portal built where it stands, with ten obsidian, a lighter and three blocks for the lintel carried, and where it comes out in the Overworld. The survival layer's food question offers the hoglin beside the trip back in the Nether, and leaves the trip out while Jev has chosen to go on without it; so does the hunt's own trip back for food, and the fortress search goes on.
257. *Getting about the Nether, third: routes that keep off the ledges over the lava sea (notes 217 and 248).* In the Nether a cell beside a drop into lava, or a fall of half the health or more, now costs a route as much as a cell with lava beside it: a step's drift or a push off it ends the same way. It is a cost, not a ban, so a narrow span is still walked where it is the way. And the step back from an edge holds: the cells it stepped back from, and those beside the drop within its own reach of them, are kept out of routes while any of the mobs it stepped back from is still about, so the fortress leg's next route does not walk it back along the same ledge.
258. *mid-202-c, called a loop at minute 105 that was not one.* Mining cobblestone for its Nether blocks, the mine and the pickup of each block traded names every few seconds while seventeen to go became twelve; cobblestone is filler to the measure of things gained, so the audit (and the bot's own flip watch) failed it as a loop. The step's own count going down is now progress in both.
259. *mid-229-b, three hours and no Nether: kicked for spam 230 times.* Its wool search (four wool carried, of more than one colour, no target) spun at twenty passes a second saying a changing line each pass, and the server kicked it for chat spam every few seconds to minutes, 230 times; each reconnect began again. The bot's chat already dropped a line repeated within two minutes; it now also keeps under the server's limit, a burst of five and then one each 1.2 seconds, the rest dropped. Left in the notes: the spinning wool search itself, and 2,968 "shelter verification failed" in the same run.
260. *mid-229-b's wool search, spinning for three hours.* Four wool of two colours carried: a bed is three of one colour, and the search reckoned wool short against the total, none. Jev chose the nearest biome to look for sheep in, the walk there failed at once and the target was dropped, and the same biome was offered and chosen again, six times a second. The wool still wanted is now counted by one colour, and a biome the walk could not reach rests ten minutes before it is offered again.
261. *mid-244-b, a loop at minute 36.* Taking the base's bed along was offered with no bed standing at the base (its home unfinished), chosen, and "the bed is not where it was placed" five times. It is offered now only when the bed stands there, or the base is out of sight to say otherwise.
262. *mid-231-d, drowned at minute 46, not yet understood.* Stuck in a night mine at y 16, working free toward the sky, the bot pillared to y 24 and dug up into water: the flooded column rose to y 28 under a lid, it swam up against it and drowned with nothing but granite overhead, the survival layer's surfacing finding no air (the step unchecked for 47 seconds). The option for digging up had said water beside it would pour down onto the bot; what it did not say is that the water would leave no air within reach. Left in the notes.
263. *mid-229-c, shot at minute 24, the sheep search spinning again.* The rest for a biome the walk could not reach (note 260) came only when the walk threw; here each walk returned at once without throwing, no nearer, and the same biome was chosen six times in a second while skeletons shot the bot to death. A walk toward a flock or a biome that ends no nearer than the nearest yet is now a failed walk too, and the place rests.
264. *mid-242-c, the first fortress found, and burned a second later.* Fifty-six minutes to the Nether and thirty more on the fortress search's tenth leg, the bot saw a fortress at (-106, 34, 103), the first any trial has found; walking the lava sea's shore at y 34, three above its surface, it went in a second later, at the moment the leg turned to the fortress, and burned: the way out of lava found no dry cell in the open sea. Left in the notes: what took it in (the route toward the fortress, or a push), and a way out of the open lava sea (blocks placed to stand on, or the shore behind).
265. *mid-244-c, killed by spiders at minute 50.* At night at five health and thirteen hunger, with nothing to eat and so no healing, among spiders and zombies, Jev chose to search for food twice while they came on, and the spiders finished it. With mid-231-c (nine health, twelve hunger), the second death this evening where hunger below eighteen left no healing and every answer was priced beyond the health left. Left in the notes: food, before the night, as its own question.
266. *Every question now knows how the bot died lately (the user's suggestion).* Jev was asked each question afresh: mid-230-c walked back to the drowned that had just killed it, and mid-244-c chose to search for food at five health, as two deaths before it had. A death now keeps the game's own words for it ("was impaled by Drowned"), what was about, what the bot wore, its hunger, and Jev's last choice before it; every question about playing the game is given the deaths of the last two hours (recentDeaths): how, where from here, what was about, and what was chosen last, with a line saying the same answer in the same place seldom ends differently.
267. *mid-227-c, burned in the Nether at minute 156, an hour after crossing.* At y 92 on the fortress search, hurt, the bot stood waiting to heal before its next fight (recover_before_combat) in the open, and something it never saw, most likely a ghast's fireballs, took it from nine health and set it alight. Left in the notes: waiting to heal in the Nether is waiting in the open, and a ghast out of sight is still in range.
268. *mid-211-f, three hours and no Nether: the cast frame's water thrown away.* It chose to cast its frame and spent the run on it: 834 passes filling lava buckets and 202 on the cast, the frame's first slot still walled at the end. Twice, for room for planks, Jev dropped the water bucket, told only that it breaks a fall and puts out fire. With the frame chosen or begun, the question to make room now says the water bucket is what turns each cast block to obsidian and that the frame cannot be cast without it, and an empty bucket is said to be for the frame's lava. Left in the notes: why the lava buckets took 834 passes.
269. *Two things a night asks for, seen to before it (the user's call).* The evening's deaths were mostly out at night, too hungry to heal (hunger under eighteen) with nothing to eat, among crowds, and the scoreboard put nights at a fifth of the time before the Nether with a bed seldom carried. The upkeep question now offers, beside the pickaxe, wood and blocks: taking the base's bed along while it stands within a short walk (what it buys, and that the spawn point and a death's drops go with it), and, in the last minutes of daylight with less than a kit's food carried, finding food before dark (the food carried, the hunger, the seconds to dusk, and that health comes back only at eighteen hunger or more).
270. *Cows bred in the field, for food (the user's call).* The base's pen had a breeding chore that never ran in a midgame trial, the pen never holding two cows, and breeding in the field was for sheep (wool) and chickens (feathers) only. Two cows in view with two wheat carried are now a side trip too, said in food: the calf grown in about twenty real minutes, one to three beef a grown cow, eight hunger a steak cooked, and the food carried now. The sheep's option says its mutton as well as its wool.
271. *mid-231-e, killed by phantoms at minute 73.* Three nights without sleep bring phantoms, and the bot had gone that long, at seven hunger, told of it only in the stay-up option of one question. Every question about playing the game now carries the nights without sleep, and when phantoms come or that they are coming.
272. *mid-243-c, drowned at minute 31 working free under water, the second such tonight (note 262).* Casting its portal frame, the bot was at y 32 to 39 below the site, stuck, and working free one move at a time; its head went under and its air ran out while the air watch fired every five seconds ("0 air and no rescue under way"), the step unchecked for thirty to forty seconds at a time, so the survival layer's surfacing never had the turn. Left in the notes: what held the step unchecked while it drowned, and working free under water with no air within reach.
273. *mid-215-e, the second fortress found, and into the lava sea the next second.* On a Nether leg at y 65, crossing a gap on a laid span toward the fortress search's target, the bot saw a fortress at (202, 65, -58); a hoglin behind it, the fight reflex swung at it and turned the bot about, and the crossing's step, holding forward, walked it the other way: four blocks a second, no route, off the far end, thirty-five blocks into the lava sea. The held-key move turned back to its aim, but kept the keys pressed while it turned. The keys now let go while the heading is wrong and are pressed again once it is right. mid-242-c's fall at its fortress (note 264) was likely the same.
274. *mid-230-e, three hours and no Nether: the food question skipped.* Jev chose to cross twenty-nine times, and the bot still hunted for Nether food over four hundred passes at ten to fourteen points: the food gate is called from the Nether step and from the ladder's own step, and from the ladder's the task carried no decision client, so the question was skipped and the gate hunted. The ladder's call now passes Jev's client.
275. *mid-235-d, a loop at minute 150: the base asking for the bed it had taken along.* Taken along (note 269's upkeep option), the base's bed counted as the base's only while it was in the pack; put down in a nook for the night, the base found its bed gone and asked to claim one at home, three times. Taken along, it is now the base's bed wherever it is until a death drops it, which clears the mark and the claim.
276. *A critical review of every decision question (a Fable reader, all findings taken).* Four fixed now. (1) A piece of armour Jev set aside "Nether first" was still fetched by the combat kit before the crossing: the kit now honours the rung's set-aside. (4) The real-time guidance and every night figure said seven minutes: a night is about eleven real minutes dusk to dawn, and the guidance now names a death's cost plainly, then the minutes. The sword text had the hits wrong (iron four, diamond three); iron armour's text gives three fifths and says a creeper's blast still hurts. (9) The step edge reflex backed the bot off its own pillar and out of a held moving stance, as flee already did not; and keep_working now states when a creeper lights. (10) Recorded here; the rest of the review (nights underground, holding continue_request, a portal-method budget with like facts, one crossing-kit question for the hidden gates, side trips grouped, a fortress-approach question) comes next.
277. *The review's second and third findings: nights underground, and "carry on" asked every pass.* Underground the dark is the same at noon, and its mobs neither spawn more nor burn at dawn, yet at y -50 mid-dig the bot was asked about the night every two minutes: a shelter, the walk home "climbing out of the mine first", staying up "outside in the dark". The climbs out were 110 of 408 pre-Nether minutes. Underground the night is now not a question until two nights awake, when the phantoms waiting on the third make a bed worth the climb; the night comes up with the work, and staying up says underground when it is. And on the surface at hunger eighteen, with food short of the reserve, "get food or carry on" came back every pass while Jev said carry on, each with its route surveys, until one pass said food. Carrying on is now held as a food trip is: five minutes, until hunger falls two or health four, or dusk.
278. *The review's facts, one number for one blast.* A creeper's blast was told as 11, 18.5 and "up to 20 at point blank" for the same encounter by three options, and the guidance said full iron takes three fifths off every hit. The game's explosion formula (power 3) on Normal gives 43 at point blank, 24 at two blocks, 16 at three and 10 at four, and iron's fifteen points take about half off a zombie's hit but only an eighth off a blast. Every option that names a blast now says it by distance after the armour worn, from one function (combat-estimate.js creeperBlast), and the estimate prices it at two blocks, where one goes off beside a player backing from it. A big magma cube hits for 6, and a hoglin's 3 to 8 is said beside its 6.
279. *mid-237-d, three hours in full iron and no Nether; and two of the review's lesser findings.* Its portal was to be cast, and the frame was put where the bot stood, on the surface at y 68, with the lava at y -54 and one bucket carried: every obsidian was a round trip of some hundred and twenty blocks each way, fill_bucket took 1,003 seconds to cast_portal's 186, and none was cast in three hours, with eight iron ingots carried that make two more buckets. Seventy minutes went on nights (a shelter, the night mine, the climbs), which note 277 answers; the cast site and the buckets go with the portal-method work (review #5). Here: the bed is no longer hidden while any mob is within ten blocks; it is offered with the monsters within the game's eight sideways and five up or down counted, as the home bed's was. And beside "armed and armoured", a yes or no that read three iron pieces and a stone sword as no, Jev is told the pieces worn and the weapon.
280. *The review's seventh finding: the win question was a list of fifteen to twenty-five, and the home base seven rungs ahead of the armour.* The ladder's rungs, Nether first and every side trip (loot, trade, travel, breeding, the deep dark, the carried bed and a dozen more) went to Jev as one flat list, the first rung labelled "the ladder's next step" and every other "ahead of the ladder's order"; it was asked again whenever a trip came into view, and every description went twice, in the options and in the state. The home base (site, level, chest, bed, water, plot, pen) sat on the ladder before the armour, though none of it is needed for the Nether, rods or pearls. Now the top level is the rungs, Nether first and one side trip, the trips its children with their facts; rungs are worded alike; a trip coming into view does not re-ask; and the home base is a side trip offered with what it buys and the minutes already spent, taken up from where a half-built one stopped.
281. *The review's eighth finding: the fortress approach, and the reflexes on a span.* Both fortresses found went into the lava sea within a second (notes 264, 273), and how to reach one was the code's alone: a walk at its own height, then at the bricks', a dig down, a span of up to sixty-four blocks, the staircase, and a silent give-up after six. Now a fortress in view more than six blocks off asks Jev (fortress_approach): walk the pathfinder's route (with the cells beside lava counted), dig down, lay a span at this height (the survey's cells, blocks against those carried, how many over lava, what stops it, the mobs in sight, that a hit on a one-wide span over lava is the fall), tunnel, or leave it ten minutes and search on; held while the way makes ground, asked again with what failed. And on a one-wide span over a drop, or while one is laid, the reflexes stay put: the shield turns to no shot, a hunt begins no fight, flee holds crouched instead of stepping, and a mob at arm's length is struck crouched and still, with no critical's jump (a jump lets the sneak go) and no key down; a crouched player with its keys up cannot walk off an edge whichever way it faces.
282. *mid-243-e, knocked forty blocks down by an arrow at minute 22, shield in hand.* Tunnelling toward lava at y 12 with a skeleton in sight, it stood a block from a forty-block drop, and the edge rule walked it to firm ground three blocks off. The pathfinder had moved it half a block in a second and a half when the skeleton's arrow came, the knockback put it over, and the fall took the rest. It carried a shield, which takes an arrow and its knockback whole. The walk off an edge, in flee and in the step's copy, now stops for a shot on its way and the shield comes up to it. And the fortress work's rule (note 281) that the shield not turn to a shot on a one-wide span is undone: the turn is made crouched with every key up, which walks nowhere, and on a span an arrow's knockback is the fall.
283. *The review's fifth and sixth findings: the portal route on a clock, and one question for the kit at the crossing.* The portal method was chosen once and held for good (mid-237-d cast for three hours with one bucket, the frame on the surface at y 68 and its lava at y -54). Now it is asked again every twenty working minutes, the method in hand told what its minutes made (obsidian, the frame's slots standing, diamonds, the distance to a ruin), and every option carries the same facts: the nearest known lava and the walk, the depth of diamonds, the pickaxes, diamonds, obsidian and buckets carried, iron (three a bucket) and the ruins remembered. Two options are new: cast beside the lava, with the round trip per bucket against casting here, and make buckets from the iron carried, with the trips they save. And the gates between "Nether first" and the portal (forty food points, sixteen health, a hundred and twenty-eight blocks, eight logs and a table, a spare pickaxe, the walk home to stash) are one question, crossing_kit: each item what is carried against what it was wanted for and why, cross now or top up one, the stash with its walk; held until the set changes or ten working minutes. The Nether-first option and the state say what the kit is short of now, instead of "nothing left is needed".
284. *mid-220-e, into the lava sea at minute 87, thirty minutes into its first Nether.* The server said "tried to swim in lava": no mob. A magma cube had been fought and charged from y 42, the charge's walk stalled, and the fortress leg's route took the bot down a slope toward the sea, the last of it a drop of three blocks to the shore at y 30; it landed in the lava and burned from twenty in four seconds, the way out not found. The route never plans a cell of lava (a probe of drops of one to three onto lava of every level finds none), but a drop cannot be steered: the body carries on past the cell it was aimed at until it lands. A drop of two or more onto a cell with lava beside it at the feet or the floor is now not a route at all; a step down of one, and walking the shore, keep the lava-beside cost.
285. *mid-239-b, slain by zombies at minute 9, having chosen the pillar nine times and never gone up.* At the foot of its staircase at y 9, zombies coming down the stairs above it, Jev chose the pillar from seventeen health down to three. Each pass the swing at the zombie at arm's length came first, and it jumped for a critical: the bot's height went 10, 11, 10, 11 through the fight, and the pillar was measured and run from the air, its feet floored a block high, its own headroom check refusing it (no pillar was begun). Now a swing while a stance other than the fight holds is plain, no jump, and the stance question and its run wait for the feet to land before anything is measured. And the pillar was told as out of every walker's reach, with the zombies a block and two above the bot's feet on the stairs: it now says which mobs stand above the feet, how far up, and that two up is within their reach.
286. *mid-215-f, a loop at minute 65: lava dug toward through a resting staircase.* Fetching lava for its cast, the bot stood over a pool a block or three below it with no scooping spot a route reached, so each pass dug a staircase toward the nearest lava; that staircase had been set aside after three rounds without getting nearer, the dig was refused at once, the stall was asked about, and the next pass chose the same lava, some thirty times in a minute. The lava fetch now counts lava whose staircase rests as no lava here: the other pools known are walked to, else the deep lava on the first heading whose staircase is not resting, and with every way resting it says so rather than trying again.
287. *mid-235-e, a loop at minute 57 on its first Nether search (the Nether at minute 41).* On an island in the lava sea with twenty-nine blocks carried, every leg of the fortress search needed a span; the stall offered the crossing (the survey counting twenty-nine to lay against twenty-nine carried), Jev took it, and it came back within a second with nothing laid, forty times in a few minutes, the leg turning between the crossing and the search until the flip watch ended the trial. Why each span stopped went only to a field nobody read. A crossing that ends no nearer now rests five minutes from that eight-block area toward that target, with its reason, and is neither run nor offered again meanwhile: the stall's other answers, another heading or the staircase, come up instead.
288. *mid-229-d, a loop at minute 150: a cast frame's slot waiting to drain.* A slot of the frame held flowing water, and the cast waits for flowing water to run off; it never did, for twenty minutes, while the bot wandered up to fifty blocks from the frame between stalls. The cast's own water, poured on the last slot's lava, is scooped back after, but only from where the bot stood: out of reach the scoop failed every pass, quietly, and the source it left ran on into the next slot. The scoop now walks back to the water first; and a slot of flowing water with a source feeding it (at its level or above, within three blocks) has that source scooped rather than waited on.
289. *mid-227-d, slain by a drowned at minute 105, sinking in a flooded pit.* Waiting out a zombie beside the lava it had come for, in one iron piece, the bot went six blocks down into water with a drowned in it. Asked four times, Jev chose the fight, the fight, the pillar and the bunker, while the bot sank from y 19 to 14 and its air ran low; no option said it was in water, and none was the way out. The stances now say so in water (the air, that the bot sinks unless it swims, that a drowned swims faster), the pillar, the pocket, the bunker and digging down are not offered there (none holds in water), and swimming for dry ground, digging a step into the bank if need be, is a stance of its own.
290. *mid-230-f, "doomed to fall" at minute 75 in a ravine.* Fetching lava for obsidian, the bot stepped back from an edge with zombies below and the walk ended thirteen blocks down on a one-wide ledge at y -28, twenty health to ten. There the span rule (note 281) held it still and crouched, with the zombies at arm's length: they hit it from ten to seven and the knockback put it off the ledge, twenty-three blocks down. Crouching stops a walk off an edge, not a hit's throw. On a one-wide ledge with a mob that hits within six blocks, the open sides are now walled first, the floor beside the feet and a block on it (four blocks for a ledge open both ways): a player thrown into a wall stays where it is, and the ledge is no longer one. How the walk from the edge came to fall thirteen blocks is not yet seen.
291. *mid-231-f, slain by a drowned at minute 89, swimming.* Crossing water on a food search with a drowned beside it, the bot was held still and crouched, "on a span": the edge measure took open water on both sides for a drop, so the span rule (note 281) fired in the sea and a crouched swimmer sinks. After a restart the swim across went on for sixteen seconds while the drowned hit it from twelve to nothing: the swim loops checked the air and never the mobs, and the threat was logged each tick without anything answering it. Water is now no drop, beside the feet or under a ledge (a fall into it does not hurt), and the swims across and for land check for a mob at the swimmer, which hands the turn to the encounter question and its way out of the water (note 289).
292. *mid-242-e, two deaths in seventeen minutes.* First, a fight Jev chose was held while a creeper came on, and the fight's run closes on the nearest mob within eight blocks: it ran at the creeper, twenty health to one; eaten back to seven, it ran at the next, and was blown up. The fight option itself said closing on a creeper is walking into its fuse. A charge is now never at a creeper (physical safety: a run at one ends beside it as it lights); held facing it, the swing takes it where it comes, as in the dance. Second, respawned, Jev chose to seal a pocket with two zombies at arm's length beside a hole twenty-two deep, and a hit threw the bot into it before that side was walled. A pocket now walls the side over a drop first, from the bottom.
293. *mid-241-d, "suffocated in a wall" at minute 36, at 1.1 health (not fixed).* Casting its portal at y -54 it lost health a point a second from eighteen to two (fire or lava beside the slot, by the rate; the record does not say), healed to eight, and a fall on a span (the water-as-drop measure of note 291 may have been it) took it to two. With hunger at sixteen and no food carried it could not heal, and Jev chose to climb for food each time it was asked, rightly: only food heals there. Climbing a staircase it lost a point at y -9 and its last at the top step, suffocating. What put its head in a block is not in the flight record (a gravel fall, a server position correction); the vitals frames carry no cause of damage. Next: the cause of each damage event recorded with its frame (the server's damage type), so a point lost is traceable.
294. *The flight record says what hurt the bot.* Note 293's death could not be traced: the vitals frames showed each point lost and nothing of why. The server's damage event now makes a frame of its own, "hurt: in wall", "hurt: mob attack by zombie", with its damage type named from the registry the server sends at login and the mob that caused it, for the bot's own damage only.
295. *mid-231-g, blown up at minute 11 in its own shaft.* At dusk with a creeper eight blocks off, Jev chose the shelter and the shaft pocket, told of the creeper; the bot dug five blocks down and the creeper came down the open shaft on top of it (the first death the damage frames of note 294 named: "player explosion by creeper"). The seal counts a creeper's walk against the blocks left before every block (note 226); the shaft did not count at all. It now does, before the first block and each after, pricing the dig as a dig (each block's dig with the tool it takes and the drop into it, then the cap) rather than as blocks placed; with the creeper winning the race the shaft rests half a minute and the next stance answers the creeper.
296. *mid-235-f, slain by zombies at minute 28 in a tunnel, told the fight cost nothing.* Mining diamond at y 7 in full iron with an iron sword, three zombies came down the tunnel. The fight estimate bounds the biters at arm's length by the open cells round the bot, and in the one-wide tunnel, and later in the shaft it dug, it counted none: "0 damage" with zombies at 2.3 blocks, and again at 5.9 health with one at 0.4. Between those, with the count at five, it said 25.7. Jev went pillar, fight, pillar, dig down and fight (the last at 0.91 on "0 damage") while the hits came each second. The estimate now counts the biters already within three blocks however few cells are open, in the fight and in every stance's price.
297. *mid-242-d, three hours and no Nether: a cast two hundred blocks from its lava, never asked about again.* The frame stood at x -257; the nearest lava pool known was underground at y 35 and was marked spent when the walk there found no lava from the surface, so each bucket came from a pool 212 blocks off: return_to_portal took 33 minutes and the walks to lava 24. The portal method is to be asked again every twenty working minutes (note 283), with casting beside the lava among the answers, but its clock counted a pass's time only when the pass ended, and saved it only on a later save: passes minutes long, with the bots restarted every few minutes for the fixes above, counted nothing. The portal method's clock and the crossing kit's now count as the pass runs, ten seconds at a time, and save as they go.
298. *mid-244-e, "doomed to fall by Ghast" at minute 116, two minutes into its Nether (reached at 114).* On a fortress leg along a ledge at y 89, a ghast in sight at seventeen to nineteen blocks shot, and the fireball threw the bot off: twenty-nine blocks down (the damage frames, note 294: "fireball by ghast", then "fall"). It wore full iron with a shield, and nothing answered the ghast: a shooter counted as a threat only within sixteen blocks, the reach of a bow, and neither the shield, the step off the edge from a ghast (note 219) nor the encounter question came. A shooter is now a threat within its own reach (a ghast's forty, a witch's ten, a blaze's sixteen), so a ghast in sight brings the shield, the edge rule and the stance question.
299. *mid-227-e, a loop at minute 53: a cast frame where it could not be cast.* Casting beside its lava (note 283), the frame went down at y 28 in a cave; each pass failed, "nowhere to stand to pour into the frame slot", and after the bot pillared fifteen blocks up to work free, "no route", until the stall watch counted four and ended the trial. A frame was moved only when its ground had been dug away. A cast that fails three passes running at its site with no obsidian in the frame now leaves the site, remembered with why, and another is chosen at least six blocks from it.
300. *mid-235-g, slain by drowned at minute 32, eating that never finished.* Two drowned at arm's length on a tunnel's shore; Jev chose to eat at twelve health and again at nine and at five. The meal began every half second and was never eaten (hunger seventeen throughout, the damage frames a drowned's hit every half second to a second), and the stance was held or chosen again each time. What cut each meal short is not in the record (a swing, the hand swapped, a new consume cancelling the last). However it was cut, a meal not eaten now keeps the eat stance off the list for ten seconds, so the question is answered from the others.
301. *mid-242-f, into the lava sea at minute 49, eight minutes into its Nether (reached at 41).* In the Nether the ladder still wanted iron (armour for the fight, review finding 1), and the iron ore step, finding no ore about, set a staircase toward the ore's depth, y 16, below the lava sea; eleven blocks down it broke into a cave over the lava, fell ten blocks into it, and burned from thirteen in three seconds ("lava", the damage frames said). An ore step for a block found only in another dimension now says so ("no iron ore in the nether: it is only found in the overworld") instead of digging for it, so the stall sets the step aside or takes the way back.
302. *mid-218-b, a loop at minute 106: a portal corner with nothing to place against.* Building its frame from carried obsidian, the bot's temporary corner support at (-384, 66, 692) had no solid block beside it, and "no adjacent solid anchor" came back 126 times over eight minutes of passes until the stall watch ended the trial. Note 299's rule (three failed passes with no obsidian in the frame leave the site) covered only a cast frame; it now covers any frame but a ruin's, whichever way it is being built.
303. *mid-205-g, slain by a zombie at minute 138 under phantoms.* At night at eight health, with a zombie and two phantoms diving, Jev chose between retreat and the pillar, the pillar twice, told that two up is out of reach of all but a list of climbers; phantoms were not on it, and the pillar was no answer to what was hitting it. The climbers' list now carries the fliers: a phantom (only a roof keeps it off), a blaze, a vex, a bee, an allay. Whether note 277's nights underground, which let two nights pass without a question, brought the phantoms on sooner is to be watched.
304. *mid-207-d, a loop at minute 138: every route "partial" (not fixed).* Beside its half-cast frame at (25, 69, 317), phantoms about and its pockets full (Jev dropping coal to make room), the bot's every walk came back "no route (partial)" with no path at all: the log it was mining at (24, 71, 301), the work-free moves Jev chose, the climb to the surface (failing three times, resting three minutes each). No way was found out of wherever it stood; the cast's temporary walls boxing it in is the likeliest, not seen in the record. Next: a stuck bot's surroundings (the blocks round its feet) written to the flight record when every route fails, so the box can be seen.
305. *mid-229-e, slain by zombies at minute 73 in its own shaft.* Mining gold at y -21 with three zombies about, the fight and then the retreat were chosen, and the retreat's shelter was a shaft pocket dug where it stood; two zombies followed it down the open shaft and stood in its cell, a hit a second for ten seconds while the dig went on, eighteen health to five; the seal Jev chose then came too late. Note 295 stopped the shaft for a creeper that would reach it before the cap; a biter at arm's length now stops it too, before the first block and each after.
306. *mid-202-e, three hours and no Nether: nights with no bed, four wool in the pockets.* Nights were the half of it again (37 minutes in a shelter, 28 night-mining, 47 climbing back up), and the bot carried no bed all run with two black wool, a light gray and a gray: three of one colour make a bed, and a sheep of the right colour was searched for. Wool of any colour is dyed with a dye and the game's recipe book has it; a bone makes three bone meal, three white dye. Now wool of mixed colours with the white dye to hand (bone, bone meal, dye or a lily of the valley) counts as a white bed's: the bed steps dye three white first and then craft the bed, and the planner, where no one kind of wool covers every craft, splits the crafts across the wool carried (it had planned a black dye to make a third black). The bed option says that mixed wool is dyed. Whether a bone was carried at the right time in this run is not clear; the rule is for the next.
307. *mid-230-g, into the lava at minute 75, ten minutes after finding a fortress (the Nether at 61, the fortress at 65).* Waiting to heal before the blazes at y 59 by the fortress, it was hit by four ghast fireballs in six seconds and the fourth threw it into the lava ("tried to swim in lava to escape Ghast"). The ghast was never in view: the fireballs came from further out, and nothing answered a shot whose shooter was not a threat seen. A shot on its way (a fireball, an arrow) is now a threat of its own when nothing else is: the survival turn is taken, the shield comes up to it, and a fireball counts as a ghast's push for the step back from a deadly edge. The wait to heal in the open (review lesser finding) is still where it stood.
308. *mid-202-f, froze to death at minute 4 on a mountain at y 121.* Climbing its staircase up to find food it came into powder snow; the way out of the snow was taken once, and then the staircase took it back down into the snow, where it froze a point every two seconds from twenty to nothing while the food question was asked every half second (the damage frames: "freeze", ten times). The escape was never taken again: it is run when the bot's cells are seen to be powder snow, and powder snow has no collision box. The server's freezing damage now sends the bot out as the blocks do; with no way through the snow found, it walks to the nearest firm ground; and firm ground is no longer a cell of powder snow.
309. *mid-211-g, three hours and no Nether: a cast that never cast.* After forty working minutes on the cast, the portal question (asked twice, note 297's clock working) still said ten of ten to cast, and Jev kept the cast at 0.84 and 0.97, told the nearest lava was a pool seven blocks off. The lava fetch was not using that pool: its staircase rested, and every bucket went to staircases toward the deep lava instead, 114 of them set aside for pacing, 38 minutes of fetching for nothing. One bucket was carried and no iron for more. The lava the portal question is told of is now lava the fetch would use (not a pool whose way rests), so the question sees the cast as it is. Why the pool's way rested, seven blocks off, is not in the record.
310. *mid-242-g, into the lava at minute 27, seven minutes into its Nether (reached at 20).* In iron with no gold, a piglin went for it (piglins attack a player wearing no gold on sight): twenty to fourteen to eight in two blows, and the second threw it off the edge into the lava ("tried to swim in lava to escape Piglin"). Golden boots were a rung Jev could set aside for the Nether first, and the crossing kit said nothing of gold. The kit now carries it: a piece of golden armour, what piglins do without one, and a top-up that makes golden boots (worn in the Nether when carried).
311. *mid-243-f, a loop at minute 66: the walk to the lava for casting beside it.* Jev had chosen to cast beside the lava (note 283); the pool was fourteen to eighteen blocks off, below a hillside, and every walk to within six of it ended where it began, the bot going up and down the hill between y 73 and 86 for minutes until the flip watch ended the trial. Three walks there that come no nearer now let the lava go: the frame is cast where the bot is, the pool left with why. (No unit test: nothing in the suite drives the portal step this far; the rule is small and read by hand.)
312. *mid-244-g, "doomed to fall by Spider" at minute 59 in a ravine.* Climbing out, spiders came up after it; it held on its pillar, came down, fought, and Jev chose the fight at 0.39 told no drop was within three blocks, and one second later a spider's hit put it twenty-two blocks down. The no-route frame of note 304, from a moment before, showed open air under the bot's own cell: it stood on the corner of a block over the edge. The drop measure looked at the cells round the feet and never under them; the bot's own cell is now measured first, and the stance options say a drop under the bot as such.
313. *mid-231-h, shot by a skeleton at minute 96, charging at 3.7 health.* At night on the surface with three skeletons, Jev chose the charge at a shooter (0.77), told of the one in sight sixteen blocks off and an estimate of 2.8 damage; two more were three and four blocks off out of sight, and two arrows ended it. The fight option says the mobs out of sight about; the charge did not. It does now.
314. *mid-218-c, a loop at minute 58: a part-cast frame with nowhere to stand.* The frame at (-231, 65, 733) had slots cast, and the next slot had no cell beside it with a floor that the pours could be made from: "nowhere to stand to pour" two hundred and one times. Note 302's rule leaves a site only while no obsidian is in the frame, and obsidian cast is not taken back without a diamond pickaxe, so this frame stayed. With no stand beside a slot, one is now made: a temporary block put under a cell that would do otherwise (placed against solid ground within two blocks, recorded with the cast's other temporary blocks). Not unit-tested: the test world's own temporary walls always gave a floor, so the case did not arise there; what took the floor away in the trial is not in the record.
315. *mid-227-f, slain by zombies at minute 79, a meal begun nine times.* The same as note 300: three zombies at arm's length by its cast frame, Jev chose to eat at nine health, and the beef was begun every half second and never eaten, hunger falling from seventeen to sixteen. Note 300's mark on a meal cut short was cleared whenever the consume came back, and it came back in half a second with nothing eaten. A meal now counts as eaten only if hunger or saturation went up; otherwise it is cut short, and the eat stance stays off the list for ten seconds.
316. *mid-242-h, into the lava at minute 45, twenty-five minutes into its Nether (reached at 20).* Laying a span toward a fortress, a blaze in sight stopped the span (as it should) and its fireballs came: the shield blocked four (note 307), and the bot drifted half a block with each blocked shot, crouch released between, until it went off the one-wide span thirty blocks into the lava. Note 290 walls a span's open sides for a biter within six blocks; shots on their way and a shooter in sight now bring the walls too.
317. *mid-211-h, into the lava at minute 52, two minutes into its Nether (reached at 50).* On a ledge at y 89 over the lava sea at six health, with a magma cube four blocks off, Jev chose retreat and then the pillar, told that walkers cannot reach a player two up; the cube jumped up to it, hit it to three and threw it off, fifty-five blocks into the lava ("to escape Magma Cube"). The climbers' list now carries the jumpers: a magma cube (higher than two blocks, and its hit throws) and a slime.
318. *mid-241-e, a loop at minute 129: a cast frame thirteen blocks below with no walk down.* Its cast frame stood at y -9; the bot came up to y 4 (for food) and every walk back returned "no path", thirty-nine times, a walk not digging down. A cast frame more than eight blocks off is now walked to first, and when the walk fails the staircase is dug to it, as a lit portal's is. (No unit test: the walk here is the real navigation, which the suite's cast fixtures replace.)
319. *mid-244-h, a loop at minute 38: note 318's walk to the frame, undoing the lava fetch.* Note 318 walked the bot back to a cast frame more than eight blocks off at the start of every cast pass; the lava fetch is a cast pass too, so each staircase toward the deep lava was four steps down when the next pass walked it back up, until the flip watch ended the trial. The walk to the frame is now made only with something to cast (a lava bucket or obsidian in hand).
320. *mid-243-g, burned to death at minute 44, four minutes into its Nether (reached at 40), not fixed.* On a fortress leg at y 41 with its route heading east, the bot moved west two blocks a second, went over an edge ten blocks into the lava sea, climbed out at eight health and burned on, a point a second, standing to recover (in the Nether there is no water to put a player out). What moved it against its route is not in the record: no damage before the lava, no controller, the pathfinder running. The flight record's observations now carry the bot's velocity, whether it stands on the ground, and the keys held, so the next one can be read.
321. *mid-235-h, slain by zombies at minute 92 (not fixed).* Walking to a landmark with four zombies about, in full iron with an iron sword at ten health, the options were all bad and said so honestly: the fight at 25.7 damage against 10.3 health, a pocket of thirteen blocks with a zombie at arm's length, and no way to run that passed none of them. Jev chose the fight (0.53) and it went as told. The facts were right; what put the bot among four zombies at ten health on a walk is upstream, and not yet looked at.
322. *mid-211-i, ended as a loop at minute 13 while getting there.* Walking to the lava to cast beside it, the walk failed and the staircase took over, a block nearer a pass (thirty-one blocks to twenty-three), but each pass tried the walk again first, five seconds failing, so the step turned between the walk and the tunnel and the flip watch counted it a loop. Once the walk to the casting lava has failed, the staircase now goes on pass after pass without it; note 311's three passes with no ground made still let the lava go.
323. *mid-244-f, three hours and no Nether, four minutes of it a spin.* With a creeper left be (keep_working) and a skeleton at under three blocks, the skeleton stopped the work each pass while the stance, held for the creeper, did not see it: its line of sight read differently to the threat check and to the stance's list a moment later. The pass spun twenty times a second for four minutes, the skeleton shooting it from seven health to under three. The mob that stopped the work is now always among the stance's mobs, so a newcomer at arm's length ends the held stance and the question is asked. (No unit test: the flicker is of real sight lines.) The run's other hours went on the surface climbs for food (36 minutes) and the walks to the casting lava (15).
324. *mid-227-g, a loop at minute 33: the kit's sword and the staircase's pickaxe.* The combat kit, run before the crossing each pass, put the sword in the hand; the staircase to the lava put the pickaxe back; the next pass the sword again, until the flip watch counted it a loop. The hand holds the tool of the work in hand: a sword carried is ready (the fight takes it up), and the kit no longer puts it in the hand, readiness judged as kitReady judges it.
325. *mid-244-j, "speared by Zombie" at minute 6.* At ten health with spear zombies ten blocks off, Jev chose to retreat; the next pass survival had nothing to answer (the zombies outside its eight), and the loop's loot came next, a minecart just spotted: the bot stopped to open it and the zombies were on it in four seconds, their spears six a hit, ten to none. Loot now waits while a hostile is in sight within sixteen blocks.
326. *mid-242-i, a loop at minute 43: a cast frame in a hillside.* "Nowhere to stand to pour" a hundred and seven times at (12, 73, 63): the frame, part cast, had rock where a stand would be, and note 314 only put a floor under an open cell. With no open cell and no floor to make, a stand is now dug out beside the slot (feet and head, the fewest digs, never a frame cell, the slot's walls or a temporary block).
327. *mid-242-j, a loop at minute 3: a staircase dig refused for the drop it would open.* The staircase toward iron ore chose a step whose dig the digger refuses ("refusing to open a drop beside the feet"), four times a pass, and the mine step and the tunnel turned between themselves until the flip watch ended the trial; the staircase rested only after rounds without ground made. A staircase dig refused for a drop, or for digging beneath the feet, now rests the staircase at once, as a step with no safe way does, so another heading is taken.
328. *mid-243-h, a loop at minute 44: a part-cast frame on a mountain, "nowhere to stand" after a stand was dug.* At (63, 104, -11) the stand was dug out twice (note 326) and still no pour could be made from it; the frame had obsidian cast, so note 302's rule (three failures, nothing cast) never left the site, and it failed ninety-five times. A frame with obsidian cast now leaves its site after ten failures in a row: a cast block is one lava trip, less than the minutes a stuck site costs. What blocked the pours from the dug stand is not in the record.
329. *mid-231-i, blown up at minute 77 at two health.* Searching for food at 2.2 health, the bot had a creeper follow it in from nine blocks to two with no stance asked, and it went off. A mob Jev chose to leave be (keep_working) stayed left be until three blocks, which for a creeper is where its fuse lights. A creeper left be is now a threat again once within its fuse's reach and a second and a half's walk (seven and a half blocks). Whether the creeper here was one left be or one a charge could not reach (still left be to five, trial 73's ledge) is not in the record.
330. *mid-229-f, into the lava at minute 133, three minutes into its Nether (reached at 130).* On a fortress leg after a retreat, walking down a slope with the route running and no hit taken, the bot dropped twenty blocks from y 51 into the lava sea: the same as mid-243-g (note 320), and again not explained by the record (the new velocity fields came only on damage frames). One mechanism fits both: the walk's gap jumps, allowed where the columns jumped over are survivable, but a jump that falls short lands where the body goes, and in the Nether that can be the sea. Gap jumps are now off in the Nether (a physical-safety rule; a gap there is bridged or walked round). If such a fall comes again, this was not it.
331. *mid-218-d, a loop at minute 88: at sea with no shore.* In open water the shore search found no land within sixty-four blocks, and "no reachable dry shore" came back every pass while the bot drifted, until the stall watch ended the trial. Out at sea with no land candidate, none in view and no unloaded ground about, the bot now swims for the land remembered (the sea crossing), and else holds one heading twenty seconds a pass until land is in view, turning when a heading is blocked. (No unit test: the swim runs twenty real seconds.) Also: the flight file's slimmed observation frames now keep the velocity, footing and keys (note 320's fields were being dropped from them).
332. *mid-202-g, into the lava at minute 133, eighteen minutes into its Nether (reached at 115).* On a span a block over the lava sea it held still, crouched, four seconds with a ghast in sight twenty-two blocks off; note 316's walls came only for shots on their way or a shooter within eight, so none went up before the fireball, which threw the bot into the lava. A shooter in sight within its own reach (a ghast's forty) now brings the walls.
333. *mid-205-h, slain by zombies at minute 153 in cave water (not fixed).* Searching for food underground at 13.8 health, the bot walked into three zombies; Jev chose the fight and then the pillar (0.97), the pillar did not go up (the bot ended two blocks lower, in water), and the fight went on in the water to the end, retreat and pillar chosen again at low confidence as health fell. Why the pillar came down instead of up is not in the record; note 289's water stances did not apply until it was in the water, by which time three zombies were at arm's length.
334. *mid-227-h, slain by a magma cube at minute 57 after two falls, fourteen minutes into its Nether (reached at 43).* The first record with velocity and keys (note 331) showed it: at the end of a stretch of span at y 106 the bot slid on with no key held, velocity 0.11 a tick, off the end, ten blocks down, then fifteen more, from twenty to two among magma cubes. The span let go of the walk and the crouch at once, and a crouching player holds at an edge only while crouched; the step's way on carried it over. The crouch is now let go only once the body has stopped (half a second at most).
335. *mid-230-h, "obliterated by a sonically-charged shriek" at minute 151.* Recovering at y -52 in the deep dark, the bot stood still while a warden sixteen blocks off behind the rock boomed it for ten a time; nothing reacted, since the warden was out of sight and past the eight blocks a biter counts at. A warden's boom passes walls and reaches about twenty: a warden within twenty-four is now a threat seen or not, the flee counts it, and the shelter stances (pillar, bunker, pocket, dig down) say it reaches them, so no stance claims to shut it out.
336. *mid-243-i, "impaled by Drowned" at minute 43.* In full iron with a shield, on the way to the portal, the bot met a drowned with a trident ten blocks off; the fight and the retreat were tried and failed, and Jev chose the pillar (every option said more damage than the bot had). Two up, the held pillar returned at once each pass, nothing done and nothing reported: the loop ran twenty passes a second for twelve seconds, the hurt watchdog stopped the work at each hit, and four tridents took twenty health to none. The shield never rose because a thrown trident was not among the projectiles the guard looks for. A trident flying at the bot is now incoming (the shield goes up to it as to an arrow), and the pillar held up top faces the nearest mob and waits a quarter second a pass, reported as pillar_hold, so the swing and the shield do the work.
337. *mid-211-j, a loop at minute 86: a portal that would not light.* The cast frame at (385, 67, -223) was whole, ten obsidian, but a cobblestone stood inside it at (386, 70) that was not among the frame's recorded temporary walls (a stand or a scaffold, the record does not say which); the walls were cleared before lighting, the flint and steel set fire beside the cobblestone, the portal did not fill, and the wait for it timed out a hundred and twenty-five times until the loop watch ended the trial. Before lighting, every block inside the frame now comes out, whatever put it there: the portal takes only air and fire.
338. *mid-218-e, "slain by Spider" at minute 30, sitting in a boat.* A boat crossing had reached its far side when the bots were restarted (for note 336), and the server put Jev back in its boat on joining. Mineflayer runs no physics for a rider, and the work did not know it was seated: the session hung minutes on end (no survival check for two and a half minutes; the stall watch said so and could not act), was restarted again (for note 337) into the same seat, and a spider took it from twenty to none where it sat, the position not moving an inch through the knockback. A crossing always leaves its boat before it returns, so a rider between work passes is one left there: the loop now gets out first, and a seat given within five seconds of joining is left at once.
339. *mid-244-i, a loop at minute 106: on a mineshaft bridge over a cave at y 3, no way up offered that could work.* Down for lava at the bottom of the world, the bot stood on a one-wide plank bridge with open cave on every side. Every staircase heading's first stair had no floor ("no floor 5"), eight headings set aside in twenty seconds; straight up was ruled out by gravel in the column; the staircase was the only way out offered, chosen again at each ask, and the loop watch ended the trial. Two things are fixed. The climb now also offers laying a level span of blocks across the open cave toward the way up (bridging.js, crouched, surveyed first: cells, blocks laid of carried, what it stops at, cells over lava), asked again from the span's end; and the climb is asked again whenever a way not offered before is open, not only the column. And while it stood there with zombies about, the step off the edge (a deadly drop two blocks off) had no ground three from a drop and fell back to ground merely not beside one, which was the cell it stood on: it walked to where it was, twenty passes a second for minutes. A step off the edge now never targets the feet.
340. *mid-229-g, "burned to death" at minute 52, sixteen minutes into its Nether (reached at 36).* On a fortress leg a ghast's fireballs came in one after another; at 13.8 health Jev chose to seal a pocket, and from 22:00 the bot took ten in-fire hurts in seven seconds, from eleven health to none, while the seal was built: the fire the fireball lit was where it stood. The out-of-fire reflex (vitals.js, note on trial 101) runs in the survival layer's turn, and the hurt watchdog, which would have handed it that turn, gives way to a stance Jev holds. Standing in fire is now never a stance carried out: the watchdog stops it, as for no stance. And the server's in-fire hurt counts as being in fire, as its freeze hurt counts as being in powder snow (note 308), whatever the blocks at the feet show.
341. *mid-207-e, three hours and no Nether.* The cast was chosen twenty-two times and the portal never made: twelve frame sites were left, seven of them for "refusing to open a drop beside the feet", which came from the tunnel down to the lava (the fill_bucket step), not from the site. Note 302's count of a site's failures counted every failure of the cast, the lava trips' too, so three refusals far below a good site left it and a new site was picked, the lava walked for again from there. Only a failure within twelve blocks of the frame counts against its site now; one on the way to the lava is the trip's. Where the rest of the run went: filling buckets 29 minutes, the night mine 24, mining 24, smelting 13, surface climbs 12.
342. *mid-242-k, a loop at minute 76, twenty-five minutes into its Nether (reached at 51): the fortress leg and a block of crimson nylium.* At (42, 57, 0) in a crimson forest, the leg south was blocked at head height by crimson nylium, which neither the staircase's list of ground it may dig (tunneling.js natural) nor the crossing's (bridging.js) had: every step south was "crimson nylium in the way", the crossing stopped after two cells, and the tunnel paced the same three cells, the leg turned, until the loop watch ended the trial on the crossing and the leg trading names. The Nether's forest floor and trees (both nyliums, the wart blocks, shroomlight, stems and hyphae, glowstone) are now ground to dig through; magma is still not.
343. *mid-230-i, a loop at minute 39: making obsidian, the same lava dug for thirteen passes.* The staircase toward the lava at (240, 9, 1037) was refused each time for the drop it would open, and rested (note 327); the obsidian step chose the same lava again at the next pass, as the lava-bucket step did before note 286's rule, and the loop watch ended the trial. The obsidian step now skips lava whose staircase is resting, as collectLava does: the pour spots, the pools here, the lake remembered, and the deep lava on the four headings, in that order; with every one resting it says so.
344. *mid-205-i, "slain by Spider" at minute 58.* Mining iron at y 38 in iron armour with a stone sword, the bot met three spiders, one on a ledge a block above its head; Jev chose to fight. The fight and its swing at the mob in reach (defend) traded the survival layer's name four times in two seconds, and the flip watch took that for a stall ("turning between defend and fight") and set the swing aside: from there no swing is in the record, only the fight chosen again every three seconds while the spiders took it from nine health to none. The swing is now one of the fight's own moves, as the raised shield was, and so is the pillar held up top (note 336); the run out of fire is an emergency, never set aside.
345. *mid-235-i, "shot by Skeleton" at minute 137, at y 5 with a skeleton and a zombie.* Jev chose to dig down at 6.7 health; the shaft pocket it runs had failed a moment before ("the saved shelter has no safe approach") and was resting, so its report threw SetAside. That was thrown out through the stance: the stance was not recorded as failed, stayed held, ran again at every tick and was refused again, the survival turn ended with nothing done, and the work step's threat check spun a hundred times a second while an arrow landed every three seconds, from 6.7 to none. A stance whose action is resting is now a stance that failed: set aside for twenty seconds here, and the next tick chooses from what is left.
346. *mid-205-j, a loop at minute 11: every way down toward gold set aside.* With no gold in view, the mine step goes down toward the ore's depth along one of four headings twenty-four blocks off; at (-296, 49, 637) all four staircases were resting, and "every way down toward the gold ore from here is set aside" came back fifteen passes running, the stall's mine-nearby detour between, until the loop watch ended the trial. The ways down are now eight headings at twenty-four and forty-eight blocks, sixteen staircase areas, near first.
347. *mid-207-f, "tried to swim in lava" at minute 32, at y -55.* Tunnelling to a deepslate iron ore, a block beside a lava source was dug a block from the bot's feet ((43, -55, 286), the source at (43, -55, 287)); the lava ran into the gap and onto the bot in a second and a half, and it burned from twenty to none trying to get out through it. The staircase's own digs look for lava behind the rock (safeExcavation), but the dig itself did not, only asking afterwards whether to plug the leak. A block with lava beside it (level or above) is now never dug within the lava's run of the bot (three blocks in the Overworld, seven in the Nether): a hard rule for the body's safety. Refused on a staircase, it rests the staircase, as a refused drop does.
348. *mid-218-f, a loop at minute 49: a ruined portal's corner with nothing to place against.* The frame adopted from a ruin at (-384, 66, 692) needed a cobblestone support at its corner, and the corner had no solid block beside it: "no adjacent solid anchor", 125 failures at the site. Note 302's leaving rule passed over ruins (a ruin cannot be moved), so nothing left it until the loop watch ended the trial. A ruin that fails at its site ten times is now marked as no frame to finish, and the way to a portal is asked again without it on offer. Clearing a ruin's slots and inside now counts as work at the site too.
349. *mid-202-h, "shot by Skeleton" at minute 94, at y 47 by a stream.* A skeleton six blocks off and a creeper at twenty-one; the fight chosen at 20:16 failed and rested, and at 5.8 health Jev chose to charge the skeleton, which failed at once without a step: the option was offered by one water test and refused by the run's other (the body in the stream) or by the feet being in the air, and the charge rested twenty seconds too. What was left was a pillar, bunker, pocket or dig-down each said to cost more than the bot had (the creeper counted as coming), and leaving the mobs be; Jev chose the last three times, at 5.8, 4.9 and 3.2 health, and the arrows ended it. The charge is now offered only where its run takes it (out of the water by either test), and the run waits up to a second for the feet rather than refusing mid-hop.
350. *mid-227-i, "slain by Magma Cube" at minute 85, twenty-five minutes into its Nether (reached at 60).* Magma cubes on a ledge at y 86: Jev chose to fight, and the fight, held only for its estimate's two seconds, was asked again eight times in eighteen seconds; each answer took about two seconds, and nothing swung while it was out: no swing is in the record, and the cubes' hits took it from seventeen to one. At 0.8 health, the fight set aside, it chose to keep working. While Jev is asked the stance, what is in reach is now struck (the swing's own reflex, as the creeper is backed from meanwhile).
351. *mid-243-j, "tried to swim in lava to escape Enderman" at minute 80, thirty-three minutes into its Nether (reached at 47).* Laying a span at y 59 across the lava sea, the bot was hit by an enderman (four a hit through the armour), Jev chose to fight, and the second hit's knockback threw it off the span, thirty blocks into the lava. The span's walls (note 290) were not raised: they went up only when no span was being laid, which kept the wall off the next cell but left every side open while laying. They now go up while a span is being laid too, on every open side but the one the span goes on.
352. *mid-218-g, "shot by Skeleton" at minute 20.* At night a skeleton shot the bot from twenty to seven over half a minute; the retreat got it thirty blocks clear, and then, hungry, Jev chose to walk back to sheep sixty blocks off, on the far side of the skeleton. The option said the walk, the dark and the health, but not the skeleton on the way; two more arrows on the way, and a third in the water it fell into. A herd seen earlier now says the hostiles within ten blocks of the straight way there, in view or not, and how far along it each is.
353. *mid-230-j, "doomed to fall by Ghast" at minute 50, thirty-four minutes into its Nether (fortress reached at 17).* Laying a span of thirty-one toward the portal back at y 68, a drop of sixteen beside it, the bot was shot at by a ghast in sight thirty-nine blocks off; the span went on (the layer stops under fire only for a shooter within twenty-four), Jev chose to dig down, then to retreat, and the second fireball threw it off, sixteen blocks, thirteen of fall from 11.8. A span now counts as under fire for each shooter within its own reach (the ghast's forty), as the threat check and the span's walls already did (notes 298, 332).
354. *mid-241-f, three hours and no Nether (not fixed).* No death and no loop: the time went a little everywhere. Waiting in shelters 25 minutes, searching for food 21 (animals scarce; the searches walked 10,000 blocks in all), climbing to the surface 36 (ascend 19, return 17), mining and smelting 16, lava for the cast 11. The cast frame (at (180, 36, -386), underground) had one failure; three sites were left, one after the walk to its lava came no nearer (note 322). What would have saved the most here is fewer trips up and down between the mine and the surface for food and nights; no one rule stood out to change.
355. *mid-241-g, "slain by Zombie" two minutes in, at y -14.* A skeleton ahead and a zombie behind, out of the list in view: the rule's charge at the shooter (close_on_shooter, not Jev's) reported, broke off at once for the zombie within three blocks, and returned as if it had acted, ten times a second, the swing never reached; the zombie and an arrow took it from four to none. The charge now does not start with a biter within three blocks, in view or not, and one that neither steps nor swings is no answer (the turn goes on, and a chosen one fails as a stance).
356. *mid-211-k, "blown up by Creeper" at minute 100, at y 38 (not fixed).* Four creepers at 3, 4, 9 and 10 blocks, full health in full iron; Jev chose the creeper dance twice in a second (asked again for the second coming within six), told that one blast two blocks off (about 21 through the armour) ends it and that the dance takes one at a time while the others come on. A blast took it from twenty to none a second later. The facts were on the option; retreat, a pocket and a bunker were on offer too. A judgment, not a missing fact or a broken rule.
357. *mid-218-h, "slain by Zombie" at minute 21, sitting in a boat again.* Restarted in its boat (note 338's case), and note 338's fix did not catch it: on joining, the server's seat packet came before the boat itself, mineflayer set bot.vehicle to nothing, and both the join check and the loop's check saw no vehicle. The bot sat thirteen minutes at y 62.11 with no survival check until a zombie killed it where it sat. The seat is now kept as the server says it (the vehicle's id, known or not), and a seat so kept is left with the dismount input whether or not the boat is known yet.
358. *mid-230-k, "slain by Hoglin" at minute 21, three minutes into its Nether (not explained).* Twenty-five seconds after the restart for note 357, at y 84, a hoglin (and a skeleton twenty off) took it from twenty to none in eighteen seconds: pillar, fight, fight, pillar, retreat and dig down were chosen in turn, one swing landed, a step off the edge came between, and the hoglin's tosses kept it in the air. The record does not say why the fights chosen did not swing more; the swing while the answer is out (note 350) was in effect. Watched for.
359. *mid-244-k, three hours and no Nether: forty-six minutes of climbing by hand.* The night mine (343 of its questions answered in the run) wore six pickaxes, 621 uses, to none by 06:39; with one stick, no wood and 128 cobblestone, no other could be made, and the climb from y -22 to the surface went by hand, a stair every forty-five seconds, from 06:41 to 07:27. The night mine's question was told the pickaxes' uses but not what came after them. It is now told how many more stone pickaxes the sticks, wood and stone carried make, and, below open sky, the climb out by hand in minutes, so the dig can be weighed against the way home.
