472. *mid-211-s-nether-3, the snapshot a fourth time: rail_and_fight offered (0.19), fight chosen (0.53), dead in twenty seconds.* The fight was priced at 2.2 damage, about one hit, from a big magma cube; a big one killed is two to four mediums where it stood, and each of those two to four smalls, every one swung at and hitting back. The estimate now fights them: three mediums when the big one dies, three smalls when each medium does (bornOf: none there before the one it came from is dead), under the same count at arm's length as any crowd. From that ledge (two open cells) about 15 damage over nine seconds, not 2.2 over two; a slime's mediums likewise. The snapshot runs again as the test.
473. *mid-244-v, three hours and no Nether: a time sink, no death.* About 105 of its 184 minutes carried lava one bucket at a time from 122 blocks down to a frame on the surface. Jev had chosen to cast the frame beside the lava; three walks there that got no nearer (survival pulling it back up) dropped that choice unasked, and the frame went up on the surface. With the inventory full, nine buckets filled kept one lava bucket: eight lost, twenty-four iron. Standing at the lava, the cast said "about 5 seconds there and back a trip" (real trips: five to seventy-seven minutes), and casting at the lava was offered only with the lava sixteen from the bot, not from the frame. Now: failed walks to the chosen lava ask the way again, saying the walks and what ended them; trips are measured from the frame, the climb included, beside the working time each trip has taken so far; cast_at_lava is offered whenever the frame is far from the lava; a fill makes room first or stops and says so, and the facts give the free slots; the obsidian frame's option gives its time and single carry beside the cast's trips.
474. *mid-236-h, "fell from a high place" at minute 7.* Working free of a flooded pocket at y 37, Jev chose step_south, "swim one block south: ends on dry ground", and fell fifty-eight blocks from four blocks further south. The step held forward and jump until the bot was on the ground in the cell; a swimmer's jump out onto land is not on the ground, so forward ran on past the dry cell and over the edge of a ravine the option never named. A step now stops in the cell, the keys up, and waits to land; and a step says what lies one block past it, where a step that runs on ends up ("one block past it, a drop of 24+ blocks").
475. *mid-205-q, "slain by Zombie" at minute 85.* Worn to 2.8 by arrows under ground, it was offered its golden apple at 0.04 beside take_cover's "0.8 damage from 2.8 health": the apple was the one stance with no damage or health said. Chosen at last at 0.9 health, the eat was cut short under a second in, the apple still in the pack, and the failed-stance rule took it off the list for twenty seconds; seal, then fight by "none of these", and the zombies. The apple is priced as the eat is (its damage while eating, from what health); an eat cut short with the food still carried stays on offer, said ("tried 1 second ago here and cut short before it was eaten; still carried"); and the stance question's state says what failed here just now (failedHereJustNow), where each question after a failure began with nothing said of it.
476. *mid-227-r-nether-1-nether-1, "doomed to fall" at minute 25 of a Nether stage trial.* Two causes. The loop: the ladder in the Nether wants blaze rods, the planner wants the full iron kit before any fight, iron ore is only in the Overworld, and "No iron ore in the nether" came 1,130 times in twenty-five minutes; the rung's set-aside was never read by the ladder (a worktree is making the kit a costed choice for Jev). The fall: a magma cube stood three blocks up on a ledge, out of every ray, for twelve seconds at the lip of a forty-six-block drop into lava; an unseen biter counted only as a creeper or a warden, so nothing answered it until its hit threw the bot over. A biter within a hit and a jump (3.5 blocks) is now a threat seen or not where the drop within three is into lava or half the health: a knock there is a fall.
477. *mid-220-g, "shot by Skeleton" at minute 22.* Eighteen minutes mining gravel for one flint beside a dungeon nineteen blocks off and in a mineshaft; about sixty pockets sealed, healed in, left, and the same spot again, until three skeletons, a cave spider's poison and a zombie. The spawner fact reached only the stance, and only within sixteen of a spawner block; the choices that decide whether to stay never saw the place, and a passage out was dug only from a creeper or a warden. Now mobSourceAbout says the place wherever staying is chosen (pocket_next's stay and leave, the shelter's wait for dawn, night_mine_target, and each resource_source): a spawner within sixteen, a remembered dungeon or mineshaft within twenty-four, the hurts and encounters within sixteen in the last fifteen minutes, and that a spawner's mobs do not leave at daylight. tunnel_out is offered from any watcher that keeps the pocket, from a spawner to beyond sixteen of it; night_mine_target gains branch_away.
478. *mid-235-n, three hours and no Nether: 140 minutes in one pocket.* Sealed under an open shaft at dawn, Jev chose to stay; then the fight_in_pocket reflex held the turn for two hours and twenty minutes without a swing landing. It fired on canStrike, which counted any mob within two blocks of the eye "whatever the ray says", and the mobs on the lid measured 1.38; the swing's own target test (strikeTarget) wanted a mob in reach, and there was none. The stall rule excused it by its name as an emergency, and pocket_next was never asked again. Now: canStrike's shortcut refuses a mob behind a full block (a stair's edge still passes), the reflex fires only on the swing's own test, an emergency is excused only while it produces results (fight_in_pocket: a swing landed within eight seconds, permittedWait's RESULTS), and pocket_next says the mobs on the lid stand on it, cannot be reached and cannot reach the bot through it.
479. *The single arbiter, in shadow (the user, 2026-09-27: "Yeah merge the arbiter").* src/arbiter.js: each layer (survival, vitals, the hunt, the work) states its claim on the turn with the facts it rests on; reflexes (lava, fire, air, a head in a block, a creeper within its fuse's reach, a biter at arm's length at low health) are taken in a fixed order, a single claim runs, and several go to Jev as turn_priority; the ruling holds until a reflex, a newcomer, a fall in health, a change in food or a minute. In shadow for now: runGoal computes what it would pick beside the layer the old code gave the turn, logs "[arbiter] would X, gave Y" when they differ, and records snapshot.arbiter; nothing it picks runs. A failed or resting shelter plan is a live claim, not a fall-through (mid-231-o's 0.9 health, notes 465, 466). The differences in live trials say where the old precedence and the arbiter part.
480. *The combat kit a choice, not a wall (note 476's loop).* The planner no longer puts the full iron kit before every hunt: the hunt lists the pieces carried and missing, and Jev is asked combat_kit — fight with what is carried (its weapon, armour, shield and a fight estimate against the hunted mob), make here what can be made in this dimension, or go back for the rest (the iron, the pieces and the walk to the portal). A rung whose sources lie all in another dimension now says so: nextGameStage reads the set-aside and asks rung_elsewhere (go there for them, with what comes back and the portal trip, or on with this dimension's next stage). And the stall names the blocker: the WrongDimension record keeps the step, its block and where it is found, and the stall key is step:iron_ore, not step:none.
481. *mid-211-s-nether-4 and mid-202-o, both "flipping find_fortress" in a basalt delta.* With none carried, Jev chose the same leg across the lava sea about thirty times: surveyLeg priced ninety-four cells of floorless air as a walk of thirty-four seconds, the failure note sat on the next heading after the sweep turned (so the failed leg looked untried), and restocking was a hidden rule at four failures that counted and mined netherrack only, in a delta of five thousand basalt and fourteen netherrack. Now a leg says the blocks it needs laid against those carried and where they run out, each bridged cell timed as a crouched step and a block; each heading keeps its own failures; and restock_blocks (the most plentiful bridging material within sixteen, by kind, basalt and blackstone counted) and return_for_blocks (the portal back) are fortress_leg options, where the hidden rule was.
482. *mid-215-h, "flipping return_to_frame <-> enter_nether" at minute 37.* A lava bucket in hand and its cast frame 137 blocks off: the walk failed, the staircase toward the frame was set aside, the step returned false, and the next pass did it all again, the stall offering nothing about the frame. Note 473's rule for the lava now holds for the frame: after the walk (in 32-block stretches where the frame is far) and the staircase fail, the portal method is asked again with the frame said on every option ("the frame at (212, 69, -393) is 137 blocks off and cannot be got back to: ..."), cast_here offered beside the others (the old frame's blocks left, a diamond pickaxe to take them), and a way kept by Jev that still cannot be got back to says so as a fact, not a silent retry.
483. *mid-237-f, "doomed to fall by Skeleton" at minute 66.* At 11.7 health it left its pocket at night and chose how to climb out with a skeleton eleven blocks off in sight; neither the staircase nor straight_up said so, and an arrow knocked it off the climb, thirteen blocks down. Every way up now says the shooters in sight and that an arrow's knockback where the side is open is a fall, the hands on the pickaxe and blocks rather than the shield (climbOptions, state.shootersInSight).
484. *mid-244-y, "drowned" at minute 67.* Its unstuck digs opened a sealed lake (water y 49 to 57 under a stone lid) into its dry pocket, the options saying only "it opens onto water"; digToShore swam it up into the lake with no count of breath; and the air search, boxed eight blocks about the bot, could not see the flooded column to air nine blocks west, and floating it priced every dig past its four-second cap. It sank, and swam straight up at a twelve-block lid with an empty hand. Air is physical safety, and now a hard rule: a route with the head under water is measured against the breath (0.75 seconds a point, less two) and refused if it does not fit (breathShort, in digToShore); the air search is bounded by the breath, not a box, and takes the shortest route that fits; straight up measures its digs first, with the best tool; and a dig that lets water in says how far that water's air is, refused only where it floods a dry head beyond the breath. Short of any route that fits, the search goes on with the seconds drowning still leaves.
485. *The arbiter live, behind JEV_ARBITER=live (shadow stays the default).* Three steps. The watch (arbiter.js watch): four looks a second beside the stall watch, the reflexes and a hostile newcomer while the work holds the turn; one that outranks the holder stops it (turn.js stopForTurn, the hurt watchdog's stop, which now calls it too) and sets bot._preempt, thrown as NeedsSafety from the task's stall check, which nested steps do not swap out as they do interruptCheck; it holds with no clock until the arbiter next rules. The turn (work.js liveTurn, arbiter.js take): runGoal and runIdle give the turn to the ruling instead of hunt, then survival, then the meal, then the work; each layer runs the same step as before. A survival step that does nothing keeps the turn rather than handing it to the work unasked (mid-242-w at 2.9 health, crafting and mining out of its pocket and shot, logged "[arbiter] would survival secure_shelter, gave work"); a meal behind a renewing shelter plan is offered beside it. Jev's question (turn_priority) carries the health, food, mobs about, what rests and why, how long the holder has had the turn and how long it has done nothing with it; the ruling holds, and is asked again after ten seconds of its winner doing nothing. Survival's claim leaves out the edge, the night mine and the evening at home, so with no survival claim its step still runs ahead of the meal and the work, as before, and says so when it acts: those lines are where the claim has more to learn. Ports listed one a line in .bot-state/arbiter-live run it live too, since a bot restarted by its supervisor or the quiet restart does not carry the env: half the trials live beside half in shadow is the comparison.
486. *mid-230-s, "flipping tunnel <-> retreat_from_tunnel" at minute 12.* Tunneling down for diamonds from the surface, the staircase stepped between two cells, the retreat taking it back from one with no step on, nine times in two minutes: a step taken reset the count, and the rest was keyed on a target that moved every round. Jev's stall question said only "reach nether, strike 1", and its "differently" walk went two blocks, its failure swallowed. Progress is now ground gained: a landing with no step no nearer the target than the last one backed off from stalls the staircase at once, naming the landing and every reason it was blocked, and rests it by the landing as well as by the target; the stall question carries those reasons (stalled.failure), and a way off that went nowhere says how far it got (stalled.lastWayOff).
487. *mid-237-g, "3× No lava pool is known here, and the deep lava on all sixteen headings near and far rests" at minute 6 (a slip in note 486).* Note 486 rested a stalled staircase by its landing and whether it went up or down; the next heading tried from that landing was refused at once and rested in turn, and all sixteen of the deep lava's headings rested within seconds. A stall says nothing of the ways it did not try: the landing's rest is now by its heading too (one of eight), so the other headings from the same place are still tried.
488. *mid-214-f, "flipping detour <-> tunnel", on after the new code.* The staircase to the lava Jev had chosen was set aside ten minutes after a dig refused beside a drop; every pass threw at once, and every re-ask of the portal way said of it "Kept, the walk goes on by staircase", 63 times across the restart. A way that rests is a fact wherever it is offered: the held lava's option now says its staircase rests, why and until when; kept while it rests, it is WaysResting, answered once, not a retry every half second; what ended the walks names the stall; and every path that meets a resting staircase sets goal.staircaseStalled, so the stall question gives Jev the reason.
489. *Who answers a creeper is Jev's (the user, 2026-09-27: "Yeah go to Jev on the routing too").* The arbiter took two cases by rule that are judgments: a creeper within its fuse's reach and a mob at arm's length at low health, each giving survival the turn at once. They are alerts now (arbiter.js ALERTS): the watch still stops what is running for them, since a creeper goes off a second and a half after it lights, and the claim is pressing, with its facts (the creeper's distance, whether seen, where it lights, how fast it walks and its fuse; the mob, its distance and the health), beside the other claims in turn_priority. Alone it runs, as any single claim does. Only the body's own physics (lava, fire, the head in a block, air) is taken by rule.
490. *mid-218-n, "slain by Drowned" at minute 33: the first death under the live arbiter.* At seven health, head under water, a drowned coming from seven blocks, Jev was asked turn_priority five times in a minute and gave the turn to the work each time but once: the options were the claims' code names and raw facts ("escape_threat", "surface", "recover_before_nether", which reads as healing). The drowned stopped the work eleven times in four seconds, "Threat nearby", and the ruling held handed it back each time. Now each claim is said in words (claimSays: "Answer the drowned 5.6 blocks off: the stance is asked next ... The work waits. Health 7."; "Swim up for air: ..."; "Go on with the work: recover before nether ... Its last try ended: ..."); a ruling for a winner stopped by a mob since it was made ends, and Jev is asked again; and the work's own step changing (mine, craft, mine) is no longer a new question, as it was eight times in a minute. The recorded question, reworded, is a replay case: answered for survival or vitals, never the work.
491. *mid-227-r-nether-3, "fireballed by Blaze" at minute 35 (live arbiter).* Three blazes thirty-two to thirty-five blocks off set the bot alight and struck it for fourteen seconds on a span; Jev gave survival the turn each time it was asked (0.67 to 0.81), but survival answered only shot by shot (the shield, the span's rail) and asked no stance: a shooter counted as a threat within thirty-two when hurt, and a blaze the hunt had claimed not at all. A shooter whose kind has hit the bot in the last five seconds is now a threat however far off, hunted or not: its fire reaching the bot is the reach. The stance question then offers the cover, the retreat and the rest against it.
492. *mid-236-j, "blown up by Creeper" at minute 29 (live arbiter).* Hunting food with a creeper coming from eight blocks, the watch stopped survival's own shelter walk for the creeper alert, the arbiter asked who acts, survival had the turn again and its shelter walk went on, and the watch stopped it again: thirteen seconds round that ring, nothing answering the creeper, until it went off. An alert now stops only another layer's work, never the layer it would give the turn to (survival's own step answers its creeper with a stance); the body's physics still stops anything. And a layer changing its own action (survival between its shelter and the creeper's answer) is no longer a new turn_priority question; an alert coming or going is.
493. *mid-241-w, "shot by Skeleton" at minute 4 (live arbiter).* An arrow knocked the bot to the lip of an eighteen-block drop; Jev gave survival the turn and chose take_cover, whose option said the drop honestly. But the wall went in the cell under the bot's own feet (it had crossed a tenth of a block), and placing it backed the body out of that cell and off the lip: 17.2 to 3.2, then the next arrow. The ledge rule in place() held only for the callers that asked for it. It is physics now: a placement that would back the body out to where no floor lies under any part of it is refused, with the ledge said, whoever places.
494. *mid-226-f, mid-214-f: a staircase to lava meets the roof of a cave.* The dig of the next stair would open a two- or three-block pit beside the feet and was refused, the staircase rested ten minutes, and the portal way bounced on it (notes 488, 490's "4× set aside (refusing to open a drop)"); mid-226-f carried 128 cobblestone the whole time. A player floors the stair: floorStair digs the stair cell and places a carried block in the gap under it, then steps on. With none carried or no face to place against, the rest says the cave (the fall to its floor, its y, the target's), and the portal way offers into_cave (drop to the cave floor where the fall costs under half the health), other_lava (a known lava whose way is not resting) and cast_here beside the rest. A way Jev keeps while it rests is held: until_rest_ends lets the minutes go to other work, one pick at a time, instead of the stall question every few seconds. (Also note 493's rule: a bot standing in the cell itself is stepOff's, and a flying one hovers.)
495. *mid-241-v, "10× invalid operation" crafting planks at minute 108.* Not the planks: four sticks left on the cursor with all thirty-six slots full (a furnace and seven logs had been picked back up after Jev dropped its flint and steel to make room for them). settleCraftInventory called the library's put-away with no free slot, which clicked slot undefined, and every craft after it began with that click. Now a craft's whole output must fit (free slots, room in matching stacks, a slot the craft empties) or Jev makes room first; the cursor's leftovers get the same, or a named error ("No free slot for the 4 stick on the cursor; the inventory is full"), never the library's assert. And the room question says what each stack is for: the flint and steel lights the Nether portal (the only lighter carried), and a step's tools count as needed.
496. *mid-202-o-nether-3, -4 and mid-218-m-nether-1: three Nether stage trials went home unasked, and one could not get back.* A hidden rule in the mob hunt's preparation (prepareMobHunt: hunger under eighteen, no food carried, not fit to fight) sent each back through the portal without a question, at food 17 each time; one came out into the night, one died to a night crowd, one wandered 374 blocks from its portal and threw "No way back to the overworld portal" on every pass across water its boat failed on. Leaving the Nether is Jev's now: leave_nether (go back, with the trip and whether it comes out by night; or keep on) for the hunt's food rule, and for the rods step waiting (go back, search on, wait here), in place of nextGameStage's silent return_overworld. A portal that cannot be reached is portal_way (a portal here, around left or right, the boat again, other work until the rest ends), asked once per rest from each place. And a portal trip's text says the hour it comes out at.
497. *mid-244-z, "doomed to fall by Zombie" at minute 2.* A zombie with a spear, told as "about 2.5 seconds and 7.2 damage": it jabbed from 2.5 to 3 blocks about once a second (1.9 to 2.5 through iron), each jab knocking the bot a block back, the bot's charges stalling on the cave floor; 20 to 2.7 in twelve seconds and a jab over a fifteen-block drop. Measured from about fifty spear hits across six trials (minecraft-data 26.1 has the spear items and damage type, not their reach or damage): a jab about 5 before armor, reach as far as a sword's but past an arm, about a block of knockback. A spear holder is now fought as it fights: twice the swinging time, as a shooter's back-off is, jabbing the whole fight at full rate and over the mob in front; the fight says its reach, jab and knock, and how many jabs away the drop is. mid-244-z's estimate is now 5 seconds and 12.5 damage.
498. *mid-242-x, mid-227-r-nether-2, mid-207-n: "No measurable progress on strategy_side nether_first".* Jev chose the Nether first, setting the bow, the golden boots, the leggings and the diamond sword aside; minutes later the ladder handed them back, one at a time. Its escape hatch was all or nothing: any resting rung with another reason (here likely the kit's "fight with what is carried") and it ignored every set-aside, Jev's included; offered the bow again, nether_first set it aside again, left a strategy_side step that did nothing, and was asked five times a second until the stall rule failed it. The ladder now brings back only rungs set aside for other reasons; what Jev chose to go without keeps waiting. nether_first moves the ladder on at once (replan, no step, no rest) and is not offered while every rung it would set aside already rests. At the Nether stage, the steps left waiting are offered as take_up_<phase>, with how long each has left said.
499. *Cover from shooters, as a player takes it.* Against skeletons underground Jev kept taking the pillar, whose own text says shooters still hit; of the day's "none of these" encounter picks with a shooter (98 by evening; 61 from one stuck spot in mid-205-t at 2.58 health, one after another), the fallback took the pillar 69 times, the lowest damage figure among ways that all stood in the arrows' line. The moves out of the line were missing. Now: out_of_sight walks to the nearest cell, within eight blocks, that no shooter in sight can see (the threats' own raycast, from each shooter's eye to the head, middle and feet of a player standing there), with the walk under fire and the biters that can reach it said; nook digs two in and one aside into natural rock where its end is out of every line, the game's dig times for the tool carried said; and bunker is offered wherever a wall can be dug, its seconds said, without the hidden three-second gate (kept only in the rule used when Jev cannot be reached). Each holds once reached. Not yet seen in a trial; the out_of_sight search is a few hundred rays, to watch for stalls.
500. *mid-218-m-nether-2, mid-202-o-nether-2: a resting way offered fresh, the fifth time, and a survival walk's failure persisted as the work.* seek_fortress_height was offered while its staircase rested; each pick threw at once and was filed under the level leg's name, whose text grew to "564 tries from there" while the option chosen said nothing of it. One helper now says a staircase's rest wherever its way is offered (tunneling.js restingSays: the target's area and the landing's heading): the fortress legs, the seek, the approach's tunnel, the climb out, the ruin's way. A seek's failures are its own (seek_<heading>). And a NoRoute from survival's escape walk, rethrown into the work at 18 health and persisted twelve times in twenty-eight seconds naming no destination while health fell to 4.8, is survival's own now: a failed stance said in the next stance question, never the work's persist; NoRoute names its destination, and persist says what it retries.
501. *mid-215-j, "impaled by Drowned" at minute 8.* In ten-deep water with a trident drowned below, Jev chose get_out_of_water; it failed in two seconds without a stroke ("No reachable dry shore found": the bank thirty-five blocks off was in view but its route not found in time over open water) and rested three minutes, and the tridents landed every two seconds, 4.5 through iron. The fight figures were honest; the swim's were not: no distance, no seconds, no damage, and "no bank within 32" where the swim looked to 64. Now the option says the nearest bank out to 64, the seconds to swim it (about 2 blocks a second, measured), whether toward the shooter or away, and its cost priced as every stance is. And where the route search finds nothing but the straight line is open water, the bot swims for it in two-second strokes and climbs out (openSwim: not into a wall); four blocks nearer counts as progress. (A trident barely slows in water, 0.99 inertia in the 26.1.2 jar: diving is no cover.)
503. *mid-243-p, "blown up by Creeper" at minute 94 (live arbiter).* At dusk Jev chose the bed with a creeper eleven blocks off; the bed's option spoke only of monsters within eight of it (sleep refused while any are), and the creeper came on while the bed went down. The shelter's option in the same question said the race; the bed and the nook say it now too (creeperSays: how far, and how soon it could go off beside the bot).
502. *mid-218-m-nether-3, "burned to death" at minute 32, at its portal on the Nether side.* Soul fire burns naturally in a soul sand valley, and one cell of it lay just in front of the portal's north face. Leaving the portal, enterPortal's held-key step back (crouched, which does not stop a drop into a cell with no collision box) took the bot off the frame into it at 7.7 health; eight seconds of burning after the last touch, a health a second, and nothing to douse with in the Nether, so the touch was the death. The pathfinder avoids fire; a held-key move did not look. Physics now: motion never takes the body into fire, soul fire or lava (the cells half a block and most of a block ahead each tick, and the drop below where nothing holds it up; a floor under part of the body still counts, so a span over the lava sea stands); leaving a portal tries the back face, then the front, and says it when both are fire; a lighting that leaves fire and no portal says so. And a held "go back" ended once the bot left the Nether: kept ten minutes, it had sent the bot straight back unasked on arriving again.
504. *mid-218-q, "slain by Drowned" at minute 3 (live arbiter).* Head under water with a drowned coming from nine blocks, Jev gave the turn to vitals, the swim up (0.97). The drowned stopped that swim at every tick ("Threat nearby"), the held ruling handed it back each time, the bot sank from y 62 to 58 with the keys let go at each stop, and survival never answered the drowned until it was on the bot. Note 490's "a ruling ends when a mob stops its winner" read only the work's lastError; now any winner whose run is stopped by NeedsSafety ends its ruling, and the next pass asks again with the threat among the claims.
505. *mid-214-g, world 214's lava again (after mid-214-f): portal_method every five to eight seconds, cast_at_lava kept each time.* No rest stood for most of it: each staircase pass toward the lava 11 below was pulled back up by survival's return to surface, note 473's walk counter reached three passes without gain and asked again, and each keep deleted the counter, so three passes later it asked again. "Kept, the walk goes on by staircase" held nothing. Now a keep holds (the staircase's own rounds decide, and its set-aside brings the next question); a rest the question has not seen is asked about at once, never walked into; nearRest reads the rest by the landing as well as by the area (tunneling.js restingWay); and the option leads with the fact that matters: "Not reachable now: … taken up again in N minutes", its trip time the real one given that, or "Not reached so far" after failed walks. (Survival pulling the stairs back up each pass is still to look at.)
506. *mid-236-k, "shot by Skeleton" at minute 6 (live arbiter).* Filling a bucket with three skeletons closing from thirteen blocks, the work threw "Threat nearby" every few tenths of a second for ten seconds, and each pass the held ruling gave the work the turn again; Jev was asked only once health had fallen six. Note 504 ends a ruling when its winner's run is stopped, but the work runs after the arbiter hands it the turn, outside that catch. The loop's own NeedsSafety catch now marks a ruling for the work as stopped, and the next pass asks again with the skeletons among the claims.
508. *mid-227-r-nether-4, "4× No measurable progress on recover_before_combat" at minute 46: health 20, food 19, needed 14 and 14.* The hunt's fitness still counted the iron kit as a condition of being fit to fight, a slip in note 480, which made the kit Jev's choice (combat_kit): not fit, the hunt went to "recover before combat", and no rest brings iron. fitness and fitToFight are health, food and fire now; the kit stays among the facts said.
510. *mid-239-g, "blown up by Creeper" at minute 65.* A skeleton at 1.7 blocks, the fight and the charge having failed a moment before: the failed-stance rule took both off for twenty seconds, and its exception (a fight stays on offer once a mob is at reach) counted only biters. Offered cover, a pocket and digging down, Jev said none of these twice (0.55); the walk out of sight took arrows and a creeper came. A shooter at arm's length is struck as a biter is: the fight stays on offer with any mob at reach.
507. *mid-235-p, "flipping find_fortress <-> cross_toward" at minute 59: the day's first fortress, left twice.* The approach worked: cross_level brought it among the bricks at minute 52 ("I'm in the fortress"). Then the patrol counted six passes as six ticks without walking, and 0.4 seconds after arriving fortress_leg offered only legs; Jev took leg_north. The same fortress's remembered bricks pulled it back, still filtered out as "leaving" for four minutes, so the next ask again offered only legs and seek said "from y 49 none is seen through the rock" with bricks five blocks off. A player stays in a fortress and waits by the spawners. Now fortress_leg offers stay_in_fortress beside the legs once a pass ends (the bricks in view, the passes, the minutes there, the blazes seen), and back_to_fortress where its bricks are in view but left behind or set aside (saying when and why); fortressInView is among the facts, and the seek no longer says nothing is seen when a fortress is. (Leaving is still measured from where the bot stood, not the fortress's extent.)
512. *mid-235-p-fortress-2, "burned to a crisp while fighting Blaze" at minute 9, the fortress stage.* Blazes at fifteen to twenty-five blocks: fireballs, fire where they landed, and the bot alight, 14.7 to none in about twenty seconds, most of it burning. The estimate priced a blaze at its fireball alone (5 before armour, every two seconds), where each that lands sets a player alight for five seconds, about a health a second that armour does not stop, renewed by the next, with no water in the Nether to put it out. A shooter that sets alight now burns as it hits in the estimate (burns: 1 a second while its fire lands, halved behind a shield as its shots are), and its note says so; one blaze in the open through iron now reads about 18 over its fight, not half that.
509. *mid-235-p-fortress-1, "doomed to fall by Blaze" at minute 4 (live arbiter).* At 5.5 health beside a fortress, a blaze 15 to 17 blocks off, turn_priority was asked six times in twenty seconds, split between the work and survival. At the last, survival claimed nothing: the blaze was 16.5 blocks off, past the sixteen counted for a blaze (combat-estimate RANGE), and no hit in the last seconds. Only the hunt, said as "hunt: hunt.", and the work were offered; Jev answered none_good at 0.80, and the next fireball, from that blaze at 16.5, threw the bot off the edge. A blaze fires at what it targets in sight within its follow range, forty-eight blocks: RANGE says so now, so a blaze in sight within it is survival's claim (danger.js immediateThreat), said with its reach, how long since it hit and whether health comes back. The hunt, night hunt, item recovery, the way home and powder snow are said in words. Three of the six questions were survival's claim going and coming, the one-claim pass between dropping the ruling; a claim's absence now counts after two passes, the ruling's winner absent one pass is a breath with the ruling kept.
513. *mid-243-q, "tried to swim in lava" at minute 63, sixteen into its Nether.* Mining on a span at y 78, it was struck twice by a ghast's fireballs, "on fire", off the span, and down fifty blocks into the lava sea; the ghast was in no frame: the threats' look reached forty-eight blocks and a ghast counted to forty. A ghast fires from as far as sixty-four; its reach is that now (RANGE), and the immediate-threat look, the span's under-fire look and the recorder's mobs reach it too.
511. *mid-229-q, three hours and no Nether: a hundred minutes climbing.* The clock's "return to surface 71" and "ascend to surface 29" were the same climbs under two labels (surface.js writes return_to_surface for every caller), and none was survival's or Jev's: when the work wanted something only the surface has (a log for a table, a sheep, a portal site) underground, it climbed at once, climb_out asking only how. Seven climbs; one ran ninety-seven minutes from y -12, for a spare iron pickaxe the ladder wanted because the carried one had 49 uses under a hidden "64", its pickaxes worn out on the way and seventy minutes dug level by hand. Now such a climb is surface_trip, Jev's: climb (the height, the quicker way out and its time, the uses it wears, the way back down) or stay_below (set the step aside half an hour, naming what the ladder goes on with); a chosen climb holds to the top. A spare pickaxe rung may wait while a worn one of its tier still works. (Survival's own climbs for a table or shelter blocks still climb unasked; and why the staircase ran level for seventy minutes is open.)
514. *mid-235-p-nether-2 and mid-235-p-fortress-3, both "burned to death" on the fortress walk.* Both were the pathfinder's own routes, not any guarded move of ours. In the first it had laid a one-wide span into the lava sea and towered on it; mid-jump off the tower the edge-sneak that holds a player at a rim held nothing (it holds only on the ground), and the bot fell five blocks onto the span's edge and slid into the lava. In the second it jumped out of a trench onto a cell with lava beside it at the feet, priced +4, not refused, and the jump carried its side into the flow. Lava, then fifteen seconds of burning with no water to be had in the Nether: 14.6 to none standing still. The movements now refuse a block laid level beside the floor where a miss falls into lava or two or more onto lava's edge (the pathfinder lays no span into the lava sea; that crossing stays Jev's, cross_toward, its cost said), and a jump up across onto a cell with lava beside it; towers straight up stand.
516. *The Nether pathfinder kept off lava (a second opinion's advice, and mid-243-q-nether-1).* Note 514 refused two kinds of move one at a time; the next lava death was a ghast's knock off a ledge thirteen above the lava sea, the pathfinder having walked the bot to its edge. In the Nether the pathfinder now refuses any cell with lava in the eight around it (feet, head or floor), and any cell beside an edge whose fall lands in lava at any depth or costs half the health or more; parkour is off there. A fortress bridge three to five wide is walked down its middle; a one-wide bridge, a broken deck or a railless edge is not walked, and crossing it is Jev's (cross_toward, cross_level, its cost said). A walk refused this way says so ("the way passes beside lava"). The portal step opts out within two blocks of its frame. What it may block: a target beside lava (none but the portal opts out today), a crossing stopped partway, and more ledges at low health, where half the health is a shorter fall.
515. *The low-health arc (mid-231-o, mid-207-l, mid-211-x, mid-231-q; a second opinion's advice).* Four deaths followed one arc: health down, no food carried, no healing under eighteen hunger, the work or the hunt going on, often at night, and a later encounter. Each option was honest alone; what Jev never had was the whole budget in front of it at once. Now every gameplay question's state carries healingSays (src/healing.js): health and hunger, whether health comes back and what each healed point costs, the food carried by kind with its points and effects and what eating it all would bring hunger to, the nearest known food (an animal in view, a sighting, a village, the home's pen or chest), the minutes to daylight, and that standing still spends no hunger. Rotten flesh is food with its effect said (4 points, an 80% chance of Hunger for thirty seconds, about three quarters of a hunger point): eaten without the hidden "food < 18 and health < 20" gate, offered in the eat stance, and said beside the safe food (lastResortSupply). wait_for_day_sealed is offered anywhere in the Overworld when hurt with no healing: seal in and wait for dawn, its minutes and its near-zero hunger said. And the crossing kit and the fortress approach say a blaze's fireball and a wither skeleton's blade through what is worn against the health there is.
519. *mid-208-j, "flipping detour <-> persist" at minute 97.* Its staircase to the portal's lava rested again and again on "refusing to open a drop beside the feet: a cave under the stair, a fall of 4 to its floor", with no face to set a floor against. A fall of four costs a health. The pit rule refused any stair cell with two open cells under it, whatever lay below: a depth, not a danger. It is judged by what the fall does now: refused where lava lies below, the column is unloaded, or the landing would cost half the health or more; a drop onto a cave floor that costs little is dug and taken, and note 494's floor-laying is left for the falls that matter (deeper ones the staircase already filters as no floor).
518. *mid-237-j, "fell from a high place" at minute 59: a waterfall's lip and a lava pool below it, three times.* The walk home for food swam up a waterfall until the air ran low; the swim-up for air (vitals airRoute) chose the spill at the fall's top as the cell to breathe in, head in air and feet in water, and the current carried the floating bot off the lip, thirteen blocks down into a lava pool. Out of the lava, out_of_lava held forward and jump into the waterfall's foot and swam it up again; the loop ran three times, the last fall fatal. A breathing cell in moving water with a drop beside it is no target for air now; and out of lava into water is out, the keys let go there. (If every cell near is such a spill, the swim goes straight up, still.)
521. *mid-239-h, "slain by Zombie" at minute 13: a failed stance taken off the list (a second opinion's advice).* A zombie at 0.6 blocks and the fight failed two seconds before: the failed-stance rule took the fight off for twenty seconds (its at-reach exception read a danger list without that zombie in it), and Jev was offered a pocket, the work and a retreat, none of them good (0.51). Taking a failed stance off for twenty seconds was itself a hidden threshold. Every stance that failed here now stays on offer with its failure said ("Tried 2 seconds ago here, and it failed: …"; the fight, with a mob at reach now said so; the cut-short eat as note 475); Jev weighs a failure said. Its tests now check the failure is said, and that eight blocks on the stance is offered fresh.
517. *Blazes fought as a player fights them (the fortress-stage deaths, notes 509, 512, 514; a second opinion's advice; mid-235-p-fortress-4's half hour in a pocket beside a spawner, "none of these" nine times).* Read from the 26.1.2 server jar: a blaze throws three fireballs about every nine seconds, each scattered by up to 2.3 × √distance × 0.5, so about 46 in 100 hit at 4 blocks, 24 at 16, 14 at 48, and a volley lands one more often than not out to 22 blocks; it melees for 6 within 2, gives up a target three seconds out of sight, and does not come to a hole on its own; a fully blocked fireball's fire is put out, its push (about a block, up to three) is not; a spawner makes four within four blocks of it every ten to forty seconds while a player is within sixteen. Now three stands are offered, in the stance question, in the hunt and as an opening in a pocket's wall: dig_in_and_fight (a hole in brick or netherrack, its dig time from the pickaxe carried, fought from inside with the back to a wall), fight_at_spawner (a cell within three of the cage under a ceiling) and back_to_wall (footing with a wall behind, away from drops), each with the blazes' behaviour, the hit chance, the push and a cost with the burn. The hunt takes no fight route ending within a push of lava or a deadly drop (such a blaze is said as not fought in the open). A blaze is claimed as a threat within 22 blocks, or to 48 once its shots land; 48 stays a fact with the hit chance by distance. And the push is said against the drop ("the drop into lava is 1 block off: one that lands puts it over"). Still to do: the estimate prices a blaze at a steady rate at any distance, not by the volleys and spread above.
520. *mid-226-h, shot at arm's length while "sealing".* A skeleton stood in one wall cell of the pocket, and every seal pass started at that cell (nearest the threat first) and failed on "Placement obstructed": about forty failures in thirty-nine seconds, two blocks placed. The rest said after each failure never held, because `report()` did not honour a hold's rest, and seal_shelter was excused by name in `permittedWait`. Only a biter at arm's length went to the stance question, so the skeleton never did, and the claim promised a stance that never came. Now seal_shelter and dig_in are excused only while a block went in within the last eight seconds, and a hold's rest is honoured unless it is placing. A cell a mob stands in is skipped and said; a pass that can't close for that reason ends as seal_failed with the cell named. Any mob within three blocks, shooters too, and a seal pass that closed nothing, go to the stance question. The stance, its seal option and the escape_threat claim all say how much of the pocket stands and which cells a mob is in (`sealingSays`).
523. *mid-235-p-fortress-6, "turning between find fortress and hunt mob" sixty times from minute 55.* The bot stood on its own span at y 49, a block from the fortress's footing, its corridors eight blocks up. "Inside" meant any brick within six blocks, so it counted as in the fortress. No floor lay near its height, so the patrol aimed at any brick and set out for two eight blocks up. Every staircase was refused at once (a deadly drop beside the step, no floor). Two refusals made a "pass", and stay_in_fortress was offered as "1 pass over every stretch in view". The stall's loose ends set the face aside, back_to_fortress took it back, and a tick that only asked Jev left the hunt's own step named, so the step names traded twice a second. Now the bot is in the fortress when it stands at the height of one of its floors (a brick with room to stand on it) within six blocks. That one test serves the patrol, the approach and staying. Short of a floor, the way to the nearest one is asked (fortress_approach). It now includes pillar_up: straight up from a column with no liquid in or beside it, said with the height, the blocks against those carried, the floor across from the top and the fall a push would be. Each stretch a pass sets out for records whether it was reached and why not, and staying says so ("set out for 2 stretches and reached 0; not reached: ..."). Both fortress options say where the floors are against the bot. With a spawner within twenty-four blocks, wait_at_spawner (three minutes by the cage, a wait the stall watch lets be) is offered beside staying. Each leg says how far the fortress's bricks run that way. The search names its own step from the first line, so the hunt's step no longer comes back between the questions. (Leaving and setting a face aside are still measured from a point, not the fortress's extent.)
522. *mid-242-y, "shot by Skeleton" at minute 17, underground at y -3.* Round a corner from a skeleton 2.6 blocks off at 7.6 health, out_of_sight was told "about 1 damage" in fifteen seconds, and "a shooter that walks round to a new line finds the bot open again" in words only; its walk stalled twice in the line, an arrow took 8.2 to 5.1, and it failed. Then at 5.4 the L (nook), priced 14.4 for its digging under fire (said as more than the bot had) and after it "round the turn, none of them reaches it": two arrows while digging, the L's end reached at 1.1, and four seconds of nook_hold, held on that estimate, while the skeleton walked into the mouth and to the turn and shot it from beside it. Read from the 26.1.2 server jar: a skeleton that cannot see its target paths toward it, keeps to that path when it gives the target up (its bow goal runs on while its navigation is not done), and shoots where the path first has a line, the bow drawn in twenty ticks; it walks about 2.75 blocks a second (movement 0.25 twice over against the ground's friction). Now each shooter in sight is walked to the hiding place by its way (bunker.js lineRegained, A* over the cells a mob stands in, the L's cells counted dug) and priced from the second it has a line again, in words and numbers ("the skeleton 4 blocks off has one after about 6 blocks of walking, about 6.4 seconds in"; "none of them reaches it at first; the skeleton after about 6.4 seconds reaches it again"). In the L, a shooter with a line in is said. And a held out_of_sight or nook is asked again once a shooter has a line to where the bot hid, or the bot is off that spot (previousStance says which). The health in the options was right: the timeline printed each question at its frame's time, seconds late; it prints the asked time and health now. Still open: the bunker prices shooters as reaching its doorway throughout, and unseen shooters are not walked.
524. *mid-230-u, three hours and no Nether: the lava for a cast frame fetched from where the bot stood.* Jev chose to cast beside a surface pool (20:59) and the frame went down at (403, 78, 941), twenty blocks from it; four of ten were cast by 21:27, between nights. Then Jev topped up food (0 food carried, 0.38 against crossing at 0.35), which took the bot four hundred blocks off by boat. Coming back, 150 to 190 blocks from the frame, the lava fetch chose by distance from the bot: each of the four pools within two hundred blocks, the frame's own among them, was tried in turn, each walk's route search gave up in five seconds ("Took to long to decide path to goal!", the pathfinder's think timeout), and each was set aside for half an hour as a walk that "came no nearer". In twenty seconds they were all gone; the fetch made for pools 350 and 460 blocks off, then dug to the deep lava below where the bot stood, 135 blocks down and 130 from the frame. One bucket, 21:44 to 22:35, fifty-one minutes, where a trip from the frame was twenty seconds. Now the lava for a cast is chosen by its carry, here to the lava and on to the frame, a walk's seconds and three a block of any climb over eight (as `lavaTrip` prices the trips Jev chose by): a known pool that is a shorter carry than the lava in sight and the deep lava is walked to first, and no pool farther to carry from than the deep lava is. And a walk to a landmark whose route search ran out of time goes a leg of thirty-two blocks toward it (as `portalLeg` does) before it is judged; one that found no way is set aside as before. Other losses seen: back at the frame at 22:36 a zombie in a stand cell and "nowhere to stand to pour" took the frame's tenth site failure and the frame with four cast was left for a new one fifty blocks off, all ten to cast again (a mob in a cell counted as the site's fault); the crossing-kit food option priced no minutes and did not say the frame would be left hundreds of blocks behind; the portal way offered craft_buckets only when asked every twenty minutes, while six of twelve iron ingots carried went to a second iron pickaxe and the cast went on a bucket a trip; the inventory ran full (206 coal, 93 lapis) so crafts timed out with "0 free slots"; twenty-five restarts for new builds; night mining 30 minutes, Jev's own choice.
525. *mid-205-v, "shot by Skeleton" at minute 24, on the portal work.* It stood on a one-wide column of its own blocks (the rail span's cross, then two more up), feet five above a cave floor, and for a minute three zombies, four creepers and a zombie villager held four to five blocks off below it while a skeleton shot it from twenty to none. None of them could get to it: a zombie's hit reaches sideways, not up, and nothing it could walk, step up or drop along came within its reach of the column's top or within three blocks, where a creeper lights (the saved world, read). Every stance priced them at the bot: cover from the skeleton "about 144 damage" with the walkers biting and four blasts, the pocket "the nearest zombie villager can be at the bot in about 1 seconds", the fight "39.6 damage to kill them all"; Jev answered "none of these" fifty times in three minutes and took the retreat or the fight. The retreat found no way off the column each time and was said back only as "it failed"; the fight stood facing a zombie below. Now the walkers with no way to the bot are found by searching back from the cells where one would touch it (walk-reach.js; unsure, a chunk unloaded or the search leaving twelve blocks, it says nothing), left out of every stance's figures and of the eight counted (eight walkers below had crowded the skeleton out of the estimate), and said on every stance and in the state (noWayToTheBot). A stance's run can say why it failed, and the retreat does ("none of the 24 spots further from every mob has a route that passes none of them"). The recorded question, told so, is cover five in five (replay case column-walkers-below-skeleton). Open: come_down was not offered, pillarDescent asking the feet within 0.18 of the block's middle and the bot 0.2 off it.
526. *mid-229-r, "slain by Zombie" at minute 149, underground at y 19.* On its way to a lava pool down a tunnel at y 21, three zombies eight blocks off, it chose dig_down at 20 health: 5.4 seconds of digging against the zombies' two of walking, which the option did not say. They reached the shaft's top before the lid, the dig stopped for a biter within three, and the bot stood at the foot of an open one-wide shaft. The fight was then priced one zombie at a time ("0 of the eight cells round the bot are open ground: at most 0 at arm's length at once", 3.5 damage), taken at 0.51, and re-asked after four seconds for being over that pace; the zombies had dropped down the shaft into the bot's own cell. From 15.9 health the pillar and the pocket were offered as "none of them reaches it" (6.3 and 2.5 damage) eight times with two and three zombies in that cell. No block goes where a body is: the pillar's block goes into the feet cell and never went down, and the pocket closed round them would have shut them in with it. Each pillar failure was said bare ("and it failed"), and the bot jumped a block and fell back, hit about once a second, from 12.7 to none in ten seconds; it wore full iron and an iron sword, and the fight at 14 damage from 20 was the one that could have ended it. Now a column open overhead onto ground a walker stands on (columnOpening) bounds nothing: mobs drop into the bot's cells, as many as come, and the fight says so and is priced with all of them at arm's length. Mobs whose bodies are in the bot's own cells (inOwnCells) are said on the fight, the pillar (it does not rise while they stay; priced with every mob reaching) and the pocket (shut in with the bot; priced with them reaching). The pillar's top in such a column is said level with the ground there. A pillar that failed says why ("no block went down: 3 zombies stand in the bot's own cells with it"). dig_down says its race against the nearest biter and what losing it leaves. The eat stance, priced over its 1.6 seconds where the rest are priced over fifteen, now says the health it leaves to meet the same mobs with, beside the fight's cost. "Still reach it" counts the mobs that do, not every mob of their kind. Replay case zombies-in-own-shaft-cell: seal 0.59 recorded, fight 5 of 5 now (eat 5 of 5 before the eat line). Still open: the estimate sums every biter's hits with no half-second immunity after a hit (three zombies dealt about one hit a second here, priced at three).
527. *mid-230-u, two leftovers of note 524: a zombie counted against the frame's site, and a food top-up told nothing of its trip or the frame.* At 22:36 a zombie stood in a stand cell of the part-cast frame at (403, 78, 941): the server refused the stand's block ("the block is still air"), "nowhere to stand to pour" followed, and that was the site's tenth failure since the frame began (the count only ever reset when the frame was finished), so the code left the frame with four of ten cast and began a new one fifty blocks off, all ten to cast. A body in a cell is a passing obstruction. Now a failure is the body's when the placement names one (`place` tags the error with the body, and a refusal with a mob in the cell after the click is said as one), or when a stand, place or pour failure has a mob in the cells the cast works in at its slot (portal-cast.js `workCells`); it is said as not counted (`bodyFailures`) and not counted. A frame with obsidian in it is no longer left by a counter: each failure at its site asks the way (portal_method) at once, said with what is cast and the failures since the last block went in, each with its reason and count, and the ones a mob caused; `new_site` leaves the frame as it stands, said with what is left there against what a new frame costs from none, and keeping it says the slot is tried again. Without Jev, the fallback still leaves after ten. And at 21:31 the crossing kit offered "gather food first" with no trip and nothing of the frame; the hunt was a search outward with no bound, and took the bot four hundred blocks off by boat. Now, with a frame begun and no portal lit: cross_now says the frame is finished first, with the hunger and whether health comes back (healing.js); top_up_food says where it goes (the home chest, the plot, else a search outward from here with no bound, how far not known), the known food each with its trip (the walk there, about fifteen seconds an animal, the walk on to the frame) and what it gives against what is short, and that the frame, what is cast and its lava are left where they stand; and top_up_food_near is offered where food is known: the known food whose trip on to the frame is shortest, nothing farther searched, asked again when it is spent (`foodSources` in healing.js gives each source its place and yield). A probe of the 21:31 question: the old text, top_up_food 4 of 4; the new, cross_now 4 of 4.
528. *mid-235-p-fortress-7, "burned to a crisp while fighting Blaze" five minutes into the fortress stage (note 523's changes live).* At 20 health on the fortress's floor, a wither skeleton came to arm's length; at 15.5 the stance question offered the fight as "about 13.7 damage ... from 15.5 health", and Jev took it at 0.52. The blade was priced right (eight on Normal with its stone sword, 4.5 through full iron), the wither not at all: each hit withers a player for ten seconds, one health every two seconds that armour does not stop and that can take the last, renewed by the next hit (the running wither was said only as "it takes health"). The skeleton was gone at 7.2; the wither and a fire it stood in left 4.2. There, on its railed span over a 26-block drop into lava, take_cover against a piglin's crossbow was chosen eight times in three seconds and "none of these" half of them: the rail span had walled the feet cell one high, the cover wanted both cells empty, placed nothing, and each failure was said as "Tried 1 seconds ago here, and it failed", six times over, with no why. Then leave_nether at 4.2 and no food: keep_on said "no fight is started" and nothing of what a hit costs there; Jev kept on at 0.58. The fortress search then turned between back_to_fortress and a leg every three seconds for twenty seconds: the bricks were looked for as the nearest 512 (findBlocks keeps the nearest it counts), the fortress's walls beside the bot filled that within ten blocks, so no floor twelve blocks off was in view and the patrol set the fortress aside as "a few bricks"; back_to_fortress was offered as "set aside as a face not approached", taken, and undone in the same tick. At 23:24:15 the stance weighed a ghast 61 blocks off; a blaze 48 blocks off in sight was not among its mobs (the stance counts to 24, a blaze's claim to 22 until its shots land) nor said. Its fireball landed a second later, 4.2 to 1.7, and the fire burned the rest; in the Nether nothing but time puts that out. Now the wither is priced where a wither skeleton bites (combat-estimate WITHER: half a health a second from its first hit to ten seconds after it dies, two of them no faster), in the fight and in every stance it still reaches; the same fight reads 19.9 from 15.5, more than the bot had. The wither on the bot is said with its rate and what is left of it. The cover goes on whatever of its two cells is open (a block on the rail), and a cover that cannot go down fails with its why ("the cells toward the piglin are both solid already"); a stance failed more than once is said once with the count. keep_on says a blaze in sight shoots from forty-eight and a ghast from sixty-four, fight or no fight, and what one fireball costs at this health (replay: go_back 5 of 5, a new case). The bricks are looked for again with 4096 when the first 512 fill the look, and a look cut short by its count is never "a few bricks"; a fortress set aside as nothing to walk to says so, and going back is not offered from the spot that found it so (the patrol would set it aside again before a step), said in the state instead. And blazes and ghasts in sight past the stance's mobs, within their reach, are said with every stance option: the fireball's chance from there and what one that lands costs against the health there is. Still open: the estimate still prices a blaze at a steady rate at any distance (note 517); the misses light the ground round the bot, which the scatter alone does not count, and no trial has measured it yet.
530. *Jev's answers in a fight, measured: 0.2 seconds, not five.* The death timeline showed questions "asked" at one time and decided four to six seconds later (mid-229-s on 25581, dig_down "asked 23:36:43.9 at hp 10.9, decided 23:36:49 at hp 5.7"; mid-239-b on 25594, turn_priority 23:38:19.6 to 23:38:23 while a spider took 7 to 3), a mean of 4.3 seconds over the last half hour on 25581, 5.3 on 25585 and 34.5 on 25590. Every recorded decision carries the call's own time (latencyMs, from the TypeSafe client's request, retries included): over the last hour, 518 decisions on 25581, 288 on 25585, 539 on 25590 and 373 on 25594 averaged 0.17 to 0.18 seconds, the slowest 0.8, none over one; the prompts are 1,500 to 5,500 tokens (encounter_stance the largest), one call per question; the one-second heartbeat frames show no gap over 1.5 seconds in either death's last minute, so no event-loop stall under the fourteen trials either. The seconds were the record's. A decision's `at` is stamped when the answer comes back, and the timeline called it "asked"; the decision's frame was written at the next step's report, after the chosen stance had run, and the timeline printed it at that frame's time as "decided". dig_down was asked at 23:36:43.7 and answered at 43.9; the frames from 23:36:45 show the stance digging, and its arrow, zombie and arrow took the 5 health before the digging's step reported at 23:36:49. mid-239-b's retreat was running from 23:38:21, the spider biting it, when its turn_priority frame was written at 23:38:23. The gaps of 17 to 34 seconds were questions like while_cooking and crossing_kit, whose chosen work runs that long. Now a decision records `askedAt` beside `at`, and is framed in the flight record the moment Jev answers (a `jev_decision` event the recorder takes); the timeline prints each at its answer's time with when it was asked, the health then and how long Jev took, and older records are placed by `at` less the call's time. A stale answer: a stance asked is discarded and asked again when the health moved four or more before it came (isFresh), 134 discards in the hour, none in a fight, as answers take 0.2 seconds. What these two deaths do show is the stances themselves (dig_down under a skeleton's arrows and a zombie's walk; a retreat past a spider at a block), not waiting for Jev.
532. *Two stances that killed the bot as they ran: mid-229-s's dig under a skeleton's arrows, mid-239-b's run from a spider.* mid-229-s (25581, slain by a zombie at 23:36:54), at 10.9 health on the surface at night, a skeleton seven blocks off, a spider at 11.6 and a zombie at 12.1: dig_down 0.34, pillar 0.33, eat 0.21. Its option said "about 5.6 seconds of digging ... about 8.8 damage ... Shut in below, none of them reaches it" and no race: the bot had spawned at 23:26:39, before note 526's race line went in at 23:35. Priced as it is now, the spider (3.9 blocks a second) is at the shaft's top in about 3 of those 5.6 seconds. And the dig did not go down at all: the first arrow, a second in, knocked the bot to (150.7, 72, -12.4), half a block off its column at (150, -12), and digShaft dug on from beside it; the saved world has the grass and the dirt of the column gone and the bot never in it. Five seconds on the surface under the arrows, the zombie arrived, 10.9 to 5.7, and the fight, the charge and the meal after from there. mid-239-b (25594, slain by a spider at 23:38:24), at 7 health with a spider four blocks off: retreat 0.59 over fight 0.32 ("1.9 seconds and 1.3 damage"), the retreat told "A way is found: 10 blocks to footing 11 blocks further from every mob about", as if the spider stood still; "Creepers and spiders follow a running player" was said only with a creeper about, and with no number. The run went ten blocks in 2.9 seconds; the food search after it was stopped by the spider at eight; the retreat, held fifteen seconds with nothing to end it but six health lost, ran again without a question, found no fresh way from there and searched routes standing still for 3.8 seconds, the spider biting 7 to 1; dead a second later. Mob speeds, from the 26.1 jar (createAttributes, Mob.setSpeed setting the input to the speed, the input kept at 0.98, the ground's friction): a spider or a hoglin about 3.9 blocks a second, a skeleton or a creeper 2.7, a zombie 2.3, a piglin or a vindicator 5.3, against a player's sprint of 5.6 by the same sum; a mob gives up past its follow range, sixteen blocks unless set (a zombie 35). Now the speeds and follow ranges live in combat-estimate (bunker's line-of-sight walk uses them too), and the retreat says who follows it: each walker's speed, and with a way found how far behind it is when the run ends and how soon after it is at the bot, a spider climbing walls ("the spider 4 blocks off at about 3.9 blocks a second, climbing walls: about 8 blocks behind when the run ends, and at the bot about 1.7 seconds after"). A retreat with a way found is held for its run's seconds, then asked again, as every stance with an estimate is. "The rest are tried as it runs" said what does not happen: they are tried before it moves, and it now says so with the seconds. dig_down's race is at the nearest biter's own speed. And the shaft keeps the bot over it: knocked off, before each block and before the lid, it steps back to the shaft's middle and drops in, or fails saying so. Replay: the recorded retreat 5 of 5 retreat, now fight 5 of 5 (retreat-from-spider-at-7); the recorded dig 5 of 5 dig_down, with the race pillar 5 of 5 (dig-down-under-skeleton-spider-near). A third death the coordinator sent, mid-239-c (25594, a zombie, 23:53:00), is not this cause: at 8.1 health among four zombies at 2 to 6 blocks, the nook, the bunker, the fight and the pocket were each priced at 26 to 30 damage "(more than the bot has)", the bites while digging counted and said, and no run had a way; Jev took the least bad of them. Open there: the nook's failure a second before was said with no why.
529. *mid-241-a, "blown up by Creeper" at minute 16, with mid-230-v and mid-239-c.* At 4.8 health, full iron and an iron sword, a creeper 4.4 blocks off coming on and a wall at its back, the fight was told "about 2.5 seconds and 0 damage" (the creeper "not counted there", its blast said beside); Jev took it (0.41), the fight's hold called the dance with nothing swung, the dance closed in and backed into the wall unstruck, and it went off three blocks off: 4.9 to none. mid-230-v was told the same with a diamond sword and took two blasts (4.8 to 1.8, then, the dance closing in on a creeper lit 4.9 blocks off, to none); mid-239-c, in a hole with no cell open round it, was told "0 damage" five times while a creeper came to 3.1, and the blast took 14.9 through a helmet and a chestplate, where "15 at 3 blocks" was said beside. Read from the 26.1.2 server jar (SwellGoal, Creeper.tick): the fuse lights when its target is within three blocks, the creeper stands still while lit, and the fuse burns on while the target is within seven and in its sight, whatever strikes it (a hit and its knockback do not touch it); beyond seven or out of sight it burns back down a tick at a time; thirty ticks after lighting it goes off, and a blast six or more blocks off does nothing. Now a creeper fought is priced by that (combat-estimate.js creeperFought): its swings, the first a quarter second after it lights and one each swing time after (the bot's own, 0.7 for a sword), against the fuse, or the fuse left where it is already lit; and if they do not fit, the blast where the bot has backed to by then, at a walk, as far as the room behind it allows (survival.js backRoom; none where the dance does not run). No weapon carried kills a whole creeper inside its fuse by this (a diamond sword's three swings take 1.65 seconds); one already struck may die first. The blast is counted in the fight's damage and its fifteen seconds, and said with the reason in the fight, the charge, fight_from_footing, rail_and_fight and the dance, the same words; stanceCost counts a creeper a stance fights (the bunker's doorway, the blaze stands) the same way, and one met while building or digging two blocks off as before. The dance said "the knockback puts its fuse out"; it no longer does. And the dance now strikes at reach before it backs, backs out to six blocks rather than four and a half, holds at reach while the swings left beat the fuse it saw light, and does not close in on a creeper lit anywhere within seven. Replay case creeper-wall-behind-fight-zero (the recorded question, told so: the dance five in five, none of the building stances). Still open: nothing offers the block in its line that would put a lit creeper out of sight, and the back-off is a straight line.
533. *mid-235-p-nether-3-fortress-1, "fireballed by Blaze" three minutes into the fortress stage (shadow arbiter).* Three things. First, fifteen fortress_approach questions in three seconds at 23:44:18: the bot stood crouched at (244.3, 57, 145.7), its box resting on the netherrack beside and the block under its middle open, as the snapshot left it. The pathfinder pressed no key for twenty seconds from there, the span failed at once ("Nothing solid underfoot to bridge from") though its survey had counted eighteen cells, and the staircase found no route to the cell the bot was resting on. keep_searching set aside sixteen blocks around one brick, so the next brick past them was a fortress found anew, its failures wiped, and its ways were asked again: 18, 34, 47, 50 blocks off, the same fortress. Now every way to a fortress is surveyed and walked from a cell with a floor: crouched over an edge, the bot steps back onto the block it rests on first (bridging.js stepOntoFooting, terrain.js restingCell, the box counted where it touches), the span's survey starts from that block, and a span begun over the edge steps onto it. Leaving a fortress sets all of it in view aside, its bricks joined with gaps of eight or less (fortressExtent, 61 blocks for a 60-brick run where it was 16), said on the option ("its bricks out to N blocks"); what failed on the approach is kept for a return, and going back is still offered with the why. Second, at 23:46:03 and 20 health, hunt_323 was taken at 0.65: a blaze 3.8 blocks off, a second at 7.2, the spawner nine away. The option said "isolated" and priced the one blaze alone, 5.9 damage and 14.2 health after, while the stands beside it were priced for every blaze in reach (22.7 to 40 over fifteen seconds) and riskNow's fight with all of them was 18.6. The hunt fought twenty-two seconds and sixteen swings unhurt, and the first fireball (23:46:25.9) did end it at once. On this shadow port the old order then ran survival's step off the edge, a second hunt_target (defer, at 16.5 and alight), and the fire reflex's four steps out of the fireballs' flames, before the first encounter_stance at 23:46:30.2 at 9.5. Three to four blazes' fireballs and burning took 20 to 9.5 in five seconds. Now a hunt fight with others that reach the bot (a shooter in sight within its reach, anything else within sixteen) is priced with them, the one alone said beside it, a blaze spawner near said, and the option is no longer called isolated. defer says the shooters still in reach keep shooting where the bot stands, so leaving is not read as out of their fire. Replayed with that text, defer five of five (new case). Third, the last twenty-six seconds: from 23:46:33 back_to_wall was chosen at 3.5 health and held by a wall no blaze had a line to. No swing (nothing in reach), two fireballs blocked by the shield, 0.5 health from 23:46:36 to the end: hunger 16, so no healing, and raw mutton (2 hunger, to 18) carried. The wall was priced at 0 (seenFrom from its cell); the meal at 7.2 "more than the bot has", from the same blazes counted in sight a moment before (danger.js remembered and attributed sight). Jev chose the wall at 0.95 over eating. From 23:46:50 the wall's walk ended short each time, said only as "it failed", and the stance was asked five times a second with "none of these" half the time until a fireball at 23:46:59. Now the meal is priced by the lines to where the bot stands, by the same check as the stands, and says how many shooters have one ("Of the 2 shooters about, none has a line to the bot where it stands"); replayed, eat five of five (new case). A stand whose walk ends short says where and why (StanceFailed). Burning in the Nether stays time's alone to put out. Still open: the estimate prices a blaze at a steady rate, so the fight in the open (18.6 over 11 seconds) reads cheaper than the stands' fifteen seconds (note 517), and the shadow order lets a hunt re-ask while survival's claim is live.
536. *The arbiter live on every port.* Notes 485 to 506 ran it live on half the trial ports beside the old order on the other half. The comparison had served: the deaths it showed on the shadow half were the old order's, as in note 533, where a hunt that runs before survival each tick kept the turn while fireballs landed and the stance was first asked five seconds and ten health later. Which layer acts is Jev's, as the user asked. Live is now the default everywhere and the port list (.bot-state/arbiter-live) is gone; JEV_ARBITER=shadow keeps the old order for a comparison run.
531. *mid-220-h, mid-226-g, mid-202-q: three-hour runs with no Nether, a third of each on nights and climbs.* mid-220-h's clock: wait in shelter 35, return to surface 31, night mine 26. The waiting was a night mine chosen and refused: in the pocket Jev picked night_mine some 330 times, five seconds apart, each at a pickaxe already under the uses nightMine keeps for a dug climb out (48 of 52, 99 of 110, 120 of 124, 58 of 60, 64 of 252) with no sticks to make a spare; the option said "the mine stops when it gets down to that", nightMine returned at once, the pocket fell back to stay, and the question came again (mid-226-g: 160 such picks, at 52 of 54 and 75 of 76). The climbs were nine episodes, 52 minutes by the step: 20:51 to 21:04, y -14 to -9 and back thirteen minutes, the cast_at_lava frame's site ("none level and dry down here") a climb to open sky that nobody asked (surface_trip asks only with another rung to stay below for, and reach_nether has none), each six stairs undone by the walk back to the lava at the next pass, never above y -8; 22:25 to 22:45 and 23:23, the stone pickaxe rung's one oak log from y 6 and y 44, climbed by hand at 12 uses left, unasked, with three shelters on the way; 21:04, 21:30, 22:03, 23:03 the food search's climb (Jev's, priced as climbFirst) and the water for the cast. Underground at dusk continue_request read "Spend the next action on the player request while outside", and secure_shelter "in the shelter it can mine or wait", neither naming the work the night held up, its minutes, or that the dark below is the same at any hour; mid-202-q's fifty minutes of night mines were told the ore and never the portal waiting. Now the night mine is a way only where it would dig (nightMineOff: under the uses kept for the climb out and no spare to make it is not offered in the pocket or the shelter question, and the stay, the shelter and the state say why); a pocket choice that did nothing rests a minute and is said (notNow), not asked again five seconds on; the night's ways say the real minutes to dawn as minutes of the run and the work they hold up (workWaiting: the ladder's step, and for the portal the frame's obsidian in, the way and the lava chosen), carrying on underground says the dark there is the same at any hour, and the leave names the work. A portal site underground is a room dug out of the rock as well as ground up top: surface_trip offers dig_site (portalSiteDig: the frame and its walkways where the bot stands, natural rock only, solid floor, no liquid or falling block beside), and the climb says how far above the lava chosen the frame would then go; a climb chosen for the site holds to the top, not undone by the walk to the lava (replay: dig_site 5 of 5, a new case). A probe of mid-220-h's pocket at 22:19 with the new text: leave 5 of 5 (night_mine 5 of 5 before); mid-202-q's at 00:05, 250 uses and 6 minutes to dawn: night_mine 5 of 5 still, the minutes and the work said. Still open: surface_trip is asked only when there is somewhere else to go, so the log for a stone pickaxe climbed unasked from y 6 and why stay_below was not on offer there is not settled (the arbiter's ruling not the work's, or the probe handing back the same rung); dusk underground with two nights awake still goes to the shelter (secure_shelter 5 of 5 in the probe), and a shelter chosen then leave chosen in the pocket reseals two minutes later.
535. *mid-242-a, "shot by Skeleton" at minute 29 on the portal work (live arbiter), and mid-241-aa, "slain by Zombie" at y -18.* mid-242-a, at night on the surface at 17.4 health, zombies seven to eight blocks off, chose the pillar (0.78) and it went up: 76 to 78 by 00:07:36.7. On its top nothing was a threat any more (the zombies out of sight under it or past the eight counted for a biter, the skeletons past sixteen and landing nothing), so survival's own claim became the night's shelter; the arbiter's ruling for survival held ("gave survival secure_shelter (held)"), Jev was not asked who acts, and survival's step, finding no threat, asked survival_priority in the stance's place. secure_shelter was offered told only "a sealed shelter" and, in the state, one ready fifteen blocks off; there was no way to it from the pillar (the walk's search: noPath at 38.2), and the site searches after it stood the bot on the pillar's top, a walk having moved it 0.9 along it to x 19.15, while a skeleton shot it 17.4 to 12.1. At 41.5 a spider at 5.3 was a threat again, and flee's edge rule read the floored feet, x 19, as off the pillar at x 18, and walked it "off the edge": no route, but the step put it over, and it fell six blocks among the three zombies and the spider. The fight at 41.6 ("about 9.1 seconds and 5.9 damage") was priced on the pillar's top, where the three zombies had no way to the bot and were rightly left out (walk-reach); it was answered with the bot already falling. The rest of its inputs held (an iron sword, 8 armour points, the spider at 1.4, the skeleton's arrows at 2.2 halved by the shield); 12.1 to 1.9 in eight seconds was the bot fighting on the ground it fell to. mid-241-aa, 25594, full iron and an iron sword in a tunnel, two zombies in its own cell at 0.4 blocks: the fight was told 5.8 damage in 5 seconds, the zombie struck landing a third of its bites; both bit every half second between them, 1.4 a bite, 10.8 to none in five seconds. Its swings: two criticals at one zombie (43.8 and 44.4, the first 0.2 seconds after the eat stance's mutton was put away, when the game's attack strength had just restarted with the item change, so weak and no critical), then 2.4 seconds with none (the fight over its estimate after 1.3, asked again with the retreat's route looked for first), then a plain swing and a critical at the other, which read 0.01 nearer: neither died. A zombie in the bot's cell can be struck (canStrike, the server takes a hit inside its box); whether each swing landed is not in the record, the flight keeps no mob health.
