Now: the mobs a stance was chosen against stay the survival layer's while it holds (danger.js stanceMobs: seen or not, within the twenty-four a stance counts, until its fifteen seconds, its estimate's seconds or six health are spent, once it has run; not keep_working), so the claim, the step and the stance go on together, and the claim says so ("the pillar chosen against it 1 second ago goes on (asked again when it fails, ...)"); a stance still ends on failing, on a newcomer within six and on its cost, as before. On its own pillar is the body over the pillar's column, not the floored feet (survival.js onPillarTop), in the edge rules of flee and the step, the stance's "up" and the pillar's own check. secure_shelter says whether the shelter kept has a way from here, looked for before the question (refugeWay, the walk reading that search once), "no way to it from here (a route search found none): chosen, it is set aside, and the way ... is asked next", and the state says shelterWayFromHere. The estimate takes the half second a hurt body cannot be hurt again (26.1 LivingEntity.hurtServer): at most two of the hardest hits a second land however many bite, the wither and a burn beside them (combat-estimate within, in the fight and every stance); note 526's open point. Three zombies against a stone sword read 27.5 where it read 30, four at arm's length in the open no longer three times a tunnel's two. A mob in the bot's own cells is not knocked out of reach by the swing: it bites at full rate while struck (inCell); mid-241-aa's fight now reads 10.5 from 10.8. And the swing finishes the mob it struck while it is in reach, not whichever reads nearer (combat.js strikeTarget; not past a creeper come nearest). Replay suite 18 of 19, the known gap. Still open: the first swing after the weapon comes to hand is made at once, weak; the question a fight over its estimate asks waits on the retreat's route searches with nothing swung; the night's site searches (six of up to 0.6 seconds) stand still under fire.
537. *mid-243-a, "flipping enter_nether <-> tunnel" at minute 31.* Casting at its lava at (21, 67, -4), the stairs down from open sky at y 84 stopped at y 78, eleven from the lava, with no site level and dry; surface_trip offered the climb (five blocks, "the frame then goes down up there, about 16 blocks above the lava") and dig_site, and Jev chose the climb each time, 0.62 to 0.74. At the top the lava was seventeen off, more than the twelve the cast walks to, so the next pass dug the stairs back down to 78 and asked again: eight climbs in three minutes (00:22:20 to 00:24:51), the step trading between the staircase (tunnel) and the climb (enter_nether, the ladder's name; the climb sets none), until the staircase was set aside for pacing its own cells. Note 531's hold kept the climb only while it was being made (goal.surfaceReturn); once at open sky nothing kept what it was for. Now a climb chosen for a portal site is held for the lava it was chosen with until a frame is set out: below the sky the climb goes on, and at the top the site is looked for up there, as the option said, not the lava walked back down to first; the site found ends the hold. (The flight frames' first column, 'return_to_surface' through it all, is the last survival action on record, from 00:18, not a climb in hand.)
534. *The block in a creeper's line, the move note 529 left missing; and mid-241-n, "blown up by Creeper" at minute 18, backed into a second creeper and then stood still to find its run.* Read again from the 26.1.2 server jar: SwellGoal runs while the creeper is lit or its target is within three blocks (distanceToSqr under 9), stops its navigation when it starts, and holds the move flag (priority 2, over the melee goal's 4), so within three a creeper stands where it is, lit or not; each tick it sets the fuse burning only while the target is within seven and `Sensing.hasLineOfSight` holds, else burning back (-1); Creeper.tick adds that to the swell, never below none, and goes off at thirty. The sight is `LivingEntity.hasLineOfSight`: one ray from the creeper's eyes (1.445 up) to the target's (1.62), stopped by any block with a collision shape. So a block where that ray crosses stops a blast no weapon carried can, and behind it a creeper within three stands and does not light; backed past three, it walks round and lights again where it sees the bot. mid-241-a (note 529: 4.8 health, a creeper coming on at 4.4, four up on a mineshaft's floor, a wall at the bot's back) was offered only stances costing more than its health. Now encounter_stance offers block_creeper (creeper-sight.js, `creeperBlockOption`): for the creeper within seven whose fuse ends first (lit before not lit, then nearest), the cells the ray crosses between the two bodies (a voxel walk), the nearest to the bot that is open, has no body in it, is within 4.5 of the eyes, has a face to place against and holds a quarter block or more of the ray; beside the bot at its feet or head, the other of the two as well, two high, placed from the bottom up with `stay`. It is said with where the block goes, the seconds until the line is cut against the fuse left (lit, its lighting seen or half taken as gone) or its walk to three blocks at its own speed and the fuse (combat-estimate.js `creeperBlocked`), the blast where it goes off if that is too late, what it does behind the block, and the rest of the mobs priced as every stance is; held, with the line stopped, it is staying behind the block. On the saved world round mid-241-a's spot the cell is over the bot's head, cut in 0.6 seconds, 1.4 before the creeper would go off: the recorded question with it offered (replay case creeper-wall-behind-block), block_creeper five in five. mid-241-n (25594, 00:11:37 to 46): two creepers, one 3.2 off a block up and one 5.4 off three blocks down behind the bot, both priced "about 6 blocks off, where the blast does nothing", as backRoom measured the room straight away from each alone; Jev took the dance at 16 health (0.47 to the fight's 0.44). The dance closed in four times without the bot moving, then backed from the first for its 1.2 seconds, off a three-block drop onto the second's level and to 2.5 blocks from it, and that one went off from 4.3: 16 to 10.9. The dance was still held (5.1 lost, under six), its creeper now 7.1 off, past the dance's six, so the question came from the held branch, where the run is not scouted: the retreat said "no route is checked yet ... found as it runs", Jev took it at 0.80, and the run's route searches (up to 24 spots at 150 ms, before it moves) stood the bot still 2.9 seconds while the creeper came down to 1.9 and went off. Now backRoom ends a step before one that comes within three of another creeper, and the dance's back-off stops there too and a step from a drop (`backingEnds`); a held stance no longer on offer scouts the run before the question, as a new one does; and the retreat unscouted says its search is made standing still, about 3.6 seconds at most. And mid-244-aa (25583, 00:26:08, 20 health to none in one blast through full iron): the fight was taken twice (0.84, 0.76) with the creeper 6.3 and 6.5 off, past the dance's six so the dance was not offered; its 19.4 was the spear zombie's, and the creeper, struck once (17 health), was priced as going off 5.5 blocks off with 2.5 of room (about 1), its three swings 1.65 seconds against the fuse's 1.5. It came down to 2.4, two blocks up a ledge, and lit; the fight's hold called the dance, which held (it counted the next swing as now and took "a step from it", 3.5 blocks, whatever the height), walked at the ledge unable to strike, then backed with about a second of fuse left, and it went off 1.6 blocks off. Now the hold is only at reach, or a step off on the bot's own level with the step counted as the price counts it (a quarter second), so it backs at once there. Still open: the dance's close-in went nowhere for 2.5 seconds at a creeper a block up (the forward key at a ledge); the back-off is still a straight line away from one creeper, and walks at 2.5 to 3.5 blocks a second in these frames where the price takes 4.3; and mid-244-aa's creeper came 2.6 blocks and down its ledge after it was seen lit.
539. *mid-243-q-nether-3, "tried to swim in lava to escape Enderman": a turn_priority question that never came back (live arbiter).* Eight seconds after the 00:26:55 restart, crossing toward a fortress leg at y 41 and 20 health, an enderman came on from four blocks to three to two. The watch preempted the work three times for it as a newcomer ("picked up newcomer" after the first two): the holder's list of known mobs is set only when the arbiter gives a turn, so the same enderman was new at each step closer, and the second stop cut the question asked after the first. After the second pickup survival claimed escape_threat (the enderman at arm's length, immediateThreat) beside the work, and turn_priority went out at 00:26:57.47. It never came back: from then to the death every frame says "decision / asking Jev: turn_priority" with one mark (forMs 384, 805, 1951, ... 5581), no answer was recorded, no status line was written, and the third preemption was never picked up. The enderman hit 20, 16.9, 14, 10.7, 7; the hurt watchdog's "stopped for the survival layer" set its abort for a loop that was not looking, and a pending preemption turns the watch off (watchOnce returns it), so even the lava at 00:27:02 preempted nothing. Nothing swung, nothing asked the stance; knockback carried it off the ledge into the lava at y 28. Why the question did not end is not settled from the record: decide's own watcher calls the task's check every 100 ms, the check throws on a preemption or the watchdog's stop (the stops logged checks 1 and 6 ms before), and in isolation that abort ends a hanging request at once, on Node 22.17 and 22.23, headers or body pending, after a forced GC. Whatever held it, the turn belonged to a question for six seconds. Now the arbiter watches the question itself (answerOrCut): the task's check throwing ends it, thrown as the abort would be; the bot hurt after it was asked, or no answer in five seconds, gives the turn by the rules' pick (the question's own fallback, survival's claim before the work's), said in the log, held ten seconds before Jev is asked again; survival's step then asks the stance of the mob that hit. The question left behind is told to stop at its next look, and it gives the turn mark back only while the mark is still its own (decisions/index.js), so "asking Jev" is not put back over the layer that has the turn. A newcomer picked up joins the holder's list, so the same mob coming closer is not new again. Tests: arbiter.test.js, a question that never settles cut by a hurt (survival runs), by the task's check (thrown), by the time (rules, asked again within ten seconds); a newcomer picked up does not preempt again at two blocks. Still open: the cause of the hang itself, and whether the watch should look for the body's reflexes (lava) while a preemption waits to be picked up.
538. *mid-231-r, three hours with no Nether and the last hour in a shaft pocket at 0.7 health.* Its clock: wait in shelter 68 minutes, search food 26, return to surface 21, night mine 9, the stone pickaxe rung's stash_valuables 7. The arc: from 21:40 the pickaxes wore out one after another (an iron one to a fight at 21:40, stones and irons made from the last spruce logs and planks at 21:48, 22:00 and 22:10), and the last iron one broke at 22:22:57 at y 20 on the stairs of a climb out from y -12, with no log, plank or stick left; the climb went on by hand, 24 seconds a stair, then straight up to the surface at 22:34, where the stone pickaxe rung wanted one log. A fight took it to 14.7, then hunger 11 turned the turn to food: forty-five minutes of search_food over frozen ocean and snowy plains (swim_across spinning, "found no route" walks, a stash_valuables trip at hunger 4 that put the coal, lapis, iron and the stone sword in a chest) found nothing, hunger reached 0 at 23:22:47 on the walk, and starvation took it from 11.7 to 0.7 in forty seconds while the search walked on. Five hundred blocks west at 0.7 a rabbit was killed and eaten on a snowy slope (hunger 3, 23:29), a second chased, and at dusk a shaft pocket dug at (-454, 81, -178). The stone pickaxe rung's twelve stalls were the turn held by food and shelter, not a recipe: the pockets held 62 cobbled deepslate and a stone axe, and only a log was missing. In the pocket pocket_next was asked 72 times over three nights and three days. At night it offered stay ("until daylight, about 3 real minutes"), leave and the mob hunts, stay taken; by day only stay ("though nothing is watching it", nothing of the day going by) and leave ("go back to the stone pickaxe step... about 1 zombie hits or 1 arrows end it"), with none_good on top about a third of the time (00:23:59: none_good 0.39, stay 0.31, leave 0.30). healing.nearestFood said "none known" all hour: the rabbits hunted a minute before sealing in were not remembered (sightings kept sheep, cows and hoglins). The move missing was a player's: by day, get out and find food, the only thing that brings health back. And leave, chosen four times by day (23:40, 23:47, 00:02, 00:05), was refused each time as "Nearby threats still block the shelter exits" with nothing in sight: a shaft pocket in snow has no side a door can be dug through (snow blocks are not among the building materials a closure is), its way out is up through its cap, and leave() knew only side doors; the refusal returned as a success, so it was chosen again ninety seconds on. Now pocket_next offers go_for_food, by day or night, where hunger is under eighteen and the safe food carried would not bring it there: the pocket is opened and the food question's own ways follow as its children (forageChoices: the hunts in view, herds and rabbits seen, home, a village, the search, each with its walk, the dark and the hostiles on the way), said with the food known, that food is the only way health comes back, the daylight left and what going out at this health costs; a way chosen is held as the food plan. By day the stay says the daylight it spends and, hurt under eighteen hunger, that staying brings no health back. A shaft pocket with no side to open is left up through its cap and climbed to where it was dug from (the refuge keeps its top); a leave that finds no door says why ("No wall of this pocket can be opened as a door", or the threats) and rests a minute in notNow instead of being chosen again. Rabbits are remembered where seen, like cows and sheep, and said as known food and as a walk back (seen_food). A probe of the 00:23:59 pocket with the new text: go_for_food 5 of 5 (a new replay case). Still open: the pickaxes were spent to nothing on a climb with no sticks left and no spare asked about (the rung for a new one came only at the surface, after the tools were gone); and the food search's forty-five minutes without an animal, at 11 then falling hunger, never weighed a walk back to known ground against another search.
540. *mid-243-q-nether-3 again: the two things note 539 left open.* The watch while a preemption waits: watchOnce returned the waiting preemption and looked at nothing else, so from the third newcomer stop at 00:26:57 to the death the lava at 00:27:02 preempted nothing. Now the body's physics (lava, fire, the head in a block, air) is looked for at every look whatever waits, and one ranked above the waiting preemption takes its place: the holder is stopped again, said ("preempted work find_fortress again, newcomer waiting"), and the arbiter picks up the physics, which its claims then give the turn by rule. Newcomers and alerts are still not looked for while one waits, so note 539's enderman stepping from four blocks to two does not preempt three times; a newcomer replaced by the lava keeps its id, and the ruling still knows it. And the question that never came back. Ruled out from the code: there is no queue, semaphore or per-bot lock on Jev calls (typesafe.js and decisions/index.js have none; withRun and withRequestSignal pass the signal through; the ledger's record is synchronous; the breaker throws at once; the retry backoff sleeps on the signal). Ruled out from the record: a starved check. The lag watch said nothing in that window (its one line that hour is at 23:52), frames came several a second, and the hurt watchdog's sinceCheckMs of 6 and 63 put a task check within the last 100 ms both times; in that window only decide's own watcher calls the check (checkStall is the only writer of that mark), and with the preemption and the hurt stop both standing every such check threw, so the abort was called and the request under it did not settle. The question before it, cut by the second stop, did end at its abort (the work's mark came back with its own start time and the preemption's error was recorded). In isolation on the trial's Node 22.23.3 (undici 6.28.1), the real path (decide, withRun, TypeSafe, fetch) against a local server holding its headers, holding the body halfway or never answering, with a forced GC every 20 ms, ended 60 ms after the check began to throw every time, and undici keeps its request's controller so a collection does not cut the chain. Not settled: what held that one request after its abort, since the process quit at the death and the question's end was never seen. So a question now ends when it is stopped, whatever its request is doing (decisions/index.js endsWhenStopped), every question and not only turn_priority. And the stages of every question are kept, always on: queued, asked (the turn taken), sent, headers (with the status and request id), body, parsed, failed or retry with their reasons, stopped (with why), settled and recorded, each in milliseconds from queued, with when the watcher last looked. They go on the decision's record, on the flight frames while the question is out (question), and in the arbiter's line when turn_priority is cut short; a stopped question whose request is still out a second on, or settles 250 ms or more after its stop, is said with them ("[question] turn_priority stopped 1s ago and its request is still out: ..."), as is any answer that took three seconds. Tests: arbiter.test.js, a newcomer waiting stands against another newcomer and a creeper, gives way to the lava, which keeps the enderman's id and is picked up and given the turn; a waiting alert gives way to the fire, the fire to the lava but not to the head in a block; with turn_priority out and a newcomer waiting, the watch takes up the lava. decisions.test.js, a question whose request never settles ends within the second its check throws, gives the turn mark back and says the request still out a second on; an answered question records its stages in order.
541. *mid-235-q-nether-2, "doomed to fall" at the fortress, and "flipping find_fortress <-> detour" before it; with mid-235-q, mid-235-q-nether-1 and mid-235-p-nether-3 knocked off edges in the same hour, and mid-235-q-nether-1-fortress-1 and mid-235-p-nether-3-fortress-2 at their fortresses reaching nothing.* The bot stood on its own span at (235, 62, 212), a block from a thirty-block drop, the fortress's floor three across and four down. For a minute before the death (00:36:12 to 00:37:10) the work turned between find_fortress and the stall's detour: every three seconds fortress_leg took back_to_fortress (the legs were 96 blocks each) and fortress_approach then took keep_searching (no route, the staircase failed, a two-block span ending four above the floor), each answer undoing the other, the detour moving the bot a block. A blaze thirty blocks off on the diagonal (south-west, two below) landed a fireball at 00:37:11 (15.5 to 12, alight to 9). take_cover was chosen at 9.4, offered as "none of them reaches it" at 3.3 damage, and put its block at head height west (234, 63, 212): the cover went on the side of the shooter's larger axis, and from the diagonal the line passed a block south of it. The next fireball landed (10.2 to 6.8) and pushed the bot 0.4 blocks east over the lip, no key pressed (the flight's velocity); fight_from_footing was chosen a fifth of a second later, already airborne, and walked nothing. Every stance but eat and seal had said the push ("one that lands puts it over"); none offered the rail against shots, which rail_and_fight gave only while something could be fought. mid-235-q-nether-1 was hunting hoglins for food when a ghast's fireball threw it off a ledge, 16.5 to 8.2; survival's claim was the hunt, not the ghast, the turn went to the work six times, and the bot stood in the ghast's line until the next fireball (the walk back was not walking: it stood, "cannot be reached"). Now take_cover puts each block in the ray from the shooter's eyes to the bot's, which it must have to fire (creeper-sight blockPlan, with eye heights for blazes, ghasts, skeletons, piglins and the rest), two high where the cut is beside the bot, with the block under it first where the cut hangs over air; it says where each goes, the shooters it cannot cover and why, and a line already stopped is said as that. rail_and_fight is offered with only shooters about too, as walling the drop sides and holding behind the wall, priced with the shots. A threat that is not the hunted kind is survival's claim over a hunt Jev chose, and stepOnce answers it before the hunt's step. keep_searching keeps where it was chosen and the ways it was chosen over; from that spot back_to_fortress is not offered (the same ways asked again) and the state says so, as note 528 does for a spot that found nothing to walk to. And the Nether's edge rule is one model: an edge is a fall by a push or by a drop's own drift. The pathfinder and the staircase refuse a step beside a deadly drop only while something can push the bot (danger.js pushersAbout: a shooter in sight within its reach, a biter within eight in sight or four out of it, a shot on its way); with nothing that can, the edge is walked at its cost, as mid-235-p-nether-3-fortress-2's seventy-three minutes of refused bridge edges (81 passes, no stretch reached) and mid-235-q-nether-1-fortress-1's approach from three above its floor, flipping as this one did, needed. A drop of two or more onto a lip beside a deadly drop stays refused, pushers or not (mid-244-q's drift), in the pathfinder as in the staircase; lava beside the feet stays refused. Replay: the stance at 9.4 with the cover in its line and the rail on offer, take_cover five of five (new case). Not the same cause, and open: mid-235-q stood three and a half minutes stranded on its span over the lava sea with no route and no building blocks but four oak logs and three crimson stems it never made into planks (restock offered only mining), and a piglin that came into view at 6.5 knocked it off with its first arrow; mid-235-p-nether-3's retreat from a wither skeleton stood two seconds searching routes (said: "up to about 2 seconds standing") while it closed from 6.6 to 1.8 and hit twice, the second a knock off the floor's edge at y 58.
545. *mid-244-ac, "fell from a high place" at full health, and mid-235-q-nether-2-fortress-2, "tried to swim in lava" in the Overworld: two of the pathfinder's own moves over a fall.* mid-244-ac, walking back toward three sheep from a cave floor at y -3, was routed down a three-block drop onto a one-wide ledge; at walking pace the body carries on about a block and a third over a three-block drop, so it missed the ledge altogether and fell forty into the cavern beyond, 20 to none. No stall recovery, no dig and no broken block: a drop maxDropDown allows, landing beside a void. mid-235-q-nether-2-fortress-2, at 10.6 health on a hunt for food, towered one block to lay a block level beside the tower's top; the pathfinder lays such a block by backing to the edge crouched, turning as it goes, and mid-jump the crouch held nothing and the unturned body backed the other way, off the top and six down into a lava pool: 7, 4.3, 1.5, dead. The Overworld had no rule for either (note 514's were for lava beside the landing, or a miss in the placement's own direction). Now, in every dimension, a drop of two or more or a gap jump is refused where the cell one on in its direction, or either side of that, falls into lava or costs half the health or more; and a block laid level beside the floor is refused where any side of the cell it is laid from does. Walking level beside an edge is unchanged. The rule is a separate check (overFall) in src/movement.js, apart from note 541's work on the edge rules there.
544. *mid-244-a, "slain by Zombie" at minute 45, on the surface at night: the run ended with its chasers ten blocks behind, and the night's pocket was asked in the stance's place.* At 14.5 health among five zombies at 1.4 to 3.1 blocks, after a pillar they reached from a slope, Jev chose the retreat (0.82), told "a way is found: 14 blocks ... about 2.5 seconds at a run" and, since note 532, that each zombie would be "about 10 blocks behind when the run ends, and at the bot about 3.5 seconds after". The run went as said: 00:34:10, four zombies 9.7 to 11.1 blocks behind and walking up. The retreat was held for its run's seconds and no longer (note 535's stanceMobs), and at ten blocks a zombie is past the eight counted for a biter, so nothing was a threat: survival_priority was asked (wait_for_day_sealed 0.63, told only "Within 24 blocks now: 7 hostile mobs"), then shelter_method with the zombies at six to eight blocks in its state and in none of its options: shaft_pocket 0.82, "the fewest blocks, done in seconds". About six seconds of digging against their two of walking; the first bite came as the dig began (13.1 to 11.7 to 10.1), the dig stopped for a biter within three, and retreat, a shot and a pillar from among them took it to none in five seconds. What the retreat had said was true and was dropped the moment it ran out. Now a mob is known to be coming by its own walk: threats keeps where each was over the last two seconds (danger.js trackMobs), and approach is how much nearer to where the bot is now it walked, blocks a second, so a zombie after a running player reads 2.3 while the gap grows. The mobs a stance was chosen against, once it is over, still in sight, within the twenty-four a stance counts and their follow range, and coming a block a second or more, are the survival layer's (danger.js followers): the claim says them with their speed and how soon they are at the bot (comingAtTheBot), and the stance is asked again, not the night's questions or the work, with previousStance.askedAgainFor saying it is over and they still come; a mob standing, or left be, or behind a wall is not. The questions that stand the bot still say who is coming and how soon at its own speed (danger.js coming, the jar's speeds), against their own seconds: shelter_method's shaft pocket says its seconds of digging and the cap (shaftSeconds, dig_down's reckoning) and the race, and that a biter within three stops the dig part-way; the room and the shelter kept say whether the mob coming is there before the walk and the sealing; the pocket's race is at each mob's own speed, not three a second for all; survival_priority's wait for daylight, the shelter and rest_to_heal say it against a shaft dug here; all three questions carry comingAtTheBot in their state. Replay: the recorded shelter_method, 5 of 5 shaft_pocket as recorded; with the shaft said as it is now (its seconds reckoned from the column's grass and dirt; the walk's race not reconstructed, the flight keeping no mob positions), saved_shelter 5 of 5 (a new case, shaft-pocket-zombies-coming). Suite 24 of 25, the known gap. Still open: the stance asked again after the run was not recorded (it was never asked), so what Jev takes with the chasers at ten blocks and coming is untested live; and a walker coming from further off that no stance was chosen against is said to the night's questions, not made a threat, so the site surveys before shelter_method (up to six of 0.6 seconds) still stand the bot still with one walking up.
542. *mid-243-f, "slain by Cave Spider" at minute 102, at y 39 in a mineshaft.* Full iron, an iron sword, hunger 17 and nothing to eat, cutting cobwebs seven blocks from a cave spider spawner (the saved world: SpawnData cave_spider at (127, 39, -33), and a second at (170, 36, -26)). Three fights with a cave spider in two minutes each took a bite within a second of starting, and the poison did the rest: the flight's health, to the hundredth, fell a point every 1.25 seconds exactly (00:50:46.79, 48.04, 49.34, 50.58, then renewed by a bite at 50.73: 51.44, 52.47, 53.69), 8.07 to 0.23, while the bites themselves took 0.88, 0.42 and 0.42. At 00:50:48.4 and 6.1 health the stance offered the fight as "about 1.3 seconds and 0.4 damage to kill them all" and Jev took it at 0.67: the estimate counted one cave spider, its bites alone. Three things were wrong. First, poison was not priced: cave_spider carried only the note "poisons" (so did the bogged and the bee), and the state's line said "about one health a second or so" with "about 7 seconds left" two and a half seconds after the bite that gave seven (mineflayer keeps the length sent, not what is left). Read from the 26.1.2 server jar: PoisonMobEffect deals one magic damage every 25 >> amplifier ticks, only while health is above 1 (so 1.23 goes to 0.23, and the poison alone never kills; the bite after it does); magic is in bypasses_armor and not bypasses_cooldown; a cave spider's bite gives seven seconds on Normal and fifteen on Hard, a bee's sting ten and eighteen, a bogged's arrow a hundred ticks, a witch's potion of poison forty-five seconds; a new bite renews it, one poison over another adds nothing, and it ticks when the time left is a multiple of twenty-five, so a fresh bite's first point is three quarters of a second after it. Second, the '?' cave spiders were not in the figures at all: the stance's mobs are those in sight (or seen within eight in the last three seconds), and the two at 5.6 and 5.8 round the corner were said only as "Out of sight but about"; a second later they were at 3.8 and 4.8. The spawner was said as "a mob spawner", not what it makes. Third, the healing facts were right as far as they went (no healing under eighteen hunger, and hunger was 17) but said nothing of the poison: natural healing is not stopped by it (FoodData looks at no effect), and at a point each four seconds the poison outruns it three to one; at full hunger with saturation it is up to a point each half second (the flight shows 0.3 a half second after its meal at 00:49:10). Now poison is priced where the wither is (combat-estimate POISON, poisons on the cave spider, bee, bogged and witch): from a poisoner's first bite, three quarters of a second on, to its length after its last, one poison however many bite, with what is left on the bot now (effectLeft, the effect stamped when it came, session.js) running in the fight and in every stance; counted to 1 at most wherever the health is known (poisonFloored: the fight, and costSays for every stance), and said: the fight's "About 5.1 of it is poison: the poison on the bot now has about 4.6 seconds left, and each cave spider bite that lands poisons the bot (cave spider 7 seconds, renewed by the next); one health every 1.25 seconds that armour does not stop, however many poison it, and only while health is above 1 ...", each stance's figure the same way, "7 hits ... end it, or one once the poison has taken it to 1", and the state's effect line with its rate, what is left now, how many more points and that the bite or hit after it kills. A wither or a poison a stance's mobs give while it builds or digs now runs its length past the reach (it stopped with the hits). The biters out of sight within the eight a biter counts from, with a way to the bot (walk-reach), are counted in the figures and said so ("Counted in the figures though out of sight, each with a way to the bot: a cave spider 6 blocks off, a cave spider 6 blocks off"), and are among the mobs the stance was chosen against. A spawner says what it makes, read from its block entity ("A cave spider spawner is 7 blocks off ... it makes more cave spiders"), in the stances and the place's facts. The healing state says the poison beside the healing ("three times as fast as health comes back here"). The same fight is now "about 3.9 seconds and 9.4 damage ... (more than the bot has)", the seal 9.3, the bunker 9.4. Replayed with that text: retreat five of five, where the recorded question took the fight (new case poisoned-cave-spiders-by-spawner). Replay suite 24 of 25, the known gap. Still open: the future spawns are said, not priced; the retreat's footing was not weighed against the second spawner fourteen blocks from where the run ended; the half second after a bite in which a poison tick does nothing (and a bite after a tick lands only its excess) is not modeled; a stance's own expected damage (its hold) is not floored; and whether the server sends a spawner's SpawnData in the chunk (the name said only then) is unconfirmed live.
543. *mid-231-r, mid-220-h, mid-241-x: three hours each, no Nether, the last pickaxe spent far from wood.* Where a pickaxe's uses were said to Jev: climb_out (the uses against the climb's digs), surface_trip's climb (the same, when asked), the night mine's kept uses (note 531), the crossing kit (24 uses), a ladder option that spends wood (pickaxeLeft), and upkeep, whose spare_pickaxe came only when every pickaxe was under 24 uses with the makings carried, and whose wood_reserve listed the pickaxes and the depth. Nowhere were the uses set against the work ahead: the step in hand's digging and the way home to open sky after it. mid-231-r at 22:19:25 was asked wood_reserve or carry_on at y 31 with 226 uses of iron pickaxe and no log, plank or stick, told "about 32 blocks under the surface", and carried on (carry_on 0.54, none_good 0.37); the step was a staircase down to lava at y -11, up to 120 digs, with 74 blocks to open sky from there, some 340 in all. By 22:22 it had 46 uses, the ladder counted that worn (a fifth of its uses or 64, a rule Jev never saw: the rung read only "stone pickaxe"), and the climb for the stone pickaxe's log broke the last pickaxe at 22:22:57 at y 20. mid-220-h at 22:25, 66 blocks down with an iron pickaxe at 12 uses and one plank, climbed straight up for the same rung's log unasked; the pickaxe was gone fourteen seconds later. In the three logs surface_trip was asked once (mid-231-r 22:08, stay_below), against some fifty climbs counted in them (many only seconds long); mid-241-x's stone pickaxe went from 12 uses at y 59 (23:41) to none, and it ended its three hours on the iron pickaxe rung. Two things kept the climbs from Jev: surface_trip asked only with stay_below or dig_site on offer, and stay_below named the ladder's next rung even when it wanted the same log (the iron pickaxe's sticks, the shield's planks), so on a wood-starved ladder it was either absent or not what it said; and a climb made unasked was stored as the climb picked and held as if chosen while a surface return stood. Now the pickaxes' budget is one fact (src/pickaxe-budget.js): the uses carried, the digs of the step in hand to its target if all rock (three a stair, two a block across), the way home from there to open sky (straight up and by stairs), how far short and what the rest costs by hand (24 seconds a stair), the pickaxes the pockets make and what is missing for one, and the nearest wood seen or remembered. Upkeep offers the spare when the uses fall short of the step and the way home, not only under 24, and a carry-on from before is asked anew once they fall short; underground wood_reserve reads as the climb for wood it is, before the step in hand, with the heads carried; carry_on says the digs it runs short. surface_trip's stay_below leaves with the step every rung after it whose first gathering is the same climb and names them, is offered only if one is left that is not, and says the budget; a new mine_first digs the ore in view with the uses the climb leaves spare, asked again after each; a climb holds only if Jev chose it (wood chosen at upkeep is its climb chosen). A pickaxe rung with a pickaxe still carried says it is a spare, the ladder's worn rule, and the budget. A probe of mid-231-r's 22:19 upkeep as it would read now, the step the staircase to the lava: wood_reserve 4 of 5, none_good 1 (a new replay case; the first wording, "cut a few logs now" with the by-hand rates leading, drew none_good 5 of 5). Still open: why each unasked climb was unasked could not be pinned from the logs (the flight's goal snapshot keeps neither rungTime nor surfaceTrip); the ladder still counts a pickaxe under a fifth of its uses as worn, now said rather than asked; and the step's digs are known only for steps with a target (a night mine or a search is not measured).

546. *mid-242-aa, "flipping cast_portal <-> fill_bucket" four times from 01:03 to 01:14 (strike 4 twice), and "fill bucket and retreat from tunnel" once, minutes 50 to 61.* Not a bucket filled and emptied, nor the cast refusing its site: the frame at (-7, 47, 38) was cast a bucket at a time and finished (ten of ten by 01:14), its lava from a pool forty blocks off and eighteen above, one bucket a trip (four iron ingots carried; craft_buckets was on offer, 0.07). Ten trips took thirty-nine minutes of working time, about four a trip, for three reasons. The trips were dug: from the frame every half-second route search to a scooping spot ran out of time, the spot was passed over as if no way reached it, and the step dug a staircase up to the pool instead, where the walk the stall's detour made there at 01:08:57 took thirty-three seconds. The fetch was renamed every pass: the cast named its step fetch_lava, then the fill's staircase stepped a stair and named the fill, two changes a stair, so at 01:08:46 the stairs going four blocks in five seconds toward the pool were raised as "turning between cast portal and fill bucket 4 times in 2 seconds without getting anywhere", strike 4 (the slip mid-110-a's obsidian and its tunnel made). And at the pool, ten buckets in, every source beside the shore had been taken and left flowing lava; the scooping spots were only shore cells beside a source, so none was left, and from 01:09:38 to 01:13:36 the fill dug round the pool's rim toward the air over the sources two blocks in (a stair can never stand there), backing off and turning with its retreat (the strikes at 01:09:37, 01:10:42 and 01:13:36) until a source by the far shore came in range. Now the lava is scooped from any dry place a block or two above the pool with a source in a bucket's reach and nothing solid in the line (obsidian.js scoopSpots; a pour of water still wants the shore beside the sources); a spot whose route search ran out of time is walked to with a walk's own minute, and a walk from here that comes no nearer rests ten minutes, the staircase the way from here meanwhile; and the cast names a fetch once, until another of its phases names it afresh. No question changed: which lava, how many buckets and where to cast were already Jev's, and none of the three was a choice between ways. Seen and left open: the held cast_at_lava option still said the lava chosen before, (-6, 46, 35), was seven blocks off and "a trip for lava is a few seconds" after that pool no longer served the fetch, while cast_frame beside it said eighty seconds and craft_buckets the four minutes a trip measured; and the first fetch out of the frame's pit paced two cells, (-5, 49, 40) and (-4, 48, 40), until the flip watch took it at 01:03:05.
552. *mid-208-k-nether-1, bitten to death by a hoglin while it ate.* In the Nether at 9.4 health, a hoglin twelve blocks off walking up at about four blocks a second. Three things let it in. The fight from footing's hold counted its eight seconds from a record of a hold at the same hoglin half a minute earlier, never cleared, so the moment the new fight began the hoglin was left be as out of reach; danger then left a walker marked out of reach as no threat even while it came straight at the bot. And the meal's check for a mob close counted by the registry's type, which calls a hoglin an animal: the bot ate with it at 1.2 blocks and again at 2, bitten 9.4 to 4.6 and 4 to none, the claim reading only "Eat beef now, about 1.6 seconds". Now a hold is one run after another; two seconds between starts a new one with its own eight seconds (src/survival.js). A walker left be as out of reach is a threat again once it is coming at the bot (src/danger.js). The meal counts as hostile what the danger list does (a provoked neutral too, not a piglin left be for the gold worn), and one whose walk has it at the bot before the meal is done is close; a meal claimed with biters still coming says it is eaten standing still and names each with its speed, how soon it is at the bot against the meal's seconds, and its hit through the armour worn (src/vitals.js, src/arbiter.js). Tests for each; replay suite 27 of 28, the known gap unchanged.
547. *mid-243-aa, "blown up by Creeper" (port 25583, 2026-09-28 01:11:53 to 01:12:03): the block in the creeper's line, held, walled the bot in.* Jev took block_creeper (note 534) and the first column went in at 01:11:55.2, diagonally north-west of the bot at (74, 70, 38), the creeper 6.2 off and not lit. It did not stand behind it. It walked round the columns at three to four blocks for about four seconds (5.9, 3.8, 4.1, 4.2, 3.0), and each time a line opened the stance, held on "the line is stopped", re-planned and put another two-high column in it without asking: west at 56.5 (creeper 3.0 off), north at 58.6 (3.3), north-east at 59.8 (2.8, lit). It came in on the east, the one side left open, to 1.73 blocks, lit; with the bot's own blocks on the west, north-west, north and north-east, the retreat said no way out and the dance had no room to back into. Jev took the dance at 0.40 (none of these 0.25), and one blast took 20 through full iron. The placements are from the stance's reports, not the world (the saved world has only the crater). The 26.1.2 jar read again: within three blocks the swell goal takes the move from the melee goal, seen or not, and the creeper stands (note 534 holds there); past three, the melee goal follows without sight, finishing its path and making a new one to the bot's cell about a second later, so a creeper behind a block walks round it and stops at the first point of its way within three, lighting there with a whole fuse if it sees the bot and standing if not; out of sight three seconds on end it forgets the bot, and picks it up again only by sight within sixteen. Note 534 said a creeper past three "walks on ... and stops where it comes within 3"; it did not say that way goes round the block to a line. And the state's fight estimate said the creeper cost nothing ("goes off 6 blocks off") while the fight and the dance each said a blast of 23: the state's estimate was built without the creeper's fuse, health or the room behind the bot. Now the block option works out the creeper's way to the bot with the planned blocks solid (creeper-sight.js creeperWalk) and says where it first comes within three, how many blocks and seconds that is, and whether it sees the bot there; where it does, that it lights there with its whole fuse and when it goes off, that the block buys those seconds for backing past six blocks or striking it, and that staying costs the blast, which is counted in its price; and that out of sight three seconds it forgets the bot. The stance keeps where the creeper stood when its line was cut, and is asked again the moment a line opens or the creeper has moved 0.75 blocks (blockCreeperHeld), with the reason in previousStance.askedAgainFor; a new line is never blocked unasked; each report says where the creeper is. The state's estimate carries the creeper's fuse, health and room, as the options' do. The dance, where a lit creeper can be neither killed nor backed from inside its fuse, says "Nothing the dance does here stops it" with the blast. Tests: survival.test.js, the walk round to a line priced with its blast (and one within three, out of sight, standing); the hold asked again as the creeper walks and as it comes round, no block placed unasked; the state's estimate and the dance for a lit creeper 1.7 off with no room. Replay: creeper-wall-behind-block's "behind it" sentences re-said as the code says them now (its way worked on the test scene of the same spot), block_creeper 5 of 5; suite 27 of 28, the known gap. Open: the last approach, about 2.97 to 1.73 blocks in 1.3 seconds round the north-east column, does not fit "stands within three"; the frames are a second apart and disagree by up to a block (2.8 at 01:12:01.99 against 1.7 at 02.02), so nothing is built on it.

553. *mid-242-aa again: three prices for one lava trip (note 546, left open).* Asked at 01:03 with the frame at (-7, 47, 38) half cast, the held cast_at_lava said the lava chosen before, (-6, 46, 35), was seven blocks off and "a trip for lava is a few seconds"; cast_frame beside it said about eighty seconds a trip to the nearest lava, reckoned from its distance and height; craft_buckets said the four minutes a trip the trips made had taken. Each bucket came from a pool forty blocks off and eighteen up: the lava chosen before had no source left to scoop. Each option priced its own trip, from its own lava, one with the trips measured and two without. Now there is one trip for every way (portal-cast.js fetchTrip): from the frame, or from here with none begun, to the lava that serves the next bucket and back, the climb in it; and once this frame's cast has made trips, their working time each is the price, the reckoning beside it. It is said in the same words wherever it is said (fetchSays), and n trips cost n of it (tripsCost), in cast_frame, build_new's bucket-a-block comparison, craft_buckets (which had added ten seconds of its own) and cast_at_lava's "against". The lava said is one a bucket can be filled from: of the sources in sight, those a scooping spot reaches (obsidian.js scoopable, note 546's spots) come before lava no bucket reaches, such as a source at the bottom of a shaft. And the lava chosen before, seen within forty blocks with its own block no longer a source and no source a bucket takes within eight of it, is said as gone: the held option says the frame goes down beside it but it has no source left to scoop, each bucket fetched from the nearest lava that serves at the one trip's price, not "a few seconds". A held lava out of sight, or whose block is still a source (under a roof, the staircase going to it), is not judged gone. No question changed: the options and what each does are as they were; only what they say a trip costs is now one figure.
548. *mid-235-q-nether-3, mid-208-k and mid-235-q-nether-2-fortress-1, three deaths to blazes in the Nether that each began with the fire reflex; and mid-208-k-nether-2, "tried to swim in lava" out of a fire.* In each of the three, the bot was alight with no flames about, and the fire reflex held the turn for five to seven seconds with the keys let go while a blaze 5 to 48 blocks off kept shooting: 20 to 9, 15.3 to 5.9, 9 to 5. The stance was not asked until the reflex ended. The Nether has no water and nothing steps out of a burn, so the reflex had nothing to do. mid-208-k was then asked its stance at 5.9 health, alight, with the fire in no figure. mid-235-q-nether-3, sealed at 7 health with nothing to eat and eight blazes 23 to 28 blocks off by their spawner, was told its pocket was "watched by nothing". It was offered a window toward them that "0 of the 2 shooters in sight would see in", at "about 0 damage in the next fifteen seconds" (the 20 seconds of digging by hand ran past the fifteen, and nothing after them was priced). It chose the window. The choice was held as the plan for ninety seconds, so the window was dug three times, the seal going back between, with no new question. On the third opening, a blaze that had come down to the bot's height in front of the mouth shot it from 7 to none. mid-208-k-nether-2 was in fire at 19.5 health; the out-of-fire run went to a cell beside an edge, carried on over it at a sprint, and fell 47 blocks into lava.
Now:
- The fire reflex takes the turn only when it can do something: flames at the feet or beside the body, or the server's in-fire hurt; or alight with water at hand (a water bucket, or a pond within eight blocks) outside the Nether (vitals.js fireToAnswer).
- Otherwise the burn is priced in every stance. session.js records how long each fire lasts (a fireball 5 seconds, a fire block 8, lava 15). combat-estimate burnLeft reads the seconds left, and every figure counts them at one health a second that armour does not stop. The fire is one fire however many blazes light it, where four blazes in sight had counted four health a second. It is said in the effects as well.
- The hole and window stances now count every blaze within 48 blocks. That includes those that would come into the mouth's line once they sink to the bot's height, as a blaze after a target does (the 26.1.2 jar: it keeps its eyes about the target's, from about seven below to six above, a new height every five seconds). A blaze that sees in shoots for the whole hold and is not priced as killed by the sword. The price covers the digging plus fifteen seconds held after it.
- A pocket choice that opens the wall on the mobs (the window, or open_on_watcher) is carried out once; sealed again, the pocket is asked again.
- The pocket's stay text now mentions blazes and ghasts beyond 16 blocks that are within firing range. The leave text counts the fireballs that would end the bot, with their fire. tunnel_out is offered away from the blazes, ending eight blocks further from them.
- The out-of-fire run goes to no cell with a fall beside it that note 545's rule counts as deadly (movement.js fallBeside, fallOff on the world's blocks). A shallow drop beside a cell still does not count.
The 01:12:09 pocket question is a replay case (pocket-window-toward-eight-blazes): with the texts as the fixes leave them, Jev took tunnel_out five times in five.
550. *mid-235-p-nether-4, "slain by Wither Skeleton" at 01:17:41 on 25590, and mid-235-p-nether-4-fortress-2 the same way at 01:30:53 on 25585: the fight priced at the sword's recharge, not at the bot's own pace.* Both were at 20 health in full iron with an iron sword and a shield, a wither skeleton seven blocks off, and both fights were told "about 2.5 seconds and 10 damage to kill them all": four swings at a sword's 1.6 a second, every one landing, nothing for getting to it. In both the charge ran for about four seconds (a route to the spot the skeleton stood on, GoalNear, ending only there or on a stall) while the skeleton walked in to 0.9 to 1.8 blocks, and nothing was swung: three hits, 20 to 15.5 to 11 to 6.6, then the first swing (a critical, 01:17:39.6; 01:30:34.2). nether-4 swung twice (two criticals, 18 of the 20) and was dead by the wither and a fourth hit six seconds in; fortress-2 swung once and retreated four times from 6.6 as the wither ticked, and was caught at 3.6. Measured over the flight records of 2026-09-26 to 28 (780 files with swings; 1,923 runs of swings at one mob, 1,052 of them ending with the mob gone within reach; 612 fight answers followed until the mob was gone, the bot dead or another stance), with the mob's death taken as a second before it left the entity list (its 20 ticks): from the first swing a kill took the swings its health needs at about 0.9 seconds each after the first on the median (1.12 on the mean; iron sword against a zombie 2.7 seconds median over 186 kills, where 1.9 was priced from the first swing; diamond against a zombie 1.9 over 50; iron against a spider 2.1 over 29; iron against a skeleton 2.9 over 20; stone against a zombie 3.6 over 15), the gaps between recorded swings peaking at 0.6 to 0.8 (plain) and 1.0 to 1.2 (a critical's jump), four swings in ten a critical. From the answer to the first swing: none within three blocks (median 0), 2.4 seconds at four to five blocks, 4.4 at five to six, 4.5 at six to seven, 13 at seven to eight, about two seconds a block past three and a half where a walk is a quarter second. The mob landed 0.18 hits a second of that closing (a quarter of it at reach, 0.69 a second there) and about 0.27 a second from the first swing to the kill, near the third reckoned. The 52 fight answers against one biter that ended in its kill took 243 seconds and 105 health in its blows; the old estimate priced them at 129 and 67. Read from the 26.1.2 jar: the swords are one plus three plus the material's bonus (wood and gold 4, stone and copper 5, iron 6, diamond 7, netherite 8) at 1.6 a second, as the table had; the healths are as the table had but for the parched (16, not 20); and a zombie wears two armor points (Zombie.createAttributes, kept by the husk, the drowned, the zombie villager and the zombified piglin), so a stone sword's five is 4.9 and a zombie takes five of them, not four. Now (combat-estimate PACE, swingEvery, leadFor): a biter's swings are priced at the weapon's own swing time plus 0.2 (a sword 0.9 seconds), the first at once where it is at reach; the first mob out of reach is closed on for 1.6 seconds a block past three and a half before its first swing, landing 0.18 of its hits a second meanwhile; the mob's armor is taken off the bot's swings; the figures fitted to those 52 fights come to 252 seconds and 106 damage. Shooters, creepers and the stances' own fights keep their pricing but for the swing time. The fight says the pace in words beside its figures (fightHere.pace and closingFirst; survival.js puts it in the option): "With the iron sword, about 4 swings that land: the wither skeleton 4 (20 health, 6 a swing). In the bot's own fights so far one came about every 0.9 seconds, not the 0.63 of the weapon's recharge: the jump for a critical, the knockback walked back and the misses. Closing on the wither skeleton 7 blocks off came first: about 5.8 seconds before the first swing ..., the mob landing about one hit in each five or six seconds of it on the average; one that walks straight in is at reach in about 2.1 seconds at its own speed, and at reach before the first swing they landed about 0.7 hits a second." The two questions now read about 8.5 and 8 seconds and 18 and 17.3 damage from 20; asked again with that text (the other options as recorded), Jev took the pillar 5 of 5 in both, where it had taken the fight (the pillar's recorded text priced fighting the skeleton at its foot as it comes, with no charge). The retreat side, fortress-2: every retreat said the skeleton would be at the bot again after the run ("about 12 blocks behind when the run ends, and at the bot about 3.9 seconds after"; 15 and 5 seconds; 14 and 4.7; 5 and 1.4), at 2.7 blocks a second against the run's 5.6 and giving up only past 16 behind; it was 8.6 to 9.2 blocks behind where each run stopped, three to six fewer than said, and closed during the next question and route search each time. Tests: the wither skeleton's fight (8.5 seconds, 5.8 closing, the words), the zombie's armor and the jar's healths and swords, and the fight option's words; the estimate's older tests take the new pace. npm test 1715 of 1715; replay suite 26 of 28 and the known gap (poisoned-by-witch-apple-offered 3 of 5 on the first pass, 5 of 5 run again; the cases carry their recorded text). Still open: the charge itself (survival.js charge navigates to the mob's starting spot with no stop on reach; a stopWhen on canStrike would end it as the mob comes in), which is what the closing's seconds and its hits price; a retreat's "behind when the run ends" reckons a straight run from the mob, where the runs recorded ended three to six blocks nearer; a pillar's fight is priced with no closing, which is right only for mobs that come.
554. *The replay case warden-booming-no-way-away, "stay 3 of 5" on main.* It asks mid-230-n's pocket at 10 health, a warden 8 blocks off and two booms taken in the last minute, with stay and leave and no way away; staying is death at the next boom. The prompt the suite sends was captured at f29fe71 (the suite's first commit, when it passed), 7981604 (note 530), 747b4c9 (531), 3a8691d (538), 90173a4~5 and now: state, instructions and options are byte for byte the same at all six, so notes 531, 538 and 548 changed nothing this case is sent (the option labels and `when` lines are not sent at all). The failures are a knife edge that was there from the start: stay about 0.40 against none_good about 0.45 on every call, and every kept replay-suite result since 18:02 on 2026-09-27 has stay 1 to 4 of 5 at times (a pass allows one). The expectation holds: in the 26.1.2 jar the boom (SonicBoom) is 10 damage scaled by difficulty (6 on Easy, 15 on Hard; `"scaling": "always"`), in the bypasses_armor and bypasses_enchantments tags, fired within 15 blocks across and 20 up or down, released 34 ticks into a 60-tick charge with a 40-tick cooldown after, so about one every five seconds; the warden's fight activity has it beside the melee, whatever the walls. At 10 health on Normal one boom ends the bot. What the options said around that was "healing from 10 health" and "about 10 damage, every few seconds", with nothing putting the two together, and the question's own guidance, sent with every pocket, said "A pocket is safe but gains nothing". Now wardenSays gives the boom's damage for the server's difficulty, "about every five seconds", and, with the warden in reach or a boom taken, how many booms end the bot at its health ("At 10 health, one boom ends it, whatever heals before it"). The pocket guidance says what a pocket's walls keep out and what they do not, for any pocket: mobs that walk and what they shoot, while the walls stand; not a creeper's blast, which can break one open, a warden's boom, or fire, poison or hunger already on the bot; and that health comes back at eighteen hunger or more, where it had said "while fed". The guidance alone moved nothing (stay 5 of 10 in a probe); with the boom said at the bot's health, stay fell to about 0.23 and none_good took 10 of 10. The two warden cases carry the new text. warden-booming-passage-offered now answers none_good 5 of 5, where it had been tunnel_from_warden about 3 of 5: the passage is 23 seconds of digging within reach of a boom that ends the bot, which that option now says. Both answers are expected there. Replay suite: 28 of 29 passed, the known blaze gap still open.

549. *The reflexes, asked of Jev (the user, 2026-09-28: "Which reflexes do we have? Can we get rid of them and just ask Jev").* No death behind it: a list of what the code did by rule before Jev was asked. The body's own dangers: in lava, the walk to the nearest cell out (water first), a pillar when that cell was two up, else back toward the last dry footing, else up; alight, the bucket poured, else the water run into, else nothing; in fire, the run out; a head in a block, the step aside, then the dig; out of breath, the swim to air. And the encounter's first moves, all before encounter_stance: on a one-wide span the hold (still, crouched, walls, off away from a creeper), off an edge with a mob close, the swing at arm's length, the shield at every shot, a mob in reach in a sealed pocket fought, and the hunt's step off a deep drop when hurt while it healed. Now the body's dangers are one question, body_way (src/body.js), asked the moment the step meets one, with the ways the code can carry out from here, each with where it goes and its seconds, and how long the body lasts at its rate (lava about eight a second before armour, burning one, a head in a block two, the breath's seconds then two); Jev answers in about 0.2 seconds (note 530), and the old order is the fallback when it has not answered in a second. The ways are the old mechanics (survival.js lavaWays, vitals.js fireWays, headWays, airWays), with an enchanted golden apple offered in lava or alight, and in the Nether the bucket and the pond are not offered and burn_out says nothing else puts it out there. Note 548's gate stands under it: the fire takes the turn from the other claims only while the body can act on it (in flames, or alight with water at hand, and now an enchanted golden apple carried, in any dimension, vitals.js fireToAnswer); and 548's price of the burning (combat-estimate burnLeft) is body_way's too, the seconds of fire left and the health they take, said as all the health the bot has where they are. Burning left to burn out by Jev's choice is held until it could have ended or health falls four more: the survival step does not ask again meanwhile, and the arbiter's fire reflex does not take the turn for it (observeReflexes), where standing in fire still does. The vitals ask with the client they are handed, which every caller now hands them (the survival step, the work's turn, the idle loop, the End fight): before this the survival step called them with none, and its head in a block went to the fallback unasked. The encounter: with Jev reachable and no stance holding, flee asks the stance before anything is done; the span's hold is a stance, hold_on_span, said with its open sides, the blocks for them and what can push; the edge is the fight_from_footing stance and the drop said on every stance; the swing is every stance's own and the question's wait (it swings meanwhile, as before); a mob in reach in a sealed pocket goes to the stance; the hunt's step off a deep drop hands a mob it can see to the survival step's stance. The shield is shield_policy: a shot is in the air a fraction of a second, less than an answer, so what to do about shots is asked for the shooters about, beside the stance and not waited for, with each shooter's shot's flight (rough speeds, not measured) and what a shot does through what is worn; take_shots keeps the shield down (projectile-guard.js deflect) until a shooter not counted comes, health falls four, or a minute. Without Jev every one of these is the code's first as it was. A lastDry kept before the dimension was recorded counts again (the lava's way back had refused it). Replay, five new cases built from the questions as the code asks them now: in lava at 14 with no armour, the cell out 3.2 seconds off and an apple carried, eat_golden_apple 5 of 5 (the old rule walked, about 25 health of lava); alight in the Nether at 6 with twelve seconds of fire left, eat_golden_apple 5 of 5; alight at 7 with a bucket, douse_bucket 5 of 5; a skeleton at twelve at 5 health, shield_at_shots 5 of 5; a zombie on a span over the lava sea, pillar 5 of 5 (two up out of its reach, the drop said with it; hold_on_span offered and not taken). Suite 32 of 33 with the known gap (warden-booming-no-way-away passed on its second run, 3 stay in 5 on its first). Still open: a held stance still swings by rule between its questions, as its text says, and raises the shield as shield_policy stands; the hunt's step off an edge with nothing seen to ask about is still the code's; and none of this has run live yet.
555. *Note 550's open point: the charge that routed to where the mob stood.* In both wither skeleton deaths of note 550 (mid-235-p-nether-4 on 25590 at 01:17, mid-235-p-nether-4-fortress-2 on 25585 at 01:30) the fight's charge (survival.js charge) walked a route to the spot the skeleton started from, GoalNear with nothing to end it but arriving or a stall, for about four seconds with the skeleton 0.9 to 1.8 blocks off, and swung nothing until it ended: three hits, 20 to 15.5 to 11 to 6.6, before the first swing. Now the charge follows the mob where it is (GoalFollow, re-routed as it moves) and ends the moment the sword reaches it (stopWhen canStrike, or the mob gone); the swing (defendNearby) comes next. The same for every close-in that approaches a mob to strike it: the creeper dance's close-in aims at the creeper where it is when the step begins and, at reach and not lit, strikes at once rather than on the next look; the food hunt's walk to an animal (foraging.js) ends at its own swing distance, 2.8, not the follow's two blocks; the End fight's approach to a perched dragon head ends as the sword reaches it, for strike_head to be offered. charge_shooter (closeOnShooter), the step onto a hovering blaze, the night hunt and the mob hunt's approach (mob-hunt.js, stopWhen canStrike, re-routed to the mob each four seconds) already stopped at reach. Re-measured on a synthetic field (test/close-in.test.js: open stone, the bot walking at 4.3 blocks a second, the mob standing or walking in at 2.3 or 2.7 to a block off; no route search time, no server lag): from the charge's start to its first swing, a standing mob at 4, 5.5, 6.5 and 8 blocks is struck at 0.3, 0.6, 0.8 and 1.2 seconds, from 2.9 to 3.3 blocks; one walking in at 2.7, at 0.2, 0.4, 0.6 and 0.8 seconds; in every run within 0.1 seconds of the sword first reaching it. The old charge on the same field swung 0.5 to 0.9 seconds after reach (0.66 to 1.64 seconds in all), from 0.2 to 1.4 blocks, having walked on into the mob; in the trials, with a real route and the mob on its spot, it was four seconds. So note 550's measured closing (1.6 seconds a block past three and a half, 13 seconds from seven to eight) is largely this charge, and a walk is about a quarter second a block: PACE.closePerBlock and leadFor (combat-estimate.js) should be fitted again from the trials' fights once this is live; they are not changed here. The charge's refusals (a creeper, more than two up or down, from an edge, lava beside the mob) are still read where the charge begins; the follow walks with no digging, towering or drop past two, as before. Tests: the charge's first swing within a quarter second of reach at a skeleton walking in from seven, one standing still struck from its reach, and one walking away followed and struck (all three fail on the old charge). npm test and JEV_ARBITER=shadow npm test 1733 of 1733.
551. *Three deaths to a ghast: mid-235-p-nether-4-fortress-2 at 01:27:49 under its fireballs, mid-242-aa-nether-2 "burned to death" at 01:58:04, mid-208-k-nether-3-fortress-1 "tried to swim in lava" at 02:01:18.* The first hid. At 15.3 health (01:26:52) Jev chose out_of_sight from a ghast twenty-five to forty blocks off, told it "has none within the fifteen seconds": that is a walker's way to a line (bunker.js lineRegained), and a ghast flies and drifts at random. Past twenty-four blocks the held stance kept nothing (danger.js stanceMobs counted within twenty-four), so the work had the turn and stood still, twenty-one loop passes a second, while the ghast drifted to new lines and fired at 01:27:04, :07 and :25. A hit inside the hold's slack (its estimate and one blow) asked nothing again; the stance was asked again only when the bot was off its spot (01:27:27.9 at 7.0 health, out_of_sight again at 0.75), and three more fireballs ended it. Its shots were said "2 arrows from the ghast (about 5.2 each)": 9 is Hard's fireball, Normal's is 6, 3.1 through iron, what each cost. Nothing offered sent a fireball back, and no bow was carried. The second chose take_cover (0.78, 01:57:42) behind a cobblestone already in the line of a ghast sixty blocks off; the cobblestone held and the option said the five-block drop into lava beside the bot. But behind it the ghast was out of sight, the stance kept nothing past twenty-four, and the work's cross_toward walked the bot out onto a span in the ghast's line. There the span's hold looked for shooters within forty-eight blocks only, raised no wall with 102 blocks carried, and held still four seconds with the ghast in sight fifty-eight to sixty-two off; its fireball (18 to 12.8) threw the bot five blocks down into the lava, and the burning after it took the rest. The third chose pillar_up (0.65, 02:01:06) from a ledge of the lava sea, told "on top a push is a fall of up to 9 blocks": the pillar's height, not the drop beside its foot. On top at y 64 a ghast came into view at fifty-four to sixty-three, the span's hold held still three seconds at full health by the same forty-eight, and the fireball (20 to 16.4) put the bot thirty-seven blocks down into the lava; the wall came after the hit. Checked in the 26.1.2 jar: a ghast fires only at a target it can see within sixty-four blocks, about a second after it has a line (a shriek at half), then every three seconds while it keeps it (Ghast$GhastShootFireballGoal); its fireball starts four blocks in front of it at 0.1 a tick, gains 0.1 and keeps 0.95 of its motion each tick (about 1.0 seconds over twenty blocks, 1.7 over forty); the fireball damage type scales with difficulty (6 on Normal); a player's strike at a fireball, with anything in hand or none, turns it along the player's look at a block a tick, owned by the player, before any damage (Player.attack, deflectProjectile, ProjectileDeflection.AIM_DEFLECT), and a large fireball owned by a player hits a ghast for 1000; the server takes a strike at an entity whose box is within six blocks of the eyes; an arrow does not return it (AbstractHurtingProjectile.hurtServer takes nothing); its blast has a power of 1, at most 1.3 at a block, each block taking (resistance + 0.3) x 0.3 off, so a block of blast resistance about 4 or more never breaks (cobblestone, stone, deepslate, blackstone, basalt, obsidian) and netherrack, dirt and planks can; an arrow lands for its speed times 2 rounded up, at least 6 to forty blocks and 5 from fifty. Now a held stance keeps a shooter it was chosen against as far as it fires from (a ghast sixty-four, a blaze forty-eight; danger.js stanceReach), so survival keeps the turn behind the cover and in the hiding place. out_of_sight, nook and take_cover are asked again once a shooter they were chosen against lands a hit, said in previousStance. The hiding place says the ghast drifts and when it next has a line cannot be worked out, its fireballs left out of the figures and priced a hit. Every stance with a ghast within sixty-four says when it fires and that it drifts. A new stance, return_fireball: stand in its line, look at it, and strike the fireball when it comes within five blocks, said with the flight each way, that a struck fireball kills the ghast it hits, the price of one missed, and that no trial has yet measured how often it is struck (src/ghast.js); the shield at a shot gives way to it. A ghast in sight twenty to sixty-four blocks off is offered to the bow, with the arrow's flight and the arrows that bring down its ten health. Cover, the rail and rail_and_fight against a ghast are put from a carried block its blast does not break, and the cover and the rail say which carried blocks hold. A shooter's shot is called by its name (fireballs, a potion, wind charges). The ghast's hit is 6. The span's hold looks for shooters within sixty-four, so the walls go up for a ghast at sixty. pillar_up says the fall from its top as the pillar and the drop beside its foot, into lava where that is lava, and names the shooters in view that can push the bot off it. Tests for each; a replay case (ghast-hidden-from-drifts-to-a-line) from the second question at 7 health, its text as the fixes leave it, where Jev still chose out_of_sight five times of five (now asked again at the next fireball that lands); replay suite 29 of 30, the known gap unchanged. Seen and left open: the fight's estimate still prices a ghast out of reach as "2 swings, 9.8 seconds" (combat-estimate.js fightEstimate); how often the strike lands, with the server a tick or two behind, is for a trial to measure; on a span or a pillar top the stance is not asked, so return_fireball is not offered there, only the walls.
556. *mid-208-k-nether-3, "3× The staircase toward (10, 73, -13) is set aside (no safe step toward it from (-15, 35, 14) (no floor 6)); trying another way", and mid-235-p-nether-3-fortress-3, every patrol pass reaching 0 stretches for an hour.* One cause: the staircase steps on ground and digs rock, and both bots stood where the way on was a gap of open air. mid-208-k-nether-3 chose go_back for food at 8.6 health (leave_nether, 01:56:37); the staircase was for the portal back, 37 blocks across and 38 up from the end of its own span over the lava sea at y 34. Of the eight steps (four headings, up or level), six nearer had no floor under them: "no floor 6" was that count, said as nothing. The staircase rested by the portal's area, as it should; nothing planned it again. But with the portal in view the way back (returnFromNether) was the walk in and then the staircase alone, so each pass walked (the pathfinder towered a block or two, y 35 to 49 in a minute), met the rest at once, and threw it to the stall question: stillness_detour seven times in forty seconds, "differently" (0 of 8 blocks) and a crossing at y 35 the only ways offered, none_good 0.41 once. walkToKnownPortal, for a portal not in view, already asked portal_way when its staircase rested (note 495); returnFromNether never did. At the fortress, the patrol's walk to the next stretch, 12 blocks off across a gap of two at the same height, was refused at the edge, and the staircase after it found "no floor 2" and rested; each pass after met the rest, and lastPass said "reached 0" while every fortress_leg question after that chose leg_south. The span over the gap was fortress_approach's (cross_level), asked only from off the fortress's floors. Now a staircase's blocked step says "no floor to step onto (a gap, for a span or a pillar)" and each reason is said with how many of the steps nearer it refused. The way to a portal in view is the one to a portal remembered (stairsOrWay): the crossing at this height, then the staircase, and once that rests (by the target's area or from this landing, tunneling.js restingWay) or stalls, portal_way, asked once for each rest from each place, its place now its height too (a pillar up is somewhere else). portal_way offers climb_here: pillar straight up to the portal's height from a column within five blocks with no lava or water in or beside it, said with the rise, the blocks against those carried, how far across the portal is from the top, and the push's fall ("a fall of 41 blocks into lava"); state.portalAbove says the height. On the fortress's floors, a stretch the walk did not reach is Jev's way to choose (fortress_approach toward it, state.stretch saying the walk's failure, the failed walk among what failed, the record kept per stretch): the span over the gap priced by its cells and blocks, a pillar or a drop where one fits, the staircase with its rest said, or leaving; the staircase is no longer run unasked after the walk. Tests: a stretch 13 blocks off across a two-block gap at the same height is asked and the span chosen reaches it (2 blocks laid); the portal back in view with the walk failed and the staircase resting asks portal_way with climb_here ("Pillar straight up 38 blocks ... 26 left after ... 37 blocks across"), once from a place, WaysResting met again; both fail without the fix (the staircase's rest thrown). npm test 1732 of 1732; replay suite 28 of 29 and the known gap. Still open: fortress-3 now stands 90 blocks off with no blocks carried, where every span is priced as running out at once; and a pillar's top 37 blocks across from the portal is a crossing at y 73 of as many blocks, which the climb's text says but does not price.
558. *A tool: busy but going nowhere, caught by us, not by watching.* The watcher (scripts/trials/watch.sh) stops for a death or a loop, and mid-242-aa-nether-1-fortress-1 was neither: for over seventy minutes it walked back and forth at a fortress and dug here and there, 81 patrol passes, no stretch reached, no blaze seen, and only the user watching caught it. `node scripts/trials/progress-audit.js [auto | port ...] [--minutes 15] [--history 60] [--json]` reads the last minutes of each running midgame trial's flight record (the window's files in full, the history's observation lines alone) and the tail of its bot log (placed in time by the stamps inside its lines), and says per trial: the dimension, the last milestone (the bot's own and the midgame verdict's) and how long ago; the run clock's minutes by step or rung in the window; the 4×4 columns stood in and how many are new against the history, walked against net, ground blocks gained and laid; for a fortress the last fortress_leg's fortressInView (passes, minutes there, blazes seen near it, the last pass's stretches reached); the questions asked most with their commonest answer, the none-good share, and the bot log's none-good, [stall] and [still] lines. Each flag says its threshold (under 10% new ground; 150+ walked and under 16 net; 48+ ground blocks dug with under 25% new ground; one step 80%+ of the clocked minutes; no milestone for 45 min; one answer to one question 15+ times and two thirds of its answers; none good in 20%+ of Jev's answers; at a fortress 10+ min with under 25% new ground or a last pass reaching under half its stretches; no frame for 3 min). Its first run on the thirteen live trials (2.5 seconds) flagged mid-242-ac-nether-1 at its fortress 16 min, 0 of 18 columns new, 697 blocks walked for 21 net, 2 of 5 stretches reached, fortress_leg answered 206 times; mid-242-ad-nether-1 answering stay_in_fortress 68 times in six minutes; mid-243-ab pacing 693 blocks within three of one spot on search food; mid-242-ab's portal_method 405 times cast_frame. Test: a fixture fortress trial pacing ground it walked before, digging, asked the same leg twenty times, is flagged on each count with the verdict's words; one covering new ground past a milestone is not.

557. *mid-242-ac-nether-1, "flipping find_fortress" on its fortress's floor, and mid-242-ad-nether-1, "flipping cross_toward <-> find_fortress" below it (the user: "keeps coming back to a fortress wall in the middle of the lava, looking at it, walking away, coming back").* mid-242-ac-nether-1 stood on a floor at (-122.5, 72, 106.5) walled by a lava fall, its fortress set aside eight minutes before (Jev's keep_searching on the way to one stretch set aside the whole fortress), blazes seen near it forty times, no pickaxe. fortress_leg was asked every three seconds for fifteen minutes, leg_east 49 times and leg_north 44 in the last five: each leg made a block or two to the lava's edge, every way on failed (no route, "lava in the way", the staircase resting "no tool for nether bricks"), the leg's tick was tried again a thousand times from the same spot until the stall's loose ends dropped its target, and the next ask chose it again. The hunt was not running because a blaze claims it only in view; the hunt walks back to a sighting four times (rememberedSpot) and after that only the search ran, the forty sightings one field among the fortress's facts. mid-242-ad-nether-1's legs at (-105, 43, 106) were the same shape. Now a leg whose every way fails within eight blocks of where it began ends there and its heading rests from that spot for five minutes (legRests): not offered from there, said in the state (legsResting: how far it got, why), offered again from elsewhere or after; with every way from a spot resting, the step throws and the stall's own question answers it. Each leg says its first sixteen cells in order (rock, a floor, no floor, lava a block down, then what stops it).
