The blazes lead the question: blazesSeen is the first fact (how many times, where, how lately, how many were in sight and how many heard through the walls, the hunt's walks back and how the last ended), and go_to_blazes is a fortress_leg option: walked on foot (skills.navigate onFoot: no digging, no laying) and, where that finds no way, the way there asked (fortress_approach, state.stretch "where blazes were seen"). The pickaxe carried is a fact. On a fortress's floors a way that is left is left, not the fortress: fortress_approach for a stretch or a place offers other_way instead of keep_searching; and a fortress Jev left over its ways in is offered back (back_to_fortress) from its own floors, where going back walks them rather than asking those ways again. A floor under lava is no floor (fortressFloors). Test: the ac state (a floor ringed by lava at the feet, the fortress set aside from that spot, forty sightings, no pickaxe): blazesSeen leads, back_to_fortress and go_to_blazes are offered, leg_east ends two blocks along and rests (asked once more, not every tick, and not offered), go_to_blazes walks on foot and asks the way with other_way, and the fortress is not set aside again; fails without the change. npm test 1758 of 1758 (shadow arbiter the same); replay suite 34 of 35 and the known gap.
561. *mid-244-ad-nether-2, "was building a bridge and then just stopped, and is now walking back and forth".* At 02:34:01 its leg north ended on its own cobblestone span over the lava sea at (6.5, 73, -74), 61 blocks carried and every leg needing 87 to 96 laid ("the blocks run out at cell 61", as each leg said). Jev chose restock_blocks, told "Mine 64 netherrack ... the nearest 10 blocks off, about 21 seconds ... 288 netherrack, 23 cobblestone" within sixteen. The nearest netherrack was across the drop. Nothing on foot from the span was counted apart, and each restock tick was one acquireStep: a mine step for the nearest netherrack within 48, which walked back along the span toward it, found no dry place to stand and dig from, and went exploring. Three things kept it there. The restock was looked at only within eight blocks of the leg's end, so the tick after a mine step that had carried the bot ten blocks off went to the leg, which walked it back to the span's end: mine netherrack, tunnel, mine netherrack, tunnel, ten seconds each, and no netherrack gained (still 61 carried). At twice the 21 seconds said, netherrack was set aside, and the next offer was the span itself: "Mine 36 cobblestone, the nearest 1 blocks off". It began digging the blocks at y 72 under its own walk (5, 72, -74) and (1, 72, -70). Now the restock offers only what can be dug from ground walked to from where the bot stands (bridging.js spanBlockSources): a walk of up to 32 steps from its cell, up, level or down, no digging, placing or jumping a gap, within 16 blocks. A block counts when a cell of that walk has it within reach of the eye, it has an open face, no lava is beside it, and there is something under it for its drop to land on. A floor with open air under it (a span, its own among them) is never counted. The option is priced with how many to dig (what the longest leg short of blocks needs past those carried, as far as can be had here), the kinds, the nearest and the walk to it, and the seconds (the walk, then a dig and a step a block). It also says what lies within sixteen out of reach and why, and what the last restock gained. Chosen, the restock is gathered in one go (bridging.js gatherSpanBlocks, one block after another through work.js mine with the block chosen, walked to first) until the count is carried, nothing more is to be had here, three blocks in a row give nothing, or twice the time said runs out. A restock held when a threat breaks it off is taken up again wherever the bot stands, before the leg. The leg is then chosen again from the span's end. Tests: at the end of a 21-block cobblestone span, with an outcrop beside it and an island across the drop, 61 carried, the option says "Dig 35 blocks ... from the 53 that can be dug from ground walked to from here (53 netherrack) ... 35 short ... not to be dug ...: 514 netherrack, 17 cobblestone". Chosen, it digs all 35 from the outcrop in one tick, each from where the bot stands, with no walk toward the leg's end, and a held restock ten blocks from the leg's end goes on. The basalt ledge test now digs its basalt from the ledge. Both fail without the fix (the first on the option, the second on the leg taking the tick). npm test 1760 of 1760, and the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow. Still open: when nothing within 16 can be had on foot, a short span to a face in reach (the blocks carried spent to reach more) is not offered; only return_for_blocks is.
562. *A tool: the trials drawn, so what a person sees at a glance is seen by us too.* The audit (note 558) counts; it cannot show the bot walking back and forth on a bridge, pillared up out of a piglin's reach, or circling a fortress wall, which the user spots in a second of watching. `node scripts/trials/trail-map.js [auto | port ...] [--minutes 15] [--history 60] [--out dir]` writes one PNG a trial (default artifacts/trails/<world>-<time>Z.png): a top-down map of the ground the window's walk covered, read-only from the server's own saved region files (a small NBT and palette reader, no new dependency), cut at the walk's usual feet height, each column the first block below head height (walls bright, the floor plain, ground farther down dimmer and hatched as a drop, lava and water keeping their hue at depth, open air black, chunks not in the files checkered); a side view below it along the walk's long axis, down to the ground under the walk (the lava under a bridge, the grass under a pillar); the path colored by what the run clock says it was on (the step without its rung, or the survival stance), a dot a minute labeled with the minutes before the end (minutes on one spot share one label, "-1,5,13"), start, end and deaths marked, and the mobs known at their last positions, lettered by kind, filled when in sight; and a header of the audit's own measures and flags (progress-audit.js now exports its window reader, trialWindow). A pure-JS PNG writer and a 5×8 bitmap font; thirteen live trials drew in 1.5 to 2.5 seconds. The region files are what the server last saved, so a span or pillar laid in the last minutes may be missing under the path. `--view` (a first-person render) is not built: prismarine-viewer and its headless renderer are not installed and are large native dependencies. The first run showed at a glance what the text needed paragraphs for: mid-243-ab (25598) standing on a pillar stub at y 121, twenty blocks over the treetops, 408 blocks walked within three of one spot on descend pillar and search food; mid-244-ad-nether-2 (25590) going back and forth along a bridge at y 74 over a lava cavern, minutes 7 to 11 on one stretch of it, answering fortress_leg return_for_blocks 28 times; mid-242-aa-fortress-2 (25585) out of a fortress corridor onto a span over a forty-block drop and stopped at its end on errand persist for four minutes. Test: a section's packed indices decode; a region file written by hand reads back lava, air, and unknown for a chunk or region not saved; the PNG inflates back to its pixels; positions take the run clock's label, else the step; a trial with no saved world still draws its walk, mob and death.

559. *Three wither skeleton deaths at fortresses in forty minutes: mid-242-aa (25581, 02:14:17), mid-242-ac-nether-1-fortress-1 (25590, 02:31:28) and mid-208-k-nether-3-fortress-2 (25584, 02:44:30).* mid-242-aa had died to piglins at 01:27 in an iron helmet and chestplate, thirteen blocks from its Nether portal at (-5, 48, 38); it came back through that portal at about 01:55 in no armor with a stone sword, and at 02:14, at 20 health in a tunnel at y 73 beside a fortress floor at 74, three wither skeletons about, chose the pillar at 0.65. The pillar did go up (73 to 75, 02:14:14.0 to 14.8), and its option said three times that a wither skeleton reaches a player two up; it was priced at 16.3 against the fight's 23.8, and it was the fight: the top was one above the floor round the column, the skeleton walked in and struck twice in 0.85 seconds, 20 to 12 to 5 (8 a blow, nothing worn), the first blow moving the bot 0.8 of a block and the second putting it off the top, and the skeleton stepped up onto it. Read from the 26.1.2 jar: a mob's melee range is its own box widened sqrt(2.04) - 0.6 (about 0.83) to the sides and not at all upward (Mob.isWithinMeleeAttackRange, getAttackBoundingBox), so a blow reaches as high as the mob stands (EntityType sized: a wither skeleton 0.7 by 2.4, a zombie or a piglin 1.95, a skeleton 1.99, a hoglin 1.4, an enderman 2.9); a blow knocks a player back 0.4 a tick with a hop (LivingEntity.knockback); and a skeleton kind with a blade chases at 1.2 times its speed (AbstractSkeleton's meleeGoal, MeleeAttackGoal(this, 1.2, false)), 3.9 blocks a second, not 2.7. Three rules had it wrong. The pillar measured "two up" from the bot's floor: a mob on higher ground counted only within five blocks (the ledge rule), so a floor one up seven blocks off was none. Its fight from the top was priced with no approach, where the fight here has its closing (note 550), so the same blade read seven cheaper. And the way back for the drops (corpse-run.js) waited for the kit before it asked: no kit, no question, so the kit's own way back was shut until the kit was made again, and the list of what to fetch was the pockets only, the armor worn never on it. mid-242-ac-nether-1-fortress-1, in iron helmet and chestplate with an iron sword: a wither skeleton came round a fortress corner, unseen at 3.6 blocks; nothing counted it a threat (an unseen biter was one only by a deadly edge), the stance's back_to_wall read "about 0 damage ... none of them reaches it" (the blaze stands were priced with the mobs in sight only), the survival claim lapsed, turn_priority offered the meal and work, Jev answered "none of these" at 0.45, the meal was taken, and the first blow took 6.7. It then ran three times at 6.4 health, told the skeleton would be about 10 blocks behind; at 3.9 a second it was at the bot again each time while the wither ticked, 6.4 to 2.4, and a blow finished it. mid-208-k-nether-3-fortress-2, in iron with golden boots and a shield: the pillar at 0.42 (priced 35.6 against the fight's 66.2 with two skeletons), up two, and held facing a blaze twenty blocks off (the held pillar faces the shooter, note 395) while the skeleton three blocks behind on the floor below struck it, 20 to 15.2, and knocked it off; then eat at 0.29 (priced at 13.1 while it eats, and hit for about 10), back_to_wall, and a fireball at 0.8. Now: the heights are the jar's (combat-estimate BODY_HEIGHT); the pillar reads the ground round its column (survival.js groundBeside, the eight cells from the base to the top) and counts every walker whose ground and height are over the top as reaching it, wherever it stands now, and says so ("The ground beside the pillar's column is 1 up ... two up is one above it ... one there steps up onto the top once the bot is off its middle"); a mob on a ledge within five reaches by its height, not by standing within 1.2 of the top; the walkers that reach are fought from the top as the climbers are, and that fight is priced as the fight here meets it, its closing counted, begun once the blocks are down (stanceCost fight.lead): the first death's pillar reads 23 against the fight's 23.8; where every biter about reaches the top the option opens with it ("Here two up is no cover from any of the mobs that bite ... the fight up there is the fight here, begun once the blocks are down, on a top one block wide") in place of the rule that walkers of a player's height cannot reach two up, and a blow that knocks the bot off a top one block wide is said. A held stance faces a biter at arm's length whose blow reaches where the bot stands before a shot or a shooter (survival.js shieldFacing, the guard's own meleeClose rule); a spider at a pillar's foot still does not turn it from the shooter. The chase is the jar's: a wither skeleton 3.9 a second (combat-estimate CHASE), which note 550's runs measured (8.6 to 9.2 behind, told 12 to 15), and a run says the health the wither leaves when the first chaser is at the bot again. A walker out of sight within five with a way to the bot (walk-reach, which now judges the wither skeleton and reads how far below a tall mob touches from its height) is a threat and keeps the meal off (danger.js unseenClose, vitals.js closeHostile); the blaze stands are priced with the unseen biters that have a way, as every other stance is (note 542). The death records what was worn and in the off hand (recovery.js), the way back lists it, the question is asked in the drops' dimension whatever is worn, the drops' clock runs from when the bot comes near whether it goes or not, and the kit or daylight is only the fallback's rule. Replay case wither-skeleton-pillar-ground-one-up (mid-242-aa's question re-rendered, the ground beside's cell inferred from the skeleton's step onto the top): pillar 5 of 5 with the ground and the knock said and the price at 20.9 (the closing less the building's seconds), pillar 4 and retreat 1 with it at 23, fight 4 and retreat 1 with the opening. Tests: the ground one up beside the column (the zombie seven off reaches, said and priced); a wither skeleton's pillar priced as the fight; the held pillar facing the wither skeleton, not the blaze; the stands priced with an unseen biter; an unseen wither skeleton round a corner a threat and no meal, walled apart neither; the chase at 3.9 and the wither at the next meeting; the worn armor on the way back and the question asked unarmored in the Nether; each fails without its change. npm test and JEV_ARBITER=shadow npm test 1764 of 1764; replay suite 35 of 36 and the known gap. Still open: the fight's closing (PACE.closePerBlock, leadFor) is still the old charge's, not refitted after note 555, and the pillar now shares it; a block in a walker's path (a wall two high between, as block_creeper does for a creeper) is not offered against a wither skeleton; partial iron armor (a chestplate first) is not a rung of its own, only the full set; which way the bot faces while it walks to a stand or eats is unchanged.
560. *The same question, the same answer, and nothing came of it (the progress audit of note 558, and the user's 25590).* The audit's worst: mid-242-ab answered portal_method cast_frame 532 times in fifteen minutes with no milestone in 98; mid-243-ab answered survival_priority 222 times for the same walk to a sheep, 693 blocks walked, never three from where it began; mid-242-aa-nether-1-fortress-1 "asked" body_way forty times with one option, burn_out; mid-244-ab answered night_mine_target ore_0 65 times, a walk toward it every two seconds; mid-244-ad-nether-2 chose the pillar every fifteen seconds for minutes on its own bridge while a sword piglin stood on the slope below; mid-208-k-nether-3-fortress-2 was asked shield_policy five times in eight seconds under a wither skeleton's blows. Each answer returned at once or did nothing, and the step asked again, told nothing of the answer before. The causes, one by one. mid-242-ab: nine of ten cast, a cobblestone stood on top of the last slot (18, 75, 58), the one way in for the lava; no stand could see into the slot, "Nowhere to stand to pour" 3,463 times (the saved world, read). The cast now digs out whatever stands over the slot before it looks for a stand (clear_top), and no stand's anchor is put there. mid-242-aa: burn_out was held only as Jev's choice (note 549), so as the only way it was not held, and every step took it again; the one way is held as a choice is. mid-208-k: the shield policy counted the shooters in sight when chosen, so a blaze coming out from behind cover was "new", and every blow's four health ended it though it was the shield at the shots; now every shooter about is counted, seen or not, a new one is a kind not counted or more of a kind than were about, and the health falling asks again only where the choice was to take the shots. mid-244-ad-nether-2: the bridge ran thirty blocks back, the walk-reach search (note 525) left its twelve-block bounds along it and so said nothing, and every stance priced the piglin at the bot; the search now stops at its bounds and says what it did not reach there as having no way within twelve blocks, any way round being at least so many blocks of walking (17 here), a piglin said with what it holds (a sword hits at arm's length only; a crossbow piglin shoots and is no walker); the fact leads the option to carry on, which names the work, and every other stance says the work waits and how long it has waited for these mobs. mid-243-ab stood on a tower of its own blocks twenty over the canopy and every survival walk found no route (the tower itself is not settled here); mid-244-ab's night mine walked a block or two toward its ore and asked again. mid-244-ae's turn_priority 24 of 24 was the minute's ruling asked again in a shelter at night, survival each time: not a loop. And the general rule, in decide (decisions/index.js, repeats.js). A question with one option is not asked: the one way is taken, said once in the log ("[one way]"), and not recorded as a decision, nor does it take the turn. Every question about the game is remembered by a fingerprint of its facts (its state and options, clocks left out and the numbers in words set aside, so "failed 3,466 times" is the fact "failed 3,467 times" was) and its answer; asked again with the same facts after the same answer, with nothing measurable between (moved over three blocks, what is carried changed, a block dug or placed, the stall watch's progress), the next asking says so in its facts (sameAnswerAgain: "cast frame was chosen 2 times in the last 1 second with these same facts, and nothing measurable came of any of them ...; each came back within a second: Nowhere to stand to pour into the frame slot at (18, 75, 58)", the failure from the work's own record, lastFailure, or a survival walk's). When it came back within five seconds twice running, it is not asked a third time: it is held as failed, said to every question for two minutes after (answersThatCameToNothing, the detour's too), and a stall is raised for the step in hand, which the loop answers as any stall (another way, a detour, the rung left for later). A wait chosen (stillness.js HOLDS: a shelter held, a pillar up top) and an emergency (EMERGENCIES) are said, never held, and so are turn_priority and stillness_detour, the routing and the stall path themselves. Tests: ruined-portal.test.js, a cobblestone over the last slot is dug and the tenth block cast; body.test.js, burn_out as the one way is not asked and is held, and the shield chosen holds through ten health of blows and asks again at a third blaze; decisions.test.js, a one-option question is not asked or recorded, the same answer to the same facts is said at the second asking and held at the third with the stall on the step, and moving on, new facts or a wait start afresh or are only said; survival.test.js, a sword piglin on the slope with no way onto a short bridge (sure) and a long one (a way round of 17 or more) leads the option to carry on, every stance says the crossing waits, and a crossbow piglin is said as no walker. Replay case bridge-sword-piglin-cannot-get-up, from the recorded 02:38:19 question: the recorded text chose the pillar 5 of 5; told as now, fight_from_footing 4 of 5 and the pillar once, then 5 of 5 in the suite (expected: carry on or the firm ground; forbidden: the pillar, the hold, the pocket). Jev still does not take the crossing while the piglin stands there, told it has no way within twelve blocks: open. Suite 35 of 36 with the known gap.

557. *mid-242-ac-nether-1, "flipping find_fortress" on its fortress's floor, and mid-242-ad-nether-1, "flipping cross_toward <-> find_fortress" below it (the user: "keeps coming back to a fortress wall in the middle of the lava, looking at it, walking away, coming back").* mid-242-ac-nether-1 stood on a floor at (-122.5, 72, 106.5) walled by a lava fall, its fortress set aside eight minutes before (Jev's keep_searching on the way to one stretch set aside the whole fortress), blazes seen near it forty times, no pickaxe. fortress_leg was asked every three seconds for fifteen minutes, leg_east 49 times and leg_north 44 in the last five: each leg made a block or two to the lava's edge, every way on failed (no route, "lava in the way", the staircase resting "no tool for nether bricks"), the leg's tick was tried again a thousand times from the same spot until the stall's loose ends dropped its target, and the next ask chose it again. The hunt was not running because a blaze claims it only in view; the hunt walks back to a sighting four times (rememberedSpot) and after that only the search ran, the forty sightings one field among the fortress's facts. mid-242-ad-nether-1's legs at (-105, 43, 106) were the same shape. Now a leg whose every way fails within eight blocks of where it began ends there and its heading rests from that spot for five minutes (legRests): not offered from there, said in the state (legsResting: how far it got, why), offered again from elsewhere or after; with every way from a spot resting, the step throws and the stall's own question answers it. Each leg says its first sixteen cells in order (rock, a floor, no floor, lava a block down, then what stops it). The blazes lead the question: blazesSeen is the first fact (how many times, where, how lately, how many were in sight and how many heard through the walls, the hunt's walks back and how the last ended), and go_to_blazes is a fortress_leg option: walked on foot (skills.navigate onFoot: no digging, no laying) and, where that finds no way, the way there asked (fortress_approach, state.stretch "where blazes were seen"). The pickaxe carried is a fact. On a fortress's floors a way that is left is left, not the fortress: fortress_approach for a stretch or a place offers other_way instead of keep_searching; and a fortress Jev left over its ways in is offered back (back_to_fortress) from its own floors, where going back walks them rather than asking those ways again. A floor under lava is no floor (fortressFloors). Test: the ac state (a floor ringed by lava at the feet, the fortress set aside from that spot, forty sightings, no pickaxe): blazesSeen leads, back_to_fortress and go_to_blazes are offered, leg_east ends two blocks along and rests (asked once more, not every tick, and not offered), go_to_blazes walks on foot and asks the way with other_way, and the fortress is not set aside again; fails without the change. npm test 1758 of 1758 (shadow arbiter the same); replay suite 34 of 35 and the known gap. Second, mid-242-aa-fortress-1 (the user: "walking back and forth and randomly digging instead of actually trying to map out the fortress", "where are the blazes", "now it left the fortress"): eleven minutes and ten passes on its fortress's floor at (-122, 66, 104), each pass setting out for five or six "stretches" picked from every nether brick within 128 blocks with two air over it, seen or not; two reached, the rest "No path to the goal!" and, the way asked, staircases dug straight through the walls (phase tunnel); no blaze seen, stay_in_fortress every ten to fifteen seconds, then combat_kit and return_for_kit, and it left. Now the bot keeps a map of the fortress as a player sees it (src/fortress-map.js, goal.fortressSearch.map): a floor is a nether brick, stair or slab with two blocks of air over it (lava is not air), added only when a line from the eyes reaches its top through open air; each keeps when it was walked (within two blocks of where the bot stood) and which of its sides are still unseen. A side is settled by a floor seen there (a step up or down included), a wall at the feet's height, or ground looked at; lava on the floor or the floor's edge over open air stays a way on until the edge is walked, since what lies past it is seen from there. A way on (the frontier) is a seen floor not walked with a side unseen. On its floors the bot walks, on foot (no digging, no laying), to the nearest way on joined to it by seen floors; a walk that fails is passed over five minutes with its why. With none left the choice is Jev's (fortress_leg) with the map said (fortressInView.map: floors seen and walked, how many joined to where it stands, ways on foot, spawners seen and whether floors join them, stairs seen, the nether wart room, chests, ways that failed): stay_in_fortress (walk its corridors again for three minutes, the least lately walked first, offered only when there are some twelve steps off), unwalked_1 to 3 (floors seen that no seen floor joins to here, each with the nearest crossing and what lies between: lava, open air with no floor, wall or rock; walked on foot first, then fortress_approach with other_way), wait_at_spawner (only a spawner seen through open air, never one behind a wall; the walk there on foot, the way asked once where it finds none), go_to_blazes, and the legs. Checked against the saved world (.trial-checkpoints/stages/fortress/mid-242-aa-020157, the region read and walked in simulation with fortress-map.js): the bot's stretch of corridor (x -128 to -98, z 100 to 108, about 120 floors) is cut off from the rest of the fortress (3,458 floors, both spawners) by a lava fall down a stair onto the corridor's floor at x -137 to -129 and by netherrack filling the parallel corridor's mouth at x -113 to -104; with the lava crossed, the spawner at (-108, 77, 155) is 184 steps of corridor away. The map walks the stretch, sees pieces of the corridor on past the lava (over the stair the lava falls down) and the nether wart room, and offers the crossings with what lies between; the old patrol's targets were floors behind those walls. Tests: a corridor walked on foot to its open end and the question asked once with the map (stay, then a patrol back to the least lately walked, then a leg; what was walked kept); a spawner behind a wall not known, and through a window offered as a wait walked on foot; floors across a gap of two offered as unwalked_1 with "2 of open air with no floor", the walk failing and the span chosen reaching them; each fails without the change. npm test 1757 of 1757, shadow arbiter the same; replay suite 34 of 35 and the known gap (one run 33, a live-model case). Still open: crossing lava that lies on a floor (a causeway of blocks laid into it) is no way of its own yet, and in the saved world the stretch reaches the spawners only across that lava fall or through the netherrack. Third, mid-208-k-nether-3-fortress-2 (the user: "saw the blazes, ran around a lot"): two blazes sixteen to twenty-four blocks off, both out of sight, and hunt_target asked four times; Jev chose defer each time (0.82 to 0.84). Each blaze was offered as a JSON record ("action": "Fight this observed isolated mob ...", "fight": {"hitsBot": 2.7, ...}) beside a worded defer. The stands (dig_in_and_fight, fight_at_spawner, back_to_wall) were offered only with a blaze in sight, so the only way to fight was the open one, priced at 13.4 damage to 6.6 health after, with riskNow's fight with both at 30.4. Now each fight is a sentence (huntSays): the mob, where and how far up, whether it is in sight or only heard, how it is fought with what is carried, that a blaze drops a rod about half the time and rods are what the request needs, its price alone and with the others, and what is beside it. The state carries blazes (how many, in sight or heard, their heights against the bot, a spawner seen on the fortress's map, the rods still needed and that nothing else gives them) beside the resource. The stands are offered with blazes within twenty-four whether seen or heard. Probed against the live model with the recorded state: the worded fight alone, defer 4 of 5 (the JSON, defer 5 of 5), which is the price speaking, since 13.4 of 20 health in the open with a second blaze about is a poor trade; with a wall stand beside it at a stand's price (7 damage, hypothetical), back_to_wall 5 of 5, and at 12 damage 4 of 5. The price is honest; what makes the bot fight is a stand being offered, which now it is. Test: two blazes out of sight behind a wall, a netherrack wall at the bot's back: the fight is a sentence saying out of sight, what it drops and why, the blazes fact is in the state, and back_to_wall is offered; fails without the change. npm test 1758 of 1758, shadow arbiter the same; replay suite 34 of 35 and the known gap.
566. *A mob that cannot get to the bot is no encounter (note 560's open end, mid-244-ad-nether-2 on port 25590).* On its own one-wide bridge, a golden-sword piglin on the slope below with no way onto it, the stance was asked every fifteen seconds for minutes; told as note 560 left it, the recorded question took "step to firm ground and fight there" 4 or 5 of 5 and never carry on. Why, by swapping the old and new text a part at a time (5 asks each): all of it as recorded, fight_from_footing 5 of 5; carry-on's text alone as now, keep_working 5 of 5; the state alone as now, keep_working 5 of 5; the other stances alone as now, keep_working 4 of 5 and the pillar once. Carry-on was priced by the piglin ("nearest 8 blocks"), said the drop's knock, and said nothing of the crossing; the state listed the piglin among the threats and riskNow said "fighting all here: 8.2 damage"; the firm ground said nothing of whether the piglin could get there, "the mobs hitting freely meanwhile" with none that could; and the fight, the pocket and the bow priced it walking up ("at the bot in about 1 seconds", "58.1 damage"), because a walker whose search left the twelve-block bounds (the "any way round, 17 or more" of note 560) was said apart but left in the figures. Now: a walker with no way to the bot, by walk-reach, sure or only round past the bounds, is not a threat while it stays so (danger.js immediateThreat, cannotGetToTheBot; the search kept half a second, noWayIds), unless it hit the bot in the last five seconds; a shooter is never judged (a crossbow piglin is one). So the stance is not asked for it, and the work goes on. Asked for another mob, the stance lists such walkers apart: in the state as cannotGetToTheBot (not among the threats), and left out of every figure, the round ones as the sure ones (walk-reach ids), riskNow too ("low: none of the mobs about has a way to the bot, and none shoots", cannotGetToTheBot, a fight of 0). A walker outside the search's bounds is no longer said to have no way: it was, a zombie fourteen blocks off on open ground told "any way it has goes round, 10 blocks or more". Carry-on is priced by the others' real reach: the nearest that can get to the bot and how soon it is at it at its own speed, or "none of the mobs here can get to the bot, and none of them shoots: nothing here stops the work while that holds"; and it says where the work stands (survival.js workProgress: the target and how far, the stretch's blocks to lay and those carried, and by the stall watch's record whether it was making headway when the mobs stopped it). The firm ground judges the walkers apart again from the ground it steps to (walkersApart's at): "has no way to that ground either: nothing here comes to be fought there", or "is not kept from that ground (a way there, or none the search can rule out): stepping there can put the bot where it can get to it". The bow says one with no way "does not come on meanwhile". Tests: danger.test.js, the sword piglin on the slope below a short bridge (sure) and a long one (round) is no threat, is one once it has hit the bot, as a crossbow piglin, or with a ledge up beside the bridge, and riskNow leaves it out; a zombie fourteen blocks off on open ground is not said round; survival.test.js, carry-on says where the crossing stands and that nothing can get to the bot, not "nearest 8", the state lists the piglin apart with a fight of 0, and the firm ground says whether the piglin can get there, across the valley or on a slope level with the bridge. All fail without the change. npm test 1783 of 1783, the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow. Replay case bridge-sword-piglin-cannot-get-up told as now (the firm ground's reach is not in the record, said as the search's unsure answer): keep_working 5 of 5, now its only expected answer (forbidden: the pillar, the hold, the pocket, the rail, the retreat). Suite 36 of 37 with the known gap. Still open: what can push the bot (danger.js pushersAbout: the span's hold and the Nether pathfinder's edge refusal) still counts a walker with no way within eight blocks; the fight chosen with none that can get to the bot still charges the nearest; none of this has run live.

567. *mid-242-ab (25594), "walked 982 blocks, net 0" for ten minutes in a one-wide water pit at (18, 71, 59), unstuck_move 51 times (26 climb_west), body_way 12 (11 straight_up), no milestone in 136 minutes.* The pit was no trap: every climb out worked (climb_west put the bot on the cobblestone at (16, 72, 59) each time, reach_shore on the grass at (16, 71, 61)), and the work walked it straight back in, every two seconds from 02:45 to 03:09. The portal was lit (note 560's fix cast the last slot at 02:35), and its south face stood over water. The saved world: one water source, (19, 76, 58), on the dirt at the frame's top corner, the water poured on the last slot's lava; running down past the portal's face it filled the one-deep hole before the portal at (17, 70, 59) (dug before 02:16, dry then) and the cells over it, so the doorway cell (17, 72, 59) had water under it and no floor. The saved goal: castWater (19, 76, 58), castWaterError "Water source is outside visible interaction reach", two empty buckets carried. The cast takes its water back at the start of each slot and after each pour; the last scoop failed, and at the next pass the frame was whole, the loop over the slots skipped all ten, and nothing ever came to the scoop again. Then the crossing: enterPortal's walk to the sheet (GoalNear, one block) ended afloat at (17, 72.2, 59.7), its step in was crouched, and a crouched swimmer sinks: to the bottom of the hole, "Could not step into the portal", the staircase thrown to at once and resting ("paced the same few cells"), reach_shore out onto the bank, and the crossing again. The water column itself was never the problem: it reaches the air (water at 70 and 71, air at 72), and swim_up and the climbs out were offered and did what they said; a place into the water and a dig beside were offered too. A bucket on the source was not, and could not have been from the pit (5.3 blocks up and over, past reach), and the water in the pit was running, not a source. Now the cast leaves no water: once the frame is whole its sources are taken back (leaveNoWater, portal-cast.js), each scooped, or where the scoop cannot be made, filled with one of the cast's temporary blocks as a slot's feeder is; tried on three passes, then kept with why (castWaterStanding). An earlier pour's source not taken back is kept under the next (castWaterLeft), not forgotten. A lit cast frame with its water still standing (a restart between the pour and the scoop, or this trial's saved state) has it taken back before the crossing steps in. And the step into a portal from water is a swimmer's climb out, forward and jump, upright (enterPortal): in the water, bobbing above it or in a bubble column. No question changed. Tests: ruined-portal.test.js, a whole frame with the last slot's water recorded takes it back, scooped with a bucket and filled with cobblestone without one, and an earlier source kept under the next is taken back with it; expedition.test.js, afloat before a portal whose floor is a block up, the step in climbs out of the water into the sheet (fails without the change: "Could not step into the portal"). npm test 1783 of 1783, shadow arbiter the same. Not done: the pathfinder's GoalNear still takes a node over water as the sheet's face (the climb now carries it the rest of the way), and the cast's recorded walls (castTemp) still list cells since dug away, (17, 71, 59) and (18, 71, 59) among them.
563. *mid-243-ad, "tried to swim in lava" at 03:00:48 (port 25586, the Nether): off its own span by a ghast's fireball, twenty-two blocks into the lava sea.* The bot stood on its own one-wide netherrack span at (190.5, 54, 118.3), crossing toward a fortress leg with the lava sea at y 31 under it, a ghast thirty-seven to fifty-eight blocks off; it carried three oak planks, five oak logs, sixteen gravel and two crafting tables. At 03:00:20 the turn went to survival (0.69) and the stance to return_fireball (0.61); hold_on_span was offered as "too few blocks carried (3) to wall the 2 open sides (4)", the logs not counted. Between return_fireball's watches the flee step's span branch ran the code's own hold in its place (a stance held skipped the question, and the span branch came next), four turns in twelve seconds, the stall watch's "turning between return fireball and hold on span"; that hold found no wall to put (railSpan also wanted one stack of two a side) and no firm ground within eight, and stood. return_fireball again at 03:00:32 (0.70); take_cover at 03:00:35 (0.48), told none of the blocks carried holds, put the last three planks in the ghast's line. At 03:00:36 the stance was asked again with the ghast out of sight behind them: fight, keep_working and a retreat with no way out; no hold_on_span (with the planks beside it the span was not one-wide at the feet), no rail (0 blocks counted). keep_working (0.56) said "a hit's knockback or a step back over it is into lava", the state a drop of 22 "damage 19" from 20 health (lava breaks a fall; the fall does nothing and the lava is the cost), and the hidden ghast's fireballs were priced at nothing: only shooters in sight were counted, and nothing said that one fireball landing was the fall, or what the fall was. The work walked the bot 0.8 of a block, out from behind the planks; four fortress_leg answers, a portal_way and a stillness_detour came in the next four seconds (none of these on top in three), and at 03:00:42 the ghast had a line and its fireball (20 to 16.6) threw the bot four blocks east and twenty-one down. In the lava each hurt was 4 through the iron to 2.1, twice a second: 16.6 to none in four seconds. body_way offered one way, back_the_way_came, twenty-seven blocks: nothing out of the lava within six (lavaExit), and nothing else was carried that could have saved it (no fire resistance, no enchanted golden apple, no ender pearl; a water bucket, which the Nether boils away; logs, gravel and tables, with no block to place against in the open lava). The death was settled on the span. Now: a drop into lava is priced by the lava, not the fall (terrain.js lavaFate): the nearest ground from where the body comes up, at the lava's surface or a block under it within twelve blocks, at roughly a block a second swimming, and the lava's 4 a half second through the armour worn; none, or more health than the bot has, is death, and the stance's dropWithinThreeBlocks says so (damage "death", its figures, dropFacts), as does the drop's sentence on every stance (dropNote). With a shooter that can push the bot over a drop of half its health or more, one in sight within its reach, or a ghast or a blaze out of it within its reach (it flies, and has a line again at any moment), every stance that leaves the bot open says one shot that lands is that fall, priced by it and not by the shot's own damage ("the bot's death, and everything carried lost with it"), keep_working adding that the hit that would stop the work is the one that throws the bot over; the walled stances (rail_and_fight, the span's hold with its walls) say that walled, a shot that lands costs its damage and a push into the wall (survival.js shotOverEdge). The span's hold walls for such a flyer out of sight too, in the option and in holdOnSpan, and says the walls stay up while it holds. The planks the logs and stems carried make count for the span's walls and the rail, said as made first (about a second), and railSpan makes them (shelter.js plankCraft) and asks only the blocks the open sides need. And on a span a stance Jev chose and that holds is carried out, not the span's hold in its place (flee). Tests (test/span-fall.test.js, each failing on the old code): the lava sea under the span priced as death with no shore, 12.6 health with ground three blocks from where the body comes up; with the ghast out of sight, keep_working and the fight say the fall and death, the hold and the rail do not and say the walled price, the hold walls for it; three planks and five logs wall the span and the rail, and railSpan makes one log's planks and places four; a held take_cover on a span runs, not the code's hold. Replay case span-ghast-hidden-keep-working, the 03:00:36 question with its texts as the fixes give them (the rail offered from the logs): the recorded text chose keep_working 5 of 5; the fall said on the open stances alone left it split among the work, the fight and none of these; with the walled price beside it, rail_and_fight 10 of 10, and 5 of 5 in the suite (expected: the rail; forbidden: the work, the fight). Suite 37 of 38 with the known gap; npm test 1784 of 1784, shadow arbiter the same. Open: planks are blast resistance 3, so the rail from them can be blown out by the fireball whose push it stops (said); after the rail the work still walks the open span, and a crossing that walls as it goes is not built; keep_working chosen is still not asked again when a flyer gets its line back (it says now that the hit comes too late); the lava's way out (lavaExit) still looks six blocks where the price looks twelve; and none of this has run live.

568. *mid-244-ad-nether-2 (25590), a one-wide span ninety blocks long at y 74 over a netherrack cavern whose floor was walkable fifteen to twenty below, piglins on it, return_for_blocks chosen 28 times; and mid-244-ad-nether-1 (25587), a diagonal cobblestone span at y 63 over a crimson forest walkable at y 32 to 45, then portal_way asked 95 times in ten minutes (47 around_right), none good 57 of 179, no new ground (the trail maps of note 562).* Every way on the Nether offered was a straight level line at the height the bot stood. A leg is its cells ahead at y N (surveyLeg) and its target at that height (fortressLegTarget); the crossing is at the feet's height (surveyCrossing, crossToward, run unasked after a walk fails); and the pathfinder, which the legs, portalLeg and portal_way's legs round all walk by, drops three at most (maxDropDown), digs no netherrack (blocksCantBreak), and in the Nether refuses the cells of a one-wide span while anything that can push is about, so from a span nothing it found went down. Going down to the floor was no option anywhere. The 95 portal_way askings: each around leg came back within a third of a second having moved nothing (sideLeg swallowed the walk's error, portalWay returned as if it went), "chosen from here before" was put on the last pick only, so the other looked fresh, and Jev answered right, left, right, left; with the answer and the facts changing at every asking, note 560's same-answer rule never saw a run of one answer to one set of facts. Now the floor below is a way of its own (nether-travel.js): floorBelow reads the ground under the sixty-four columns out to sixteen each way and, where eight or more show it four or more below the feet, takes its middle height; wayDown finds the way down to it within sixty-four across (a walk, a step up or level, a drop a body takes with its damage under half the health and no lip beside lava or a deadly drop at the landing, as the pathfinder lands, and with a pickaxe a step dug down into natural rock with nothing flowing behind it), the cheapest by seconds and a fall's damage weighed at four seconds a point, on a cached view of the world, capped at 8,000 cells (71 ms in a stubbed worst case); walkFloor follows the ground from the foot of it along a heading or the straight line to a target (a step up one or down three walked, soul sand at under half the pace, magma, rises of two to six climbed, walls dug, drops taken, lava on the floor and open air needing a block against those carried) with the mobs within eight of the ground walked. fortress_leg offers floor_<heading> where eight or more cells of floor lie that way, said with the way down, the floor and its first cells, the mobs, how the last such leg this way ended, and the fortress heights from down there; chosen, the pathfinder goes down the way found (its drops allowed as deep as the way's deepest, the rock of the steps down let dug, the way's own cells walked though an edge is beside them), the leg then goes on at the floor's height; a way down that does not get there ends the leg and that heading's floor rests from there (floor_ keys, kept apart from the level leg's history and rests). The stall's Nether answers offer floor_toward beside cross_toward (down, then a stretch of thirty-two on the floor toward the target, resting from that area when it came no lower and no nearer), and portal_way offers floor_way, said with the height back up to the portal at the end. portal_way keeps what each way chosen from a place came to: one that moved the bot under four blocks and no nearer is not offered from there again for five minutes in that rest and is said (triedFromHereToNothing, with the walk's own error); with every way so tried the way rests (WaysResting) and is not asked, and with the rest alone left it is the one way taken (note 560). Tests: on the span over the cavern (twelve blocks carried, a ramp down the ledge beside it, a lava pool and two piglins on the floor), floor_east reads "The way down to the floor 17 blocks below (y 57 ...) is ... dropping 3 (no damage) ... On the floor, of the 96 cells east: 93 of floor to walk, 3 of lava on the floor, all at y 57 ... 3 blocks laid, 12 carried: 9 left after ... By the floor that way: 2 piglins", chosen it goes down by the pathfinder with the drop allowance put back and walks the leg on the floor next tick, and the stall's answers offer floor_toward beside the crossing; the portal way from a span over a floor offers floor_way with "The portal is 19 blocks above the floor"; and around_right tried with no path is not offered at the next asking and is said, around_left likewise, and the third is not asked. Each fails without the change. Probed against the live model (the cavern, the span sixty out, twelve blocks carried, an iron pickaxe): floor_east 5 of 5, then 4 of 5 with leg_west once. npm test 1783 of 1783, the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow; replay suite 36 of 37 and the known gap. Still open: from the end of a span with no ground reachable within sixty-four (the ledge it started from farther back, nothing to drop onto within half the health) there is still no way down: a staircase of blocks laid down off a span (a block level, one under it, the first dug out, a block of height a step) is not built; the level crossing after a failed walk (crossToward in findFortressStep and walkToKnownPortal) is still taken unasked; and note 560's rule sees one answer's run only, so two answers alternating over changing facts escape it elsewhere too.

565. *mid-243-ab (25598), an hour on its own scattered tower twenty and more over the forest canopy, every survival walk "no route" (note 560 left it open).* It did not go up for a view, a night or a stance. The pillar stance at 01:57:57 and 01:58:12 (zombies, 0.51 and 0.42) raised it two at (23, 97, -26) and it came down to its bed at (23, 93, -31); it slept through the night at 01:58:51 and at 01:58:52 chose to walk to one sheep 92 blocks west (seen_food_0). In twenty-one seconds that walk went thirty-five blocks west over the canopy (leaves at 94 to 97) and twelve up: a staircase of dirt laid into the air from 97 to 102 at x -10 to -12, and a column on it to 105 at (-12, -34), over a hillside whose ground is 85 to 88 there. From the canopy's edge every way down fell nine or more: the drops were refused (maxDropDown three, and note 545's overFall), a block laid level off the edge was refused (note 545), the pathfinder never digs straight down under itself (getMoveDown is off) and cannot break cobblestone, and what the search had left were blocks laid to rise, a step laid beside and jumped onto (getMoveJumpUp, a placement with dy 1, which overFall does not look at) and a tower (getMoveUp). Each partial path climbed. For the next hour it was a saw: the work's descendPillar (the fallback inside the oak-log search, work.js) dug down a few blocks (113 to 105 at 01:59:19 to :38), and the next food walk climbed again (105 to 114, 111 to 119, 118 to 126), while survival sealed a shelter on the top each night: rings at 103 to 108, 113 to 120 (a furnace in it), 123 to 128 and 130 to 131 (the saved world, read). descend_pillar digs only over a solid block with two open sides, so the rings and the gaps under the column's scattered blocks refused it after a block or two, and from 02:20 the top only rose: y 130 at 03:07, 130 minutes in, no milestone for 129, survival_priority asked 410 times in fifteen minutes, forty-two minutes on "search food", four nights unslept and the phantoms come, an iron pickaxe with one use left, and a water bucket carried the whole time. No question named a way down: survival_priority offered the food and shelter walks, each "no route" at once; the stall's work_free (aim "off this spot") offered a pillar move, "rises 1", beside a single dig; descend_pillar is code's alone. Now (src/way-down.js): the top is read, the cells a walk reaches from the feet without digging or laying a block (level, a step up, a drop of three), a wall of the bot's own blocks dug through as a walk would, and each side's fall (perchOf); where the walk stays within six blocks and no way off comes down within three, it is a top. Before any walk off one to somewhere neither on it nor just above it (a portal overhead climbs as before), with no mob at hand, Jev is asked way_down (navigate, skills.js), with the ways that exist, each with its cost and where it ends: dig_down, the column underfoot a block at a time, never into a fall that hurts, with the blocks, seconds and pickaxe uses and the stone dug by hand once they run out, stopping on the block over a fall that would and saying so; ride_water, the water bucket poured at the feet so it runs off the open side and falls to the ground, a wall dug out first where the top is walled, the bot stepping off into it once the water is at the bottom, no fall damage, the bucket back empty; step_off, a side whose fall leaves more than a point of health, its damage said. Each says whether it ends where a walk goes on or still up on the tower, and the question is asked again from there, up to four times a walk, with what the ways chosen lately did. Without Jev, the way that comes off safely, else the one that gets lower safely, else the fall. Still on a top after, the walk builds no tower. And in the search (movement.js climbOverFall), no block laid to rise from a cell whose four sides fall more than three on a walk whose goal is no higher: the canopy staircase is refused at its first step off the leaves. A staircase laid down the outside is not built: under a gap there is no face to lay a block against from above. A probe of the saved world's top now (sealed in at 130): the one way, dig_down, 23 blocks in about 117 seconds, 10 cobblestone by hand after the last pickaxe use, stopping at 104 over the column's 19-block gap to ask again; on a small copy of the tower with the west side open and the bucket carried, Jev took ride_water 5 of 5 over a dig that stops still up. Tests: test/way-down.test.js, the tower copy is a top and flat ground, a hill and a shelter sealed on the ground are not; the dig stops over the seven-block gap and says so, a solid column ends where a walk goes on, nothing over lava; the waterfall off the open side (not in the Nether), and off a dug wall when sealed in; a fall offered only when it leaves health; the fallback; a walk off a top asks once, digs the column and walks on; no way offered, the walk builds no tower and the movements are put back. test/movement.test.js, no rise laid from a column top toward a goal below, a goal above still climbs, open ground still towers. Each fails without its change. npm test and JEV_ARBITER=shadow npm test 1787 of 1787; replay suite 36 of 37 and the known gap. Still open: the water ride's descent and landing are checked in code, not yet seen live; the bucket is not refilled; the unstuck pillar move is still offered on a top.
569. *mid-244-ab, "tried to swim in lava" in the Nether at 03:18:47, from 20 health, in a bunker it dug.* Piglins and hoglins 8 to 18 blocks off at y 39; Jev took bunker (0.40). The bunker was dug four times: the stance held, and every step ran digBunker again from where the last one ended (three west, one south), twelve blocks into the netherrack in ten seconds. The fourth leg's third head cell, (-4, 40, 233), had a lava fall on the rock above it (the saved world: lava from y 41 up at x -4 and -5); bunkerSide and cornerCell asked only for natural rock on a floor, not the rule every tunnel digs by (tunneling.js safeExcavation, "water or lava behind the rock"), and the lava came down into the tunnel. body_way asked three times: to_dry_ground (0.71) at (-2, 39, 233), then back_the_way_came (0.61, 0.74) to (-1, 39, 233), each "at roughly a block a second through lava (not measured)"; the bot went 3.2 blocks in 4.9 seconds, reached the last dry cell, and it was lava by then, as was the whole tunnel back to x 2 (the saved world). The movement was not the pathfinder's (out_of_lava is a held-key move, and motion.js leaves a move begun in lava alone); the body moved about as fast as the game lets it. From the 26.1.2 jar: LivingEntity.travelInLava adds 0.02 of the keys' 0.98 a tick and halves the motion every tick, sprinting or not, so 0.0196 blocks a tick, about 0.4 a second; the flow's push (Entity, 0.007 a tick in fast lava, 0.0023 elsewhere, halved too) adds or takes up to about 0.14 a second; a jump in lava adds only height. LavaFluid spreads a block every 10 ticks and up to 7 from where it pours in the Nether (fast lava), every 30 and up to 3 elsewhere: faster than a body swims either way, so once the tunnel opened under the fall the death was sealed, and the fix is the dig. Lava hurts 4 a time and is not among the damage that bypasses armour: in full iron 1.92 a hit, two hits a second, and the state's "about 2.3 seconds to death" at 18.1 was 4.7 (it lasted 4.9). Now: the bunker opens no cell, feet or head, with lava or water beside it or above it (bunker.js sideRefused, the turn too), and looks again at each cell as it reaches it, stopping short with what it has dug; a wall left for the liquid behind it is said in the option ("Not dug where there is lava behind the netherrack at (-4, 40, 233): a cell opened there lets it in, and lava in a tunnel spreads faster than a body moves through it"), and with every side so, no bunker is offered. A bunker is dug once: the dug cells are kept (survival state bunkerDug), and standing in them the option is "Stay in the bunker already dug here", the run steps back to its inside and faces the doorway without digging (the rule's own bunker, for a pack without Jev, too). The lava escape: LAVA_BLOCKS_A_SECOND is 0.4 (it was 1, unmeasured); the ways out are priced by the way through the lava (lavaRoutes: cells the body fits in, a step to a side up to one up or one down, diagonals past a corner only with room), not the straight line, which had offered (0, 39, 232) at 2.9 blocks behind the tunnel's rock corner; a cell with no way through is not a way out (one two up keeps its straight line, for the pillar); the walk goes cell by cell along that way, jumping, each cell given its 2.5 seconds and one over, and ends once out; a cell with lava beside it says how lava spreads there, and the last dry footing says when it is lava itself; the state gives lava's rate through the armour worn (fire protection not counted). Tests: bunker-dig.test.js, lava over the third cell west's head refuses that side and says it, lava on every side leaves no bunker, the turn beside lava is refused, and a bunker dug and stood in is not dug again (all fail without the change); survival.test.js, the flooded tunnel's way out is the cell round the corner by 5 blocks at 12.5 seconds, not the pocket behind the rock, walked cell by cell round the turn, and 3.8 a second through iron; the bunker option says the lava behind the wall and, dug, says to stay. npm test 1784 of 1784, shadow arbiter the same; replay suite 36 of 37 and the known gap. Open: with every wall near liquid-backed, the bunker is not offered and that is said nowhere; and a dig anywhere else that opens a cell under a lava fall is the tunnels' rule, not looked at here.

564. *Note 557's open point, lava lying on a fortress's floor and a corridor filled with rock, and mid-235-q-nether-1-fortress-3 (25590, the user: "in a fortress running in circles").* The live trial stood in a room of nether brick about 13 by 10 at (187, 57, 200), its corridor on east flooded one deep by lava running down from (202, 73, 200) and a stair down to the west; in two minutes it walked 375 blocks and ended 10 from where it began, all find_fortress. The map walk was not what paced: its failed walks were rested and said (three "No path to the goal!" in waysFailed). Jev chose stay_in_fortress at 03:07:26 (0.42) and back_to_fortress at 03:08:37, and the patrol then walked the room's four floors twelve steps off, back and forth, for three minutes at a time. Beside it, unwalked_1 and unwalked_2 were the floors past the lava, each said as "the nearest crossing is 7 blocks ... between them 4 of lava, 2 of wall or rock", a straight line through the room's wall, and no way across lava lying on a floor existed: the span refuses "lava in the way". In note 557's saved world (mid-242-aa-020157) the same held: the corridor's 120 floors were cut off from both spawners by five cells of a lava fall's run-out on the floor at x -137 to -130. Now the way to floors seen unwalked is found along the ground as a player goes (fortress-map.js crossing, one search from every floor joined to here to every part): floor walked, lava lying one deep on a floor covered (a block laid into lava takes its place, and the way goes on a block up, on the laid blocks), natural rock filling the way dug (never with lava or water behind it; the fortress's bricks are walls), open air spanned; cheapest by seconds, and a second search for the way round the lava where the way has lava on it. Parts of the fortress one way reaches through the same lava, rock or gap are one offer, the nearest named with how many more parts and floors lie that way (the live trial's two nearest parts lay across the same lava, offered apart, and the choice to cross was split between them). unwalked_N says the way cell by cell ("12 cells from (199, 57, 199) to (209, 57, 199): 1 of lava lying on the floor, to cover (a block each, walked a block up), 11 of floor, about 5 seconds; no way round the lava found along the ground"), the straight line kept only where nothing is found. Chosen, and the walk on foot failing, fortress_approach offers the ways across beside the span, pillar and staircase: cover_lava (its cells, the blocks against those carried, the seconds, where the lava comes from: a source seen, or flowing with none among the cells followed from it and running down from above, so it keeps coming; how many cells are beside lava and that a misstep there is 8 health a second in it and up to 15 seconds of burning after; that lava still running beside can run onto a laid block), scoop_lava (empty buckets carried and sources on the way: a bucket takes a source, flowing lava cannot be scooped, a cell still lava when reached is covered), dig_through (the way through rock, round the lava where there is one: blocks dug, the seconds with what is carried, and that netherrack dug by hand drops nothing), and span_round (round the lava over open air, laying a span). Each runs bridging.js crossAlong: walked crouched cell by cell, each cell read again as it is reached, a block laid on the floor under the lava, rock dug with clear's rule, a block laid level over open air, a step up with a jump; lava that has run onto the way is not walked into. The ground the bot stands on is now a floor of the map whatever it is, so its own blocks over lava or a gap join the floors either side (otherwise the far side stayed "unwalked floors across a gap" it had crossed, offered back and forth). stay_in_fortress says how far the floors joined to here run ("the 70 floors joined to here lie within 13 by 10 blocks, and walking them again is walking back and forth in that") and whether a spawner is seen among them; the map's joinedOnFoot says how many floors lie apart and in how many parts. Checked in simulation against both saved worlds (the region files read, fortress-map.js walked frontier by frontier, each crossing's blocks laid and dug in the simulated world): in mid-242-aa-020157 from the bot's corridor the map now crosses (two spans at the corridor's east end, then covering five cells of the lava's run-out on the corridor's floor from the wart room's stair, 9 seconds) and the spawner at (-108, 77, 155) is seen and joined on foot, 8 steps from where the walk ends; with covering forbidden the way round is found instead, 4 blocks of netherrack dug from the parallel corridor at about 8 seconds, and the spawner is reached that way too. In the live world the ways are found in 60 to 180 ms. Probed against the live model with the 03:07:26 state: as recorded, stay_in_fortress 5 of 5; with the way along the ground, the room's extent and the parts merged, stay_in_fortress 4 of 5 and unwalked_1 1 of 5 (stay 0.24 to the crossing's 0.22 plus 0.11), where the options are honest and the choice is Jev's. Tests: the corridor with six cells of lava on its floor, walked on foot to the lava's edge, offers unwalked_1 with the way along the ground and the way round, and chosen, the walk failing, fortress_approach offers cover_lava (6 blocks of 64, the flow, the burn) and span_round, and covering lays a block into each cell and walks across to the floor past it; the way round through netherrack beside the lava digs and never steps in or on the lava, a bucket carried with two sources on the way offers scoop_lava, and a corridor's mouth filled with netherrack offers dig_through (2 cells, 4 blocks); two parts past the same lava are one offer; a small room's patrol says its extent. Each fails without the change. npm test 1783 of 1783, the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow; replay suite 36 of 37 and the known gap. Still open: a mouth filled with rock whose far side is not seen at all (no floor seen past it) is not yet a way on of its own: the map offers only floors it has seen; crossAlong has run only in the unit test's world, not on a server; and Jev still prefers pacing a small room to crossing lava in the live state, 4 of 5.
570. *mid-242-ac-nether-2-fortress-1 (25592), fortress_approach asked 351 times in ten minutes, other_way 345 of them, every 0.3 to 0.5 seconds; the bot at 8.4 health standing still at (-167.5, 107, 60.1), walked 24, net 5.* Every leg from there rested (note 557) and the search's one way left was go_to_blazes: the busiest sighting, 13 times at (-187, 74, 162), 109 blocks off and 33 down, last seen forty-seven minutes before, none of them in sight. Taken as the one way (note 560, not asked), the walk on foot found no route, the way there was asked (fortress_approach, state.stretch "where blazes were seen"), and Jev chose other_way, "set aside for ten minutes", 0.55 to 0.65 each time against the tunnel's 0.2 to 0.3. Nothing set it aside: other_way's run only told the walk it was left, goToStep ended, and the next tick offered the same place as the one way again, 702 walks back by the end. Note 560's rule saw it, and did not stop it: the facts were the same between two askings (the fingerprint differed only when the distance said moved a block, the pathfinder's error changed wording, or a piglin came into view), so every third asking was held and a stall raised, strike 141 to 160, which the cached detour ("differently") answered with nothing; the held run was forgotten and the next two askings started a new one. And a stall raised from inside the way left state.goTo standing, so the next tick walked it again unasked. Meanwhile two blazes were 34 blocks off, heard through the walls: sightings are kept only within the hunt's 32 blocks, and the question said neither them nor any blaze, only "fortress 104 blocks off" and "about 1 fireball ends it". The same shape in portal_way (note 568): around_right and around_left in turn, each back within 0.3 of a second, no two askings with the same facts. Now, in general (decisions/repeats.js quickBefore): besides the fingerprint's run, each question keeps its last answers in a row that each came back within five seconds with nothing measurable between (the same measures: over three blocks moved, what is carried, a block dug or placed, the stall watch's progress), whatever was answered and whatever the facts said, on the same piece of work (goal). The run is said from the first such answer (lastAnswersCameToNothing: "the last 2 answers to this question in a row (around right 1 time, around left 1 time, in the last 1 second) each came back within a second and nothing measurable came of any of them ..., whatever the facts said between; the last ended: No path to the goal!") and held at the third (not asked a fourth time, said for two minutes after, the step's stall raised), as the same facts' run is at the second; waits and emergencies are said, never held, as before. In the search (mob-hunt.js): other_way does what it says, the place is set aside for ten minutes (state.waysLeft, leaveWay) and not offered again until then (go_to_blazes, the fortress's unwalked floors), said in fortress_leg's state (waysLeft: where, how far, when left, for how long more, and why) and in the step's error when nothing else is left; a way chosen that a stall ends is ended with that why (goToEnded), not walked again unasked. The blazes lead: blazesSeen is the first fact of fortress_approach too, and begins with the blazes the bot knows of now within 96 blocks, in sight or heard (blazesAbout: "2 blazes about now (none in sight, 2 heard through the walls), the nearest 34 blocks off ..."), and go_to_blazes_about is a fortress_leg option for them, walked on foot first and the way asked where the walk finds none. The same shape in the stance (mid-244-ad, 25583, 03:42:01 to 03:42:15): a crossbow piglin about seven blocks off, out_of_sight chosen forty-one times in eleven seconds from 6.4 health to 1.2, "Walk 6 blocks to a spot 4.7 blocks off" each time, and the bot moved 1.4 blocks; the option said "Tried 41 times in the last 11 seconds here, and it failed" with no why, because the walk's error was swallowed (survival.js out_of_sight), and the next asking offered the same spot. Now a hiding spot the walk did not reach is said with why ("the walk to the spot at (x, y, z) ended 3.2 blocks short of it, where it began: No path to the goal!", stanceWhy, so failedHereJustNow carries it) and passed over for half a minute while the bot stands within four blocks of where that walk began (coverFailed; bunker.js coverWithin skip): the next spot out of the line is offered with the failed one said, or none. The stance question is said, never held (NEVER_HELD): a stall raised from it would leave the bot with no stance and the mobs still there; its answers that came to nothing are said by the rule above (lastAnswersCameToNothing). Tests: survival.test.js, in the corridor with a skeleton, a walk to the side opening that fails is said with its why, the next opening is offered with the first said, none once both failed, and both again after half a minute; decisions.test.js, out_of_sight four times with health falling between is said, not held. Tests: decisions.test.js, portal_way answered around_right and around_left in turn with the facts changing each time is said at the second asking, "the last 2 answers ...", and held at the fourth, not asked; moving seven blocks starts afresh; mob-hunt.test.js, on the ac floor the way to where blazes were seen left is not offered at the next leg and is said in waysLeft, blazesSeen leads the approach and the leg, two blazes 35 blocks off lead it and are offered as go_to_blazes_about; a way to the blazes held by the rule ends with its why and goTo cleared. All fail without the change. npm test 1812 of 1812 (on 564, 565, 568 and 569), the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow; replay suite 38 of 39 with the known gap. Still open: the stance's fight was answered thirty-three times in twenty seconds in mid-244-ad too, "the last run at the piglin ... ended 6 blocks off, no nearer", and why its charge ends there is not found here; the hold's stall was answered by a detour cached from before ("differently") that did nothing, strike after strike, and stillness.js does not yet change its answer when its own detour comes to nothing; at 8.4 health, hunger 13 and nothing to eat, what a hunt among blazes costs is said (whatAHitCosts) but going for food is not among the search's ways; none of this has run live.
573. *The design review's measures in the audit (notes 558, 562).* `progress-audit.js` (and `--json`, as `review` and `targets`) and the trail map's header now say per trial over the window, each against its target: answers that came back at once to nothing (the same question asked again under 2 s after, no move over 3 blocks, nothing carried changed), per question a 15 min, under 5; askings of a question within 2 min of its hold ([repeat] in the bot log, the asking placed by its decision line's stamp), 0; the clocked minutes on persist, detour (its choices by name, "differently" among them) and shake loose, under 10%; new 4×4 columns a 15 min of Nether time, given 5 min of it (20+, ours: the review set none); the rates are of the minutes asked for, not of a younger trial's; minutes from the Nether to the first fortress seen (the bot's world file's landmark firstAt, else the trial record) and to the first blaze rod (the flight record, read from the Nether on only for a trial that has carried one), 30 and 60 (ours, from the pace told to Jev); and the rung's target (the portal, the fortress, blazes, endermen, else the step's own), its distance at the window's start and end and the best, 8+ closer or reached. First run on the thirteen live trials: fortress_approach answered other_way 1,272 times in 30 min on mid-242-ac-nether-2-fortress-1, 1,266 of them within two minutes of one of its 589 holds (the hold stops nothing: the next asking starts a fresh run); persist and detour 87% of mid-242-aa-fortress-3's clock, 67% of mid-242-ac-nether-2-fortress-1's; unstuck_move back at once to nothing 16 times on mid-243-ad-nether-1; no first rod in any trial, 42 to 398 min after the Nether. Test: a Nether window with eight quick answers, a hold and one asking after it, persist a quarter of the clock, a walk over new ground toward a fortress and a blaze, a fortress seen 35 min and a rod 50 min after the Nether, each measured and flagged or not against its target.
575. *mid-243-ad-nether-1 (25586) and mid-242-ac-nether-1-fortress-3 (25584), each "went up in flames" standing still in fire.* mid-243-ad-nether-1 stood at the west end of a one-wide basalt span at y 33 over the lava sea (its top at y 31), its own oak planks at the end, with four magma cubes two to four blocks about, on rail_and_fight then fight; a fire lit on the planks at 03:53:02 and it stood in it at (31.8, 34, 15.5) from 7.5 to none in seven seconds, never moving a block. mid-242-ac-nether-1-fortress-3 stood in fire by a fortress at (-115.3, 74, 153.6) with four blazes five to eight blocks off, 9.2 to 5.4 in four seconds, and moved two blocks only as it died. Two causes. The first, in both: the work's turn hands the vitals its task with the threat check on (work.js), and maintainVitals checked it before anything else, so "Threat nearby" was thrown before the fire was looked at, twenty-one passes a second (the loop's "spinning" lines); mid-242-ac's way out, two steps, was reported once at 04:03:42.6 and never walked, the move's own check throwing on its first tick, and mid-243-ad's was looked for once. The second, mid-243-ad's: note 548's rule refuses every cell beside a fall that kills on the run out of fire, and on a span over the lava sea every cell is beside one, so no way was found ("steps": null) though the span's cells three east were clear of any flame. Now the body's own dangers in maintainVitals (a head in a block, powder snow, fire, burning, air) are answered under a task only a cancellation stops, as body_way's question already was (vitals.js bodyTask); the threat stops the meal and what comes after, as before. The run out of fire looks for a way along cells beside a fall when none clear of one is found, and walks each step to or from such a cell crouched (sneak, no sprint, each cell stood on; a crouched body does not walk off an edge, which prismarine-physics models as the game does), a step down beside a fall not taken. A mob in a cell is no wall to the route and the keys stay held until the cell is reached. body_way's fire question offers every way there is: out_of_fire (the run clear of any edge), crouch_out_of_fire (the crouched walk, where it is the only way or the quicker, said with the steps beside the drop, what the drop falls into and the seconds at about 1.3 blocks a second), and rise_on_block (a block of anything that holds on the floor, gravel included, put in the fire's cell on a jump, which puts that flame out, the body a block up; said with the flames beside where it stands, those a level down that do not touch it, and a drop beside it), with the golden apple as before. Asked live with the span's scene, rise_on_block 5 of 5 with sixteen gravel carried (mid-243-ad had them), crouch_out_of_fire 5 of 5 (the only way) with none. Tests: vitals.test.js, on mid-243-ad's span as the region file has it the way out runs east along the span and is offered as crouch_out_of_fire then rise_on_block, walked sneaking every step, and without blocks the crouch alone; in fire with the threat check throwing, maintainVitals walks out first and throws for the threat after. Both fail without the change. npm test 1814 of 1814, the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow; replay suite 38 of 39 with the known gap. Still open: inFire counts flames beside the block under a body that has risen on it (fireNear reaches a level down), so the question may come once more from the top, offering another block; the fight stance was held by rule between its questions while the body burned, and whether its close-in walks back into flames is not looked at here; none of this has run live.
574. *return_fireball chosen in two deaths and failing both: mid-235-q-nether-3-fortress-3 (25585) at 8.3 health, 03:45:27 and :35, two fireballs landing (8.3 to 4 to none by 03:45:40); mid-242-ab-nether-2 (25585) at 19.3 health (0.59), 03:53:44, two blocks from a drop into lava, the first fireball at 03:53:46 throwing it from y 40 into the lava at y 28, dead in four seconds.* Every return_fireball in the day's logs and flight records (read only): chosen eleven times in seven runs (mid-242-aa-nether-1 at 02:40, mid-242-ad-nether-2 at 02:39, mid-243-ad at 03:00 twice, mid-243-ad-nether-1 at 03:16 and 03:22, mid-242-aa-nether-1-fortress-1 at 03:40, mid-235-q twice, mid-242-ab-nether-2 at 03:52 and 03:53); a strike was sent in four watches (02:40:31, 03:16:56, 03:45:31, 03:45:35, one fireball each); none was seen to go back and no ghast was killed (no ghastGone, no ghast tear); six fireballs landed during the watches (03:45:36.9, 03:45:40.1, 03:53:46.5, 03:22:33.9, 03:22:37.1, 02:40:31.9), and three of the runs died of them, two thrown into lava (mid-242-aa-nether-1 at 02:40:33 from 1.9 health, mid-242-ab-nether-2). Why no strike connected, checked in the 26.1.2 jar and mineflayer 4.39: the strike is its own packet in 26.1 (ServerboundAttackPacket), and mineflayer's bot.attack sends it (attackUsesOwnPacket), so it goes; handleAttack takes it while the struck box is within the attack range of the eyes, which with no attack_range on the item is the entity interaction range (3) and a slack of 3 (Player.isWithinAttackRange, AttackRange.defaultFor): six blocks, as note 551 said; no charge is asked of a plain item (cannotAttackWithItem looks only at a minimum_attack_charge) and the deflection comes before any damage, so a strike every tick is taken every tick; the fireball struck flies along the player's look (AIM_DEFLECT, getLookAngle) and is sent to the clients at once (needsSync). What failed was where the fireball was: the server sends a fireball's place only every ten ticks (EntityType FIREBALL updateInterval 10) and a client flies it between; mineflayer does not, so bot.entities had it where it was up to half a second before (its motion is right, compatibility.js undoing mineflayer's 1/8000ths on the lpVec3). The strike struck a fireball within five blocks as last sent; the server takes a strike in the last three or four ticks before the fireball lands, and an update every ten ticks falls in them at nine distances in fifty from fifteen to sixty-four (worked through the jar's flight), and then a tick late. The look was on the ghast (lookAt every 50 ms with force, sent at the next physics tick), so it was not the look. Now (src/ghast.js): each fireball is flown between its updates as AbstractHurtingProjectile.tick flies it (flyTicks: gain 0.1 along itself, drag 0.95, then the move) from its last place and its motion read from the packet (spawn_entity, entity_velocity; a vector over 4 is taken as the older 1/8000ths), or from two places; the watch looks at the ghast, waits for that look to go out with the physics tick, and strikes on every tick the fireball's box is within six blocks of the eyes now or a tick on; it counts what came of each (came, struck, sent back when the next update goes away from the bot, landed when it is gone within reach and not sent back, the ghast killed) and watches a fireball sent back home for up to three seconds more. The count is kept with the goal (goal.fireballReturns) and on survivalAction. The option says the server's six blocks and three or four ticks, the record before this change and this bot's own since ("Since then it has not been tried"), and is priced by it: the next two fireballs landing but for the share measured sent back (0 of 6: both, 12 on Normal with no armour where it said 6 for one missed); over a drop a shot can push the bot off (survival.js shotOverEdge, note 563), "the first that lands is the push over the drop below: the price is that fall, the bot's death, ... not its 4.8 damage", where it said "Priced with one fireball missed: about 4.8 damage" beside note 563's sentence.
