Tests (test/ghast.test.js): a server and a mineflayer client as they are for a ghast's fireball (ten-tick updates, the strike taken within six blocks, the look last sent, the client tick between the server's, the world's own clock): at 22, 30, 44 and 60 blocks the fireball is struck while the server takes it, goes back and kills the ghast, counted came 1, struck 1, sent back 1, killed 1 (each fails on the old strike, which never struck in time, and they pass in real time too); a fireball not struck is counted landed; flyTicks against the jar's two ticks. survival.test.js: the record and the price said, and this bot's count since changing both (2 of 8 sent back: 1.5 of 2 landing, 9); span-fall.test.js: on the span over the lava sea with a ghast in sight, return_fireball priced by the fall, death. Replay case ghast-strike-over-lava-drop, the 03:53:44 question with return_fireball's text as this change gives it: the recorded text chose return_fireball 10 of 10, the new take_cover 10 of 10 (the cover from the cobblestone carried), 5 of 5 in the suite (expected: the cover or the pocket; forbidden: return_fireball, the work). npm test 1818 of 1818, the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow; replay suite 39 of 40 with the known gap. Open: the strike is not yet measured live; the look is at the ghast's middle as it is, not where it drifts to while the fireball flies back; a busy bot's event loop (2 to 4 s stalls at twenty trials) misses the three or four ticks whatever the timing; whether the other projectiles read from bot.entities (projectile-guard.js, risk.js) are as stale between their updates is not looked at here.
576. *mid-242-ae-nether-1 (25586), "slain by Piglin Brute" at 04:06:32 in a bastion, at 20 health in an iron helmet and chestplate with an iron sword and a shield: dead in two blows a second apart.* The stance was asked five times in twenty-one seconds at 20 health (04:06:09 rail_and_fight 0.77, 04:06:12 fight_from_footing 0.32, 04:06:21 pillar 0.53, 04:06:30 rail_and_fight 0.66, with retreats at 04:05:40 and 04:05:54), eighteen piglins within twenty-four, the brute (golden axe) 6.9 blocks off at the last, two crossbow piglins in sight, a drop of 34 into lava two blocks off. At 04:06:31 the brute was at 1.7 blocks and struck (20 to 7.8), and again at 04:06:32. What the question said: the rail said its 4 blocks and 2.4 seconds of walling "anything at reach hitting freely meanwhile" and priced nothing at all, no damage and no expects: the one fighting stance with no figure, where the fight read 116.5, the pillar 89.7 and out of sight 55.9; fight_from_footing the same, a step "the mobs hitting freely meanwhile" and no figure; the fight's pace said "9 swings ... 50 health" and "0.7 hits a second" at reach, and hitsLeft (the blows the health takes) is said only under 14 health, so nowhere was it said that one blow is 12.2 through that armour (13 bare, the brute's 7 and the axe's 6) and two end the bot, nor that the brute runs 5.3 blocks a second and was a second from reach. Every stance's price brought the biters in at three blocks a second (combat-estimate arrives, APPROACH), the brute's 1.8 seconds where it took one. And the retreat said "No way out: none of the 12 spots further from every mob has a route that passes none of them": with eighteen piglins about, footing further from every one and a route passing none is nowhere in a bastion, and a bastion's own floors (polished blackstone bricks and the rest) were not footing at all (ESCAPE_FOOTING); the way off the brute's ground, past its reach with the crossbows' bolts taken on the way, was never offered, and retreat got 0.02. Read from the 26.1.2 jar: a piglin brute has 50 health, 0.35 speed (5.3 a second on the ground, a sprint's 5.6 barely ahead) and a follow range of 12 (PiglinBrute.createAttributes); it goes after the nearest player it sees within that 12 (the player sensor's range is the follow range), or one it is angry at (struck by it; ANGRY_AT, 600 ticks) within 12 seen or not, and drops the target otherwise (StopAttackingIfTargetInvalid); it strikes once a second (MeleeAttack 20 ticks); idle, it walks back to its HOME, the spot it was made at (initMemories), from within a hundred blocks (StrollToPoi), and strolls within five of it; no biome spawns one (none in the biome data): brutes come only with a bastion. So "brutes do not leave their bastion" is not the rule: one follows a player it sees anywhere within 12, and gives up past 12 or out of its sight (unless struck), then goes home. Now: every stance opens with the hardest blow of those that can get to the bot (survival.js blowsSay): "The piglin brute 7 blocks off hits for about 12.2 a blow through the armour worn (13 before it), a blow a second at arm's length: 2 blows end the bot from 20 health, and at its own speed (about 5.3 blocks a second) it can be at arm's length in about 1 second." The rail is priced as every stance is, where something can be fought too: its walling with what gets to the bot hitting, then the fight here begun once the blocks are down (stanceCost fight.lead, as the pillar's, note 559), with expects; the step to firm ground the same, its seconds of stepping then the fight there. Every stance's price brings a biter in at its own chase speed (combat-estimate arrives: blocksPerSecond; a creeper keeps the walk its fuse is reckoned with). A new stance, leave_reach: where no run passes every mob, the scout before the question (scoutRetreat, reachFootings) looks for footing further from each biter that can follow the bot than the range it follows a player to (plus two) and nearer the bot than any of them, by a route passing none of those that bite, the shooters not kept clear of; offered only where one is found, said with the blocks, the seconds at a run, the shooters' damage over them, how each biter follows (the chase, and whether it gives up), and run to the way found (leaveReach). Every chase said (chaseSays) adds, for a piglin brute, how it gives up by the game's rule: within 12 and in its sight (seen or not for 30 seconds once struck), then home to the spot in its bastion it was made at, found nowhere else (combat-estimate GIVES_UP). A bastion's floors are footing (polished blackstone bricks, cracked, polished blackstone, chiseled, gilded blackstone, polished basalt). Probes (5 asks each through decide, not in the suite): the 04:06:30 question as recorded, rail_and_fight 5 of 5; its texts as the fixes give them (the blows first, the rail 103.6 and the step 87.2 with the two crossbows over fifteen seconds, the brute's rule on the retreat), out_of_sight 5 of 5 (55.9 as recorded; the brute a second off, no stance there survives two blows at reach); 04:06:09 (brute 11.2 blocks off) as recorded and as fixed, rail 5 of 5 (63.9 now), and with a leave_reach beside it (a hypothetical way 18 blocks off, 29 from the brute, 3.2 seconds, the bolts over them), leave_reach 5 of 5. Whether such a way stood there is not known: the earlier retreats from 17, 64, -163 found 19 blocks south to footing, so it is likely, not shown. No replay case: with the brute a second off every stance recorded is death, and which way is out is not in the record. Tests (test/brute-reach.test.js, each failing on the old code): a brute 6.9 off is at the bot inside a rail's 2.4 seconds and priced over one blow; on mid-211-s's ledge with a brute seven off every stance opens with the two blows, and the rail and the step to ground carry figures and expects of 20 or more; the scouted way past the brute offered with the 24 blocks from it, the bolts, the chase and the brute's rule, run to the spot, and not offered without one; reachFootings keeps footing past 14 from the brute and nearer the bot, and a bastion's floor counts. The cave spider's stance figures take its 3.9 a second (combat-estimate test); the pillar's "no cover" and the dug bunker's opening (notes 559, 569) come after the blow now. npm test 1818 of 1818, the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow; replay suite 38 of 39 with the known gap (blaze-at-1.4-cover-missing). Open: the fight's closing (PACE.leadBites, 0.18 hits a second, fitted to slower mobs) prices the brute's walk-in at a sixth of a blow a second where it is at reach in one second and strikes once a second, so the fight here (41 with a lone brute) reads below the rail (58) it contains; out_of_sight and the nook still say a biter "still reaches it" where a brute not struck gives up once out of its sight; the brute's anger at a struck bot is not known to the bot (the 30 seconds are said as a condition); leave_reach is searched only when no ordinary way is found, a further 0.3 seconds of route searches at most (standing still); none of this has run live.

578. *mid-242-ac-nether-3 (25591), "slain by Enderman" at 04:10:16 on a span at y 54, 22 blocks over the lava sea, eight seconds after one came within four blocks at full health.* Why it turned: the span was being laid crouched, each step looking at eye height over the next cell (bridging.js creepTo, the cell's middle 1.6 up), which from a crouch's 1.27 is about fourteen degrees up (the recorded pitch 0.25), and an enderman stood still on the span's own line at 41.5, 114.5. Its eyes (2.55 up its 2.9) were in that look: the game's stare test (26.1.2 EnderMan isBeingStaredBy, LivingEntity.isLookingAtMe: the look against the line from the player's eyes to the enderman's closer than 1 - 0.025 / distance, a line of sight, no carved pumpkin) passed from about five blocks, the recorded look 0.015 radians off its eyes at 3.9, where the cone is 0.11. It was not hit first. gaze.js lowered only a level gaze while walking; a look up was left alone as deliberate. It came 2.2 blocks in a quarter second (8.8 a second: an angry enderman goes 0.15 faster than its 0.3, EnderMan SPEED_MODIFIER_ATTACKING) and hit at 04:10:09.7. Did the fight strike: yes, in the 1.8 seconds it held, one swing at 1.02 blocks 1.3 seconds after the answer (the first hit's knock had the bot in the air) and one more as the rail was asked; one more during the rail at 2.93; three in all, about 18 of the enderman's 40 at most. No teleport followed a swing, as the game has it (a hurt from a living thing does not teleport it; an arrow or a potion does, nine in ten tries from no living thing); the charge followed it where it was (note 555). Its hits: 20, 15.9, 11.9 in the fight; 7.8 and 3.8 while the planks were made and the bot, knocked a block east by each hit, laid no wall; 0.7 and none at the second rail. 4.06 a hit through iron and gold boots, the table's 4.1. The fight was told "about 6 seconds and 7.8 damage" from 20 and "5.4 seconds and 7.4" from 11.9: seven swings at 0.9 were right, the damage was a third of its hits for the one struck (STRUCK, note 550's zombies and skeletons). The bot's enderman fights in the records with three or more swings (25589 00:12 and 25598 12:56 on 2026-09-27 and 28, and this one) landed 14 hits in 20.4 seconds while struck, about 0.7 a second: at its angry speed a swing's knockback is walked back before its next blow is due. The rail was offered with "anything at reach hitting freely meanwhile" and no figure (stanceCost was used for it only where nothing could be fought); it was taken at 0.48 and 0.56 over a fight told 7.4. The retreat said it teleports after the bot; it does so only past sixteen blocks, and within them it outruns the bot's sprint. Shields: an enderman's blow is a mob attack, which a raised shield takes from the front (no bypass), as the bot already raises it between swings. Now: gaze.js checks every look each tick against every enderman within sixty-four by the game's test widened four times, and one on an enderman's eyes is turned just below them (its body, which provokes nothing), else above, else to the ground, the yaw kept, whatever set the look (a step, a block placed, a dig, a swing; the server takes those by where they are aimed); the level walk's lowering stays. combat-estimate: an enderman struck lands 0.7 of its hits a second (MOBS struck, used by fightTimeline in STRUCK's place), comes on at 1.5 times its speed (CHASE, 8.7 a second), and its note, in the stance's state, says the teleport rules, the shield, its 2.9 height (a two-high pocket or hole keeps it out) and the sixteen blocks. rail_and_fight is priced wherever it is offered, the planks' second and the walls under what is at reach, then the fight with its closing (stanceCost fight lead), with expects; hold_on_span is priced the same way (its walls first where it walls, then what comes struck where it stands, shooters shooting). The retreat says the enderman's 8.7 against the bot's 5.6. The same questions now read: fight from 20 about 6 seconds and 15.9 damage; from 11.9 about 5.4 and 15.5 (more than the bot has); the rail and the hold from 11.9 about 28.7 and 31.3 over their fifteen seconds (more than the bot has). Replay, a new case from the 04:10:11 question with that text (span-enderman-at-reach-rail-priced): none_good 5 of 5, and the best listed taken each time is the fight (it had taken the rail); Jev's none_good is fair, the move is missing (below). Tests (test/enderman.test.js): the recorded crouched look turned off its eyes, yaw kept; a look up at nothing and a strike's look at its middle left alone; the enderman's figures; the rail and the hold priced on the trial's span with the planks made first; all fail before but the look left alone. The retreat's test takes the new words. npm test and JEV_ARBITER=shadow npm test 1817 of 1817; replay suite 39 of 40 and the known gap (blaze-at-1.4-cover-missing). Still open: on a one-wide span no stance an enderman cannot follow is offered: a roof over the bot's cell and the span's cells beside it (an enderman cannot stand under two high, and from two cells off its blow, its own box widened about 0.83 to the sides, does not reach, where the sword's three does), or a pocket from the logs carried (seal counts blocks carried only, 3 planks here, not the 20 the logs make); a shield-timed fight (up through its blow, the swing in its second's cooldown) is not a mechanic; a carved pumpkin worn in the Nether and the End is not made or offered; the walls under a biter's knocks move with the bot and none stood (railSpan places at the cells of where the bot stood when it began). None of this has run live yet.
571. *Busy but going nowhere, as one cause (a design review's advice, and note 573's audit): every place that offered ways kept its own memory of what had failed, and none saw the others.* On 25592 (mid-242-ac-nether-2-fortress-1) fortress_approach was answered other_way 4,423 times; note 560's hold fired 589 times, and each hold went to the stall's detour, "differently", an eight-block walk that reset every counter, after which the search derived the same failing way again. Note 573's audit: 1,266 of 1,272 other_way answers in thirty minutes came within two minutes of a hold, because the hold was forgotten as it fired (repeats.held drops the run) and the next asking began a new one; 25583 asked again 25 times after holds, 25584 11 times. Measured on the recorded logs before the change: of the 1,580 decisions in 25592's status lines, 1,361 were the same question and answer a third time or more from within four blocks in six minutes (other_way 1,279), with 589 [repeat] holds, 640 [stall] lines and 48 "differently"; over the 30 latest midgame logs, 12,500 of 17,329 (portal_method cast_frame 4,108 in mid-242-ab, encounter_stance fight 1,689 in mid-244-ad-nether-2, survival_priority in mid-243-ab and mid-244-ad). And the memories: approach.failed, the legs' history and rests, the fortress shunned, the ways left, the detour log, goal.lastFailure, lastStruggleStep, the answers that came to nothing, each with its own radius and clock. Now one ledger (src/tried.js, goal.tried): every answer given, asked or taken as the one way, is an entry (question, answer, where it was going when the option says so or else where the bot stood, the work it was for, and its outcome: progressed, blocked with why, waited, pending), settled when its question is asked again, at the loop pass's end when something came of it or the pass failed, or when a question below it escalates. decide reads it for every question about playing the game: an option tried from within four blocks of here (toward the same target from within sixteen, for one that names a target: go_to_blazes, the unwalked floors, fortress_approach's place, portal_way's portal, the Nether crossings) in the last ten minutes and come to nothing is said on the option ("Tried 2 times toward the same place from about here in the last 1 second, and it came to nothing: No path to the goal!"); come to nothing twice, it rests five minutes from the last, left out and said in the state (waysResting). The stance, the body's way out and the shield are recorded and not read back (their own options say their failures, note 521); turn_priority is not recorded. Escalate, never re-ask: every question about playing the game names its parent in define (step, way, plan, rung: fortress_approach under fortress_leg, fortress_leg and portal_way and the stall's question under the new rung_progress; the survival questions under survival_priority; null where none is above, the stall's question taking it as before). With every option resting, or its same answer held by note 560/570's rule, a question is not asked: the answer above that led there is marked come to nothing with this failure, and the parent is asked next with it said (whatFailedBelow); an escalation to a parent not asked since the last one climbs to that parent's own, so a held way or a plan derived without a question cannot keep a child going round. A hold rests the answers held in the ledger for five minutes (the audit's point). The rung's own budget (watchRung): ten working minutes, waits not counted, without more of what the rung is for, a milestone, a new best distance to its target (the blazes seen, the fortress, the portal frame, the step's target) or sixteen blocks of new country, and the rung's question is asked: keep_at_it (what has been tried said from the ledger, the budget starting again), the stall's answers, and the rung set aside for any rung (one the game needs says so). goal.struggles is reset on the rung's new best, not on any movement, and the stall's answers are judged by the rung (a detour's eight blocks are no longer something coming of it). The stall's "differently" is offered only while a way tried for the work rests by place alone, which a step off leaves behind; when every way tried was toward its own target it is not offered and that is said. persist writes the failed step in the ledger, does not put it back in hand unless Jev chose "again" (offered only while the step does not rest from here), and asks up the chain: the question whose answer the step was carrying out, told of the failure; with none, the stall's question with the failure. One mechanism answers a failure: the recovery adviser's moves (gather footing, the surface, another standing spot, another source, down off a pillar) are recover_ options of that question, carried out by the adviser (adopt); recovery_action is no longer asked by the loop, and three failures go to persist, not five after a separate question at three. Measured again by replaying each recorded decision of the logs through decide, at its time and place with its options and a Jev that gives the recorded answer while it is offered (it counts what would not have been asked, not what the bot would then have done): 25592's fortress_approach, 1,348 recorded, before 934 asked and 414 held, after 78 asked, 4 taken as the one way and 1,266 not asked; the whole log 1,155 askings to 285; mid-242-ab 3,597 to 731; mid-244-ad-nether-1 684 to 474; mid-242-aa-nether-1-fortress-1 439 to 360; mid-244-ab 650 to 544; mid-235-q-nether-1-fortress-3 395 to 358. Probed against the live model from the 25592 state as the rung's question now asks it (ten minutes with no new best, what was tried, "differently" not offered): floor_toward 5 of 5 (0.64 to 0.69), set_aside_rung 0.21 to 0.26, keep_at_it 0.04 to 0.06; added to the replay suite as rung-no-best-every-way-spent-25592. Tests (tried.test.js): the way to the blazes whose way there fails at once, ten passes: fortress_approach asked twice, then fortress_leg asked with the failure said on go_to_blazes and in whatFailedBelow, and a leg taken; the same failing question ten times, two askings, the one way left twice, then escalation to fortress_leg, then rung_progress, never asked again; a repeat hold rests its answer at the next asking; a rung busy walking back and forth eight blocks for ten simulated minutes is asked the rung's question with its best said, the struggle count not reset; persist asks the answer it carried out again and does not put the step back, and puts it back only on "again", not offered once it rests; every parent named is a question. decisions.test.js's repeat tests now show the ledger resting first and the escalation after; recovery-adviser.test.js's loop test, the recovery move chosen among the stall question's answers. Each fails without the change. npm test 1819 of 1819, the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow; replay suite 39 of 40 with the known gap. Still open: the other memories (legRests, waysLeft, approach.failed, the detour log, portal_way's tried) stay as they were beside the ledger, special cases it now also sees, not yet folded into it; the replay counts askings, not outcomes, and none of this has run live.

572. *The design review of 2026-09-27 (Fable, recommendation D: "Search has no coverage model"), and mid-244-ab-nether-2 (25586, 04:07 to 04:21, "Every leg from here ended at once and rests" seven times, "flipping find_fortress <-> persist").* The search's legs were ninety-six blocks at the feet's height from a compass, and nothing kept what had been seen: a leg into air already looked across was offered on the same terms as one into space never seen, and the outage default was the most open air ahead, the compass's own heading at a tie, which in a simulation walked a leg out and straight back along its own open line and back again, thirty legs, in 45 of 68 starts. Now the search keeps what it has seen of the Nether as a player would (src/nether-coverage.js, goal.fortressSearch.coverage, saved with the goal, per dimension): columns of 4 by 4 blocks, sixteen to a chunk, each chunk a sixteen-bit mask; a column is seen when a line from the eyes reaches into it through open air between y 48 and 79 (never through rock or lava), and stood on when the feet were in it. The lines are sampled, not every block: 192 from where a leg is chosen, turned a little each look so looks from one place fill in between, and six a tick while the search runs (a leg walked in one go is looked along its length), read from the world's state ids; about 0.3 ms a look in the simulation, and 3 to 8 KB saved after two thousand blocks walked. Each leg (and each floor leg) now says the ground within 128 blocks of its line, ahead and on past its end, that is unseen at fortress heights, how much of that lies within 128 of the stretches of its line in open air at this height past its first eight blocks (from inside the rock nothing is seen), "Mostly seen ... so this leg goes mostly over ground already looked over" where four fifths or more is seen, and how many of its 96 blocks the bot has stood on ("it walks again ground already walked and looked at from, and what is unseen that way lies off to its sides and past its end" at half or more); and what seen before lies that way within 256 blocks: where blazes were seen, a spawner seen, the fortress bricks last found, a fortress remembered. seenSoFar in the state says what has been looked over in all and within 96 of here. go_to_blazes says which way the place lies and whether the bot has stood within 32 blocks of it ("the bot has not stood within 32 blocks of it, so whether it can be walked to is not known"). The outage default is the most ground unseen beside the leg's open air, in chunks, a leg whose line runs half or more over ground stood on after the rest, then the most open air, then the compass. mid-244-ab-nether-2 stood in a pocket at y 32 to 35 over the lava sea and was offered all four legs as ninety-six blocks of rock to dig and open air over lava; each ended at once ("lava or water behind the netherrack", "No route ... the way passes beside lava", "no ground made"), rested, and with all four resting the step threw and the stall asked unstuck_move (164 times, 158 of 231 answers none_good). surveyLeg counted rock with lava behind it as rock to dig, where every dig refuses it (tunneling.js safeExcavation). Now the leg's survey keeps the dig's rule ("netherrack with lava or water behind it (not dug) stops it at cell 80"), a leg closed at its first cell (lava in the way, or rock the dig will not open) is not offered but said (legsClosed: "leg north: closed at the first cell at y 32, netherrack with lava or water behind it (not dug)"), and with nothing left the step says why each way is closed ("No leg from here can be walked, dug or bridged: leg east: closed at the first cell at y 32, lava in the way; ..."), where it said only that they rested. Measured in simulation on the ten saved Nether worlds with a fortress in them (.trial-checkpoints/stages/nether: 202-o, 208-k, 211-s, 227-r, 235-p, 235-q, 242-aa, 242-ab, 242-ac, 242-ad; each from where the bot stood and six drawn starts at least 96 blocks from any brick, 68 starts with a leg to walk): the region files read, legs walked straight at the fortress heights' band (y 40 to 80) digging rock and laying over gaps with 64 blocks, stopping at lava or the edge of the saved world, looks every four blocks, the fortress counted found when a nether brick's face is in a clear line from the eyes within 128 blocks, ungenerated chunks counted seen (nothing to see there). Old default: found within 30 legs from 23 of 68 starts (15 in the first leg, where the most open way happened to face it), the rest walking a line out and back; new: 65 of 68, within 3 legs 37, within 5 legs 49, within 10 legs 55, median 3 legs and 248 blocks walked; fewer legs than the old from 43 starts, more from 9 (where the old's first, most open leg faced the fortress and the new went first where less had been seen), the same from 16. With the ground counted within 64 of the line instead of 128 it found 67 of 68 at a median of 5 legs, and with the line's open air not weighed 64 of 68 at a median of 4 (legs into rock, where nothing is seen, rank as high as legs into a cavern). Probed against the live model on mid-242-ac's world after a leg south and back (the old default's shape): with the facts, leg_east 3 and leg_west 2 of 5 (both into unseen ground past open air); without them, leg_east 2, floor_east 1, seek_fortress_height 1, leg_west 1; south, the leg walked, was chosen by neither (it is also said as back the way the last leg came). Tests (test/nether-coverage.test.js): a gallery is seen down its length and not through the rock past it or beside it, and nothing from y 20; after walking east and back, leg_east says mostly seen and stood on 96 of 96, leg_west the unseen ground and the blazes seen 151 blocks that way, leg_north that its line is rock past its first eight blocks, go_to_blazes that the bot has not stood near, and without Jev the leg is west where the compass said east; the sets come back the same from JSON; the fallback's order; and the pocket over the lava sea offers east (rock to dig) and west (a gallery), says south (lava) and north (rock with lava behind it) as closed, and with every way closed the step says each. Each fails without the change. npm test 1812 of 1812, the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow; replay suite 37 of 39, the known gap and wither-skeleton-pillar-ground-one-up (pillar 2 of 5, a live-model combat case this does not touch). Still open: the simulation walks straight lines, not the pathfinder, and stops at the edge of what the trial loaded (ten worlds of 625 to 880 chunks); "seen" is a column, so a fortress below a line of sight in the same column counts as seen; a leg's line closed at its first cell may still be walked round by the pathfinder, which the leg tries first, and is now not offered from there (the floor legs and the other headings are); go_to_blazes's reachability is only whether the bot has stood near, not a path; and the unstuck_move and persist turns in mid-244-ab-nether-2 are the stall's and the persist path's (notes 570 and 571), not looked at here.
579. *mid-242-aa-nether-3 (25594), "discovered the floor was lava" at 04:14:21, standing on a magma block from 20 to none.* At 04:13:57 Jev chose restock_blocks (dig netherrack nearby). The gather's stand cell was sound: (-21, 34, 47) on the bot's own cobblestone walk at y 33 over a lava lake (its top at y 31), and spanBlockSources already refuses magma floors. It dug the netherrack at (-24, 32, 46) from there, and the pickup, its routed spots not taken, fell to its last resort, the straight walk at the drop (drop-collection.js, walk_to_drop, crouched so as not to go off an edge), went west along the line to it onto the magma blocks at (-23, 33, 47) and (-22, 33, 47) and was held by the crouch at the edge of the open cave air over the lava at (-22, 33, 46). The keys were let go at (-21.58, 34, 46.74), its middle over the hole and the edge of its box on the magma. The game hurts by the nearest block under the box (the magma, terrain.js restingCell's rule); every floor check read the cell under the middle, open air. It took "hurt: hot floor" 0.7 a time twice a second through its iron helmet and chestplate, 35 hurts in seventeen seconds, and never moved: fortress_leg, portal_way and stillness_detour were each asked on it with the floor in no fact, the hurt watchdog stopped each step as "something unseen at 0 blocks", the survival step found no mob, and return_overworld, recover_before_combat (waiting for health) and the meal each stood still on the magma. Nothing looked at the floor the body stood on, only at the floors of cells it chose to walk to (vitals.js fireRoute's floor, survival.js lavaExit's), and body_way had no hot floor. Now a hot floor is a body danger beside fire (terrain.js hotFloor, hotUnderfoot; vitals.js onHotFloor): a magma block under the body, by the game's nearest-block rule, unless crouched (a crouched body is not hurt on one, the game's rule), or a lit campfire or soul campfire, crouched or not. It is a reflex in the arbiter (hot_floor, after head_in_block, action off_hot_floor, an emergency to the stillness watch), so the watch stops whatever holds the turn (a walk, a dig, a rest, a meal) four times a second, and maintainVitals answers it under the body's task (only a cancellation stops it, as note 575's fire) with body_way's ways: step_off_hot_floor, the nearest floor that does not hurt within eight blocks, walked crouched (over other magma, no step down), said with the steps, the floor it ends on, a drop beside the way and the seconds at about 1.3 blocks a second; crouch_on_hot_floor, the crouch where it stands, let go once the body is off the magma, asked again if a walk stands it up on it; rise_on_block, a block put in the cell over the magma when the body's middle is over it; and the enchanted golden apple, whose fire resistance stops the hot floor's hurt. The condition is said with its rate through the armour worn (body.js lasts, the game's armour formula on 1 a half second: 1.4 a second here, 13.8 seconds to death at 19.3). The straight walk at a drop is not taken where the line to it would rest the body on a hot floor (drop-collection.js hotOnTheWay), and the gather's stand cells, the way out of fire, the way out of lava, the mine's drop onto what is underfoot and the work's drop below refuse a lit campfire as well as magma (terrain.js hotFloor). Asked live with the scene as the region file has it (replay case body-magma-edge-over-lava-19.3), crouch_on_hot_floor 4 and step_off_hot_floor 1 of 5. Tests: vitals.test.js, on mid-242-aa-nether-3's ground from the death snapshot's region file, the hot floor is seen though the cell under the middle is open, not while crouched, and the arbiter's reflexes carry it; the ways offered are the step off (one crouched step to the cobblestone, the drop into lava said) and the crouch, the block to stand on only when standing square on the magma, the rate said through the armour; the step off is walked crouched and the crouch held; with the threat check throwing, maintainVitals steps off the magma before it throws; the straight walk toward the dug netherrack is refused over the magma. All fail without the change. npm test 1819 of 1819, the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow; replay suite 39 of 40 with the known gap. Still open: the hurt watchdog still names a hot floor's hurts "something unseen at 0 blocks" (it hears entityHurt, which carries no damage type); the restock stood seven seconds at the stand cell between the choice and the dig, not looked at here; a crouch held on the magma lasts into the next walk, which goes crouched until off it; none of this has run live.
580. *mid-235-p-nether-3-fortress-4 (25592) and mid-235-p-nether-4-fortress-3 (25600), the same death twice from one fortress stage save: into the lava sea's edge at (86, 31, 37) walking back to the portal; and mid-242-ae-nether-3-fortress-1 (25583), into the sea at (-105, 31, 106) digging down a column at 20 health.* At 04:24:14 mid-235-p-nether-3-fortress-4 was at 4.5 health, hunger 14, nothing to eat, 140 blocks from its portal at (37, 56, 3); leave_nether offered go_back ("140 blocks off, about 33 seconds at a walk") and keep_on, and Jev took go_back 0.72. The way home is code: walkToKnownPortal's legs of thirty-two blocks by the pathfinder (portalLeg, GoalNearXZ, sprinting). Jev chose climb_here at portal_way (up 7 to y 56) and dig_down at way_down, and the legs walked it down the slope: a drop onto (110, 40, 83) cost a point (4.5 to 3.5), and the third leg, toward (68, 35), followed the pathfinder's partial route (the best node so far, walked while the search goes on) down the soul sand slope to the lava sea's shore: from the saved region (04:25:10) and the pathfinder run over it, that route was (87, 35, 38), a block laid at (86, 34, 38), (84, 34, 39) on a block laid at (84, 33, 39), then (85, 33, 38) on the soul sand at (85, 32, 38) and a block to lay west at (84, 32, 38), backing to the edge crouched. The body went east and north instead, off (85, 33, 38) a block onto the soul sand at (86, 31, 37), level with the sea; no hit, no stall recovery (the flight has none after 04:25:04). mid-235-p-nether-4-fortress-3 at 20 health laid exactly those blocks (the cobblestone at (84, 33, 39) and (84, 32, 38) are in its save), found no route on at 03:40:38, and went the same way off the same ledge to the same cell. Why that cell is lava: soul sand's top is at .875 and a lava source's surface at .889 (its amount over nine), so a body standing on the shore's soul sand beside the sea has its feet under the lava's surface and is in it (Entity.updateFluidHeightAndDoFluidPushing checks the box against each lava cell's height); every rule the walk has measured a fall onto the soul sand as a one-block drop onto ground. 3.5 to 1.6 in the lava, out in a second, then the fire lava sets burned it: 1.6, 0.6, dead (on_fire, a point a second, through the iron; the Nether has no water to put it out). mid-235-p-nether-4-fortress-3 then crashed on "Converting circular structure to JSON" in body_way's report each time it rejoined, forty-five seconds apart, standing in the lava: already fixed by ae409f7 (03:50), after that run. mid-242-ae-nether-3-fortress-1 dug down its column from y 50 at full health (way_down's dig_down, "never into a fall that hurts"): each block checked only what was under it, and the last one it dug put the feet at y 31 with the sea round them, 20 to none in four seconds. Was the way priced? No: go_back said the distance and the daylight; not the lava, not what a touch of it costs at 4.5 health (at least 18.8 through iron: two hits getting out and fifteen seconds of fire), not that health would not come back on the way. Nothing could heal first: hunger 14 is under eighteen and nothing was carried, so a sealed pocket heals nothing; no obsidian for a portal here; the lava sea's fall is the risk the walk carried. Now: terrain.js standsInLava is the game's own test, the body's box anywhere in the cell against each lava cell's surface over the floor's top (soul sand's .875 read from its shape; a whole block at the sea's level holds the feet over it). Every walk measures a landing so: a fall onto ground where the body stands in lava is a fall into lava, for the pathfinder's moves (movement.js fallOff, dropFrom, fallIntoLava, so a block laid backing to an edge over such a landing, a drop beside one and the Nether edge rule refuse it, and fallBeside for the fire run), for the edge measures (dropAt, dropNear: the walk's crouch on an edge in the Nether, and every stance's drop), for the stall's blind step (skills.js shakeLoose) and for the way down off a top (way-down.js fallUnder: dig_down stops over the rock at the sea's level and says why, and no step_off lands there). The walk's crouch measured its drops from the floored feet, which on soul sand is the soul sand's own cell, a block too high: now from the cell over the block stood on. What the way back says (game-progress.js portalTrip, every go_back and trip there): in the Nether, the straight line toward the portal, what one touch of lava costs this body now (terrain.js lavaTouchSays) where the line crosses lava or a touch would be death, and whether health comes back on the way. portal_way's state says the touch where its line crosses lava, and portal_way offers blocks_then_cross in the Nether where the straight crossing's next stretch lays more blocks than are carried and a pickaxe is: netherrack mined to the reserve, then the crossing, said with its survey (mid-242-aa-nether-1-fortress-3 was asked portal_way three times 145 blocks from its portal with one block carried, around_left, around_right and wait_rest offered, none good 0.38). Probes (5 asks each through decide): mid-235-p's leave_nether as recorded, go_back 5 of 5; with the touch and the health said, go_back 5 of 5 (staying on without food heals nothing either; the fix is the walk's). mid-242-aa's portal_way at 04:41:38 as recorded, none good 3, wait_rest 2; with a blocks_then_cross beside it (its survey figures written for the probe, not measured there), blocks_then_cross 5 of 5. Tests (test/lava-shore.test.js, on the shore as the 04:25:10 region file has it, test/fixtures/lava-shore-mid-235-p.json; each fails without the change): standing on the shore's soul sand is in the lava, a whole block there is not; from (85, 33, 38) the fall east is into lava, the block laid backing west is refused and no neighbor lays one or steps onto the shore; the walk crouches on that ledge; the stall's step does not go north onto the shore; the way back says the line, the touch (18.8 at the least, more than the 4.5 the bot has: death) and that health does not come back; a column over the lava sea is dug down only to the rock at y 31 and says why; portal_way offers blocks_then_cross with its crossing and says the touch. npm test 1852 of 1852, the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow; replay suite 42 of 43 with the known gap. Open: the pathfinder still walks its partial routes, the best node found so far, which is how both walks came to the shore at all; lavaFate (the price of a push into lava, note 563) counts the swim out and not the fifteen seconds of fire after, so in the Nether it says less than a touch costs; lineSays reads the top block down from twelve over the feet and saw "96 on ground" where the walk went down to the sea; the walk home is not a question (the legs, the unasked crossing), and a crouched walk along the lava's edge is the crouch rule's only where the edge is measured; none of this has run live.
581. *mid-242-ad-nether-3 (25586), "slain by Piglin" at 04:27:09 in an iron helmet and chestplate with no gold on, and mid-244-ag (25581), "slain by Zombie" at 04:43:17 in full iron at y 10: a pocket and a bunker priced for the mobs in sight while the ones that killed it walked up out of sight.* mid-242-ad-nether-3 at 04:26:56, full health, four piglins at 15 to 18 blocks (three crossbows, one sword, none in sight), chose seal at 0.56 after take_cover, rail_and_fight, bunker, fight and eat within forty seconds. The seal said "About 23 blocks to place here, some 14 seconds of building. About 0 damage from the mobs here in the next fifteen seconds this way, the 13.8 seconds of building included"; the sword piglin, 15.7 blocks off round the rock, was said only as "out of sight but about". Rebuilt from the record (the death frame's terrain less the pocket's own blocks, the mobs where the decision frame put them) the code after note 576 said the same, word for word. Why: the stance's figures count the mobs in sight, the one the danger check names (here the unseen crossbow piglin, which shoots nothing unseen) and biters out of sight within eight (note 542); a biter out of sight further off counted in nothing, however long the stance stood the bot still building. Piglins were not taken as neutral (no gold worn, so danger.js keeps them), nor did the pocket's price assume a side closed: it counted no biter at all. And the pocket went up nearest cell first (sealHere, after the side over a drop): the walls at the feet and head, then the whole roof last, though the ground south of the bot stood two up, level with the walls' top. Sixteen blocks were down by 04:27:07 (27 netherrack to 11); the piglin walked in along that ground and dropped into the bot's head cell through the roof not begun (y 42.8 there, then 41 in its own cell at 04:27:08), and hit for 6.7 three times, 20 to 13.3 to 6.6 to none; the roof's centre was the one cell it could not place, the piglin standing in it. mid-244-ag at 04:43:02, five zombies within ten blocks of a cave at y 10 (two in sight at 5.9 and 6.2, three out of sight at 8.0, 8.9 and 10.1), chose bunker at 0.43 over the fight at 0.36: the bunker said "about 3.6 seconds of digging ... About 8.9 damage ... At the doorway, one biter at a time, 2 zombies still reach it" where the fight read 13.2; the three past eight were not in it, and one at a time was said of zombies that could be at the bot before it was in. All five were: fourteen hits while it dug and stood (two and three at arm's length at once, one in its own cell), 20 to none; asked again at 14.5 and 6.2 the bunker already read 38.6 and 30.2, as the fight did. Now (survival.js farBiters, pocketPlan, pocketRace; combat-estimate stanceCost `far`): the biters out of sight past the eight, within the twenty-four a stance counts and their own follow range (a piglin's 16), with a way to the bot (walk-reach), are priced in every stance's building or digging where they can be at the bot before it is done, at their own speed and with their own blow (a piglin 8, its golden sword, 6.7 through that iron), and in the fight after once there; not where they cannot come in time (a second's step to footing, the meal). The cost says them ("Counted though out of sight, at the bot before the building is done at its own speed: the piglin 16 blocks off, in about 2.7 seconds"), and the blows that lead every stance (note 576) take them too, said as out of sight. The pocket is built as a player closes one against what comes: after the side over a drop, the cells a walker comes in by or strikes from (the eight round the bot at its feet and at its head, and the one over its head where a walker can stand level with the walls' top: ground two up beside the column or a step up just outside the walls), those nearest the soonest biter at its own speed first; then the floor's and the roof's corners. It is priced so: a biter that can be at the bot before those are shut stands in a gap or drops in, the pocket does not close on it, and it hits from then on; one after finds them shut. mid-242-ad-nether-3's pocket now goes up the side toward the piglin first and the cell over the head tenth (about 7 seconds at the 0.69 a block it laid), where it was fifteenth (about 10.3, and the piglin came at about 10.5 and stood in it); the ways in are shut after 15 blocks either way. It reads "the nearest piglin, 16 blocks off and out of sight, can be at the bot in about 2.7 seconds at its own speed ... The piglin can be there before they are shut ... About 82.5 damage ... (more than the bot has)". The bunker the same way: the biters at the bot before it is dug in are in the tunnel with it and fought together, the doorway holding back only those after; the shaft of dig_down the same, a biter at its top before the lid dropping in and staying. And the stance question says, where piglins are about and no gold is worn, why they go for the bot and what gold would take (state piglinsAndGold, survival.js piglinGoldSays): "a piglin leaves a player wearing any one piece of gold armour alone, save one angry with it (struck by that player, and the piglins near the one struck, for about 30 seconds); a piglin brute goes for a player whatever is worn", then the piece carried and that putting it on is one move, under half a second, and what it replaces, or that none is carried and golden boots take four ingots. Asked live (5 each through decide): the 04:26:56 question as recorded, seal 5 of 5; with the fixed text (the blows lead, the seal as rebuilt, the gold fact), pillar 5 of 5, seal 0.04; replay case piglin-seal-open-roof-242ad (expect pillar, fight_from_footing, fight or retreat; never seal). A sentence added to the stance's guidance for the new facts turned poisoned-by-witch-apple-offered from the apple to the retreat (1 and 0 of 5, 5 of 5 on main) and was taken out; the facts say themselves in the options and the state. No replay case for mid-244-ag: its snapshot's terrain stops at 2,000 blocks, six columns of the cave, and the bunker's rock is not in it. Tests (test/build-reach.test.js, each failing on the old code): the sword piglin out of sight 15.6 off prices the pocket past 20 and says when it can be there against when the ways in are shut, the fight here still at 0; the pocket's order (the side toward the biter first, the cell over the head before the roof's corners) and sealHere laying the blocks in it; a pocket whose ways shut before the biter can come says so and keeps it out; a corridor bunker with two zombies in sight and three round the bend prices all five as in with it, over the fight; the gold fact bare, with boots or a helmet carried, and not with gold on. survival.test.js's shaft case now has the zombie at the lip in with the bot (2.8 blocks, 0.58 seconds against the lid's 0.6). npm test 1850 of 1850, the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow; replay suite 43 of 44 with the known gap. Still open: mid-244-ag never swung while it dug and stood in its bunker (fourteen hits, the dig_in_bunker step going on at 0.7 health), not looked at here; a stance with a short setup (the pillar, the step to footing) does not count a biter out of sight further off that comes during its hold, where the pillar's top stands level with ground beside, and the replayed question's pillar reads 0 with that ground said; the armour rule (mob-policy wearBestArmour) wears golden boots in the Nether before iron but puts an iron helmet back over a golden one, so gold carried as a helmet is said but not kept on; the pocket's biter times are straight lines, not routes.
582. *mid-243-ad-nether-3 (25586), "tried to swim in lava" at 04:39:25, off its own span by a ghast's fireball, with notes 574 and 576 live.* The bot was walking back to its portal over its own diagonal span of cobbled deepslate and dirt at y 50 over the lava sea (its top at y 31), no natural ground within twenty blocks (the death snapshot's region file), a ghast forty-five to sixty-four blocks off to the south-east; it carried two oak planks, three oak logs, sixteen gravel, five warped wart blocks, no bow and no arrows. Read from the flight record: rail_and_fight was chosen at 04:39:01 (0.80) and 04:39:05 (0.63, "the 1 open side"), and by 04:39:06.7 no side at its feet was open over the drop (the rail was no longer offered): north and west of its cell were walled. At 04:39:12.8 every stance still said "one fireball that lands ... the bot's death" (shotOverEdge looked at the drop within three blocks, not at which way a push goes), and Jev answered none_good (0.43, take_cover 0.33 taken as the best listed), then again eleven times in two and a half seconds: each take_cover put its block in the ghast's line at (93, 51, 108), where a flame the first fireball had lit burned beside the planks of an earlier cover, and each was refused "Placement obstructed by fire" (work.js place). The fireball at 04:39:15.7 landed (18.5 to 15.1) and pushed the bot a tenth of a block, into its wall: the walls held. It set the bot alight in the fire at its feet; the body's way (the vitals fire reflex; no body_way answer is recorded) put a block of gravel in the fire's cell and stood on it (04:39:16.6), and then three more, at 17.0, 17.5 and 17.9: inFire still said "in fire" from the server's in-fire hurt of 04:39:16.46, counted for a second and a half wherever the body had gone since, and from the flame in the cover's cell beside the gravel it stood on, a level below its feet (fireNear looked from one below the feet), though the body's box begins at its feet. So the bot rose from y 51 to 55 on four gravel, above its own walls, open on three sides over a drop of 23. There rail_and_fight was chosen (0.57, hold_on_span 0.29), told six blocks, 3.6 seconds and the planks first, "Walled, a fireball that lands here costs its 3.4 damage and a push into the wall": nothing said that the bot stands open until the walls stand, and the ghast fires every three seconds. It was making the planks when the fireball landed 1.3 seconds in (15.7 to 12.3) and threw it off; in the lava from y 32, 13.3 to none in three seconds. What a player does against a ghast on a span, checked: off the span to ground (none within twenty); a bow (none carried); the shield (checked in the 26.1.2 jar, ServerExplosion.hurtEntities: the blast pushes by (1 - distance / 2) x the share of the body it sees x (1 - explosion knockback resistance) after the hurt, with no look at a raised shield, so a shield takes the fireball's own hit and not its blast's push, as the hold already says); a block in its line (a ghast fires only with a line); a wall on the side the push goes (a push goes away from the shooter). Now: fire and soul fire are replaced by a placed block, as the game has them (26.1.2 Blocks: registered replaceable), and not a cell a crafting table goes in (work.js). The server's in-fire word counts only until a way out of the fire has moved the body off the cell (vitals.js leftFire, set by the rise and the walk out), and flames a level below the feet are not fire the body is in, as the rise onto a block already said; so one block is put, not four. A push is priced by which way it goes (survival.js leeFirst: the sides facing away from the shooter): where every shooter that can push the bot has its sides walled at the feet, or ground with no drop beside them, every stance says it is walled toward the push ("one that lands costs its 3.4 damage and a push into the wall, not the fall"), the fireball's return is priced by its damage, and keep_working adds that off this cell the walls are behind it. The rail and the span's hold wall the sides a push goes toward first (railSpan) and say the seconds until those stand, the planks included, with the shooter's own rate of fire (a ghast one every 3 seconds while it has a line, a blaze a volley about every 9 landing by distance, the rest about every 2): "Until the wall on the side a push goes stands, about 3.4 seconds, the bot is open over the drop: ... so a fireball can all but surely land first, and one that lands before then is the push over the drop: the bot's death"; take_cover says the same until the first block in the line stands, and counts the planks the logs make, made first (it was not offered at 04:39:18.6 with three logs carried). Tests (test/ghast-span-build.test.js, on the span as the region file has it, each failing before): a block goes into a flame's cell; the cover offered from the logs alone with its 2.2 open seconds, 73 in 100; the rail's 3.4 seconds and "all but surely", before its walled price; on a one-wide span the hold's 1.2 seconds, 40 in 100; the rail walls north then west with the ghast south-east; walled north and west every stance says the push into the wall, and with west open again the fall; after one rise onto gravel the bot is not in fire, no second rise is offered, and a fresh hurt counts again. What held it: the stay said no clock and no cost (at night a stay says the minutes to dawn and in the Overworld by day the daylight going by; in the Nether it said nothing), nothing of what the pocket had been sealed against or where that was, nothing of how the mobs outside had moved while it waited; and the leave was priced as fighting every mob heard ("Out among them, fighting them all is estimated at about 13.2 seconds and 32.8 damage, from 20 health (more than the bot has)"). Now a sealed pocket keeps what the wait has been (src/pocket-wait.js, survival.state.pocketWait, begun when the bot is first seen sealed in, a new wait after half a minute out of it): when it began, what it was sealed against (the stance that dug or sealed it within two minutes before, with the mobs it was chosen against as they were then: the stance record now keeps them, name, distance, in sight), and each mob about within 24 with where it began, its nearest and farthest, and whether it has had the bot in sight, forgotten a minute after it is last heard. pocket_next's stay says the minutes in this pocket, what it was sealed against and where that is now ("that ghast is not about now"), each mob outside about a minute or more with how its distance has gone ("the piglin 13 blocks off, about for 5 minutes of the wait, 13 blocks off all that time, never nearer", or "come from 15 to 7 blocks off", and whether it has had the bot in sight), that outside the Overworld no daylight comes so a stay there ends only when the bot opens the pocket, that at full health by day staying heals nothing, and past five minutes the rung's time without a new best (the ledger's, note 571); the leave says what it was sealed against and the mobs the same way; with every mob outside held off out of sight a minute or more, the fight with them all is said as what it costs "should they all come at the bot at once", not "out among them". The state carries it (pocketSoFar), and the turn's own claim says it ("In a sealed pocket, 5 minutes so far, sealed against a ghast in sight 25 blocks off (dig down); that ghast is not about now"); the wait's report says "Waiting in the sealed pocket" where no daylight is waited for. Nothing is decided by it: stay and leave are Jev's as before. 583. *mid-242-ae-nether-2-fortress-1 (25583), the blaze rods set aside thirty-two seconds into a trial begun from a fortress stage's save, told "Every way the find fortress had from here has been tried and come to nothing"; and the pearl step it went to, which then failed every two seconds for as long as it ran, in six trials from fortress stage saves (mid-242-ae-nether-3-fortress-1 and -2, mid-244-ad-nether-3, mid-242-aa-nether-4, mid-242-ac-nether-2-fortress-3).* The trial began at 04:47:12. In the thirty-two seconds before rung_progress, fortress_leg was answered seven times and fortress_approach three. What came to nothing: back_to_fortress at 04:47:29, back in 0.3 seconds; the legs, each ended at the first cell by the search's own rests ("out of blocks (0 carried)"); and one walk of the fortress's floors. The walk went to a floor at (-106, 55, 106), two blocks above the bot with no way to it: thirty passes in three seconds and "No measurable progress" at 04:47:36, 04:47:41 and 04:47:44, all the same walk. It was not every way. seek_fortress_height (0.15 to 0.17 when offered), restock_blocks (the answer to "out of blocks") and return_for_blocks were never tried. Nothing carried over from the save: the save was taken at 04:26, before note 571's ledger landed at 04:38, so it held no ledger, and goal.struggles was 0, so the three failures were this trial's. Two things in it were read by the wall clock, though: the stall's detour log ("chosen for this same stall 1 time in the last 30 minutes" on mine_nearby, a choice from before the save) and runClock's 34 minutes. A saved ledger would have been read the same way, with the twenty-one minutes the save lay unplayed lapsing every rest and escalation in it. Instant failures, holds and cancellations were each counted as a try that came to nothing. An answer the survival layer cut short was settled blocked when its question came up again. What escalated: at the first failure, the answer being carried out had been settled as getting somewhere, so persist asked the stall's question ("differently", which walked 0 of 8 blocks). At the second failure, persist sent the failure to fortress_leg ("[escalate] step -> fortress leg"). But the fortress map still held the floor to walk to. A walk ending 2.0 blocks from it counted as reached (three was the mark), so the floor was never passed over, and the next pass walked it again without asking fortress_leg. At the third failure, fortress_leg was still owed the second one, and note 571's rule climbed past a parent not asked since, straight to rung_progress. There the situation said every way had been tried, keep_at_it said "Nothing tried lately is in the ledger" (read by the step's key, step:find_fortress, where the ledger keeps the rung's, step:rung:obtain_blaze_rods), and set_aside_rung said nothing of how long or how much. Jev set it aside at 0.45. mid-242-ae-nether-3-fortress-1 was the same through the ways in. A hold on fortress_approach at 04:52:07.5 escalated to fortress_leg, the next pass asked fortress_approach again (walk_route, the one way left, twice), and 0.9 seconds later that went past fortress_leg to the rung, set aside. The pearls: the step had one warped forest remembered, seen across the lava sea (104, 127 and 96 blocks off in the three logs). The walk there came no nearer and its trip was set aside half an hour. warped_pearls then returned at once on every pass, since the forest still counted as known while goToLandmark refused its walk: twenty passes a second, "No measurable progress" every two seconds, persist, the stall's question. That question's answers were recorded as waits, because its never-held status (note 570) made them waiting in the ledger, so none came to nothing, none rested, and nothing above it was asked: 24 rounds in eight minutes in mid-244-ad-nether-3. Its "33 set_aside_rung" is status lines repeating the last decision: rung_progress was asked twice and set aside once. Now, in general (tried.js): an escalation to a parent that is still owed an asking goes past it only when that parent is spent (every option it offered at its last asking has come to nothing from here, kept as `offered` with each answer), or when it has been owed twice without being asked (a plan held without a question), and what was passed over is said (passedOver). A repeat hold (note 560/570) rests the answers it held and asks the question with the ways left, escalating only once they rest too (decisions/index.js). The work that holds an answer of a question ends it and asks the question when an escalation is owed to it (tried.owed): in the fortress search, the walk of the floors, a leg, a way to a place, a wait by a spawner, a patrol and a restock end, and the leg's question is asked with what failed. Off the fortress's floors, going back is among the legs, said as "its ways in from here came to nothing", rather than the ways in being asked again (mob-hunt.js). A floor not stood on (over 1.75 blocks off) is passed over after one walk. The stall's answers are not waits: they come to nothing and rest like any way, judged by the rung, with a gain in what is carried counted as getting somewhere. An answer cut short by the survival layer or a cancellation is settled "cut", said, and not counted. A saved ledger is moved on by the time the save lay unplayed when a goal is taken up again (tried.resumed, session.js), so a trial begun from a stage's save reads its tries, rests, escalations and the rung's budget by its own clock. The rung's question says how much the rung has had (workedOn: "in 0.5 minutes on it: 3 answers given, 3 coming to nothing, 0 getting somewhere; the step failed 3 times; not yet tried from here: fortress leg (asked 11 seconds ago): seek fortress height, restock blocks, return for blocks"). This goes in its situation and on set_aside_rung. So does what setting it aside goes on with, read from a copy of the goal with the rung left: the next rung and its step; for a place its work walks to first (the warped forest, a bastion), how far and how the last walk there ended or that it is set aside and why, and what the pathfinder's half-second survey from here found; and what the ledger holds of that rung from here (setAsideGoesOnWith). The stall's tried and notOffered are read by the rung's work key. The pearl step (warped-pearls.js): a forest whose walk rests is not the forest walked to. With none open, the sweep looks for another, and with the sweep resting the ladder goes on the old way. A walk that sets the trip aside is the step's own failure, thrown with where and why ("The walk to the warped forest at (153, -217), 266 blocks off, came no nearer: No path to the goal!; that walk rests half an hour") and written in the ledger. The walk's end is kept with the place (lastWalk) and in the trip's set-aside reason (exploration.js). Tests: tried.test.js, the recorded thirty seconds of mid-242-ae-nether-2-fortress-1 through decide and persist: the failed walk escalates to fortress_leg, which is asked at the next pass with the failure said, back_to_fortress resting and seek_fortress_height, restock_blocks and return_for_blocks offered untried, and the rung is not asked. The same with a step that never asks: the failure is sent to fortress_leg twice, the third goes to the rung, said as passed over, and the rung's question says 0.5 minutes, what came to nothing and the three ways not yet tried; set_aside_rung says it goes on with the pearls, the forest 114 blocks off with its walk set aside and why, and that the work begins with a search for another. The stall's answers to the pearl step failing every two seconds (mid-244-ad-nether-3's order: differently, recover_5, recover_4, ...) come to nothing, rest, and escalate to rung_progress within sixteen failures. A saved ledger read after twenty-one minutes unplayed keeps its rests and its escalation owed. Answers cut short three times do not rest. A hold with a way left takes that way, not the question above. mob-hunt.test.js: an owed escalation on the floors asks fortress_leg with no walk; off the floors it asks fortress_leg, not fortress_approach, with going back said as its ways having come to nothing; a floor within three blocks the walk cannot reach is walked once, not six times. warped-pearls.test.js: the forest whose walk failed is not walked to again at once, the failure is thrown with its why and kept in the ledger, the next pass sweeps for another, and with the sweep resting the ladder goes home. Each fails without its change. Changed tests: the note 573 hold test (the held answer rests and the way left is taken, not escalated); the ten-askings test (the plan owed once is asked again, passed over at the second); the note 570 goTo test (the streak covers every way offered, so the hold leaves none). npm test 1866 of 1866 (merged with main at 519b4d7, notes 580 and 581), the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow; replay suite 43 of 44 with the known gap. Still open: the hook that ends a held answer when its question is owed is in the fortress search only; elsewhere a plan held without a question is passed over after two failures, said. Why the 04:47:33 back_to_fortress was settled as getting somewhere is not found here (the stall watch's progress mark is the likely source). The stall's detour log and runClock still read the wall clock across a save. None of this has run live.
588. *mid-242-ae-nether-2-fortress-2 (25600), the pearls stalled for ten minutes after the rods were set aside, with Jev answering none_good at every asking: stillness_detour at 0.45, 0.57, 0.63, 0.71 and 0.75 (each taken as the best listed: recover_1, cross_toward, differently, mine_nearby), upkeep at 0.57, and the stall watch saying "turning between tunnel and warped search 4 times in 8 seconds" at (-41.6, 35, 81.5).* The rods were set aside at 05:16:43, before note 583 was live, for thirty minutes. From the archived goal and the flight: the bot stood on a ledge of its own stairs, (-43..-36, 35, 81) and (-36, 35, 81..90), "no way down at a walk", every cell below it refused ("Refusing to open a drop beside the feet"), carrying five planks and no other block, no gold (its 26 nuggets went with a death at 05:01), no pearls, no rods. Both warped forests known, (-77, -4) 95 blocks off and (-61, -147) 231 off, were set aside half an hour ("the walk there came no nearer than before"). The fortress at (-70, 140) was 66 blocks off and 10 up; the portal back at (17, 58, 8) 94. What the stall's question offered: another way (a walk off that had got 0 of 8 blocks 24 times, "No path to the goal!"), cross_toward (one block, then out of blocks), mine_nearby and the recovery adviser's moves. What it did not offer, and what Jev was saying was missing: the rods it had set aside; any other route to the pearls; and working free of the ledge. The pearl routes were never Jev's. The ladder (game-progress.js) takes one by rule: barter when gold is carried and a piglin is in view, a bastion's gold, the warped forest (a known one, else a sweep), then home; and in the Overworld, short of rods, it went straight back into the Nether, so the Overworld's endermen were no route at all while the rods were short. set_aside_rung is offered in the stall's question only for a rung that may wait, and nothing offered a rung set aside back. Working free (unstuck.js) is offered when walks are failing, read from the stall's error and goal.lastError; the walk off's own "No path to the goal!" was said at every asking and not read, and walksFailed did not know mineflayer's "No path to the goal" as a walk failing. The flip: with both forests resting, warped_pearls swept for a third. Each leg's walk failed at once, the sweep dug a staircase toward the leg, and tunnelStep, not handed the sweep's step, named the step 'tunnel'; the next pass named it 'warped_search' again. Each staircase failed ("no floor to step onto (a gap, for a span or a pillar)") and was swallowed into search.lastError, the pass returned as work done, the heading turned every three tries: 233 tries, no leg, and the sweep would have run its quarter of an hour. Now, in general: a rung set aside and still resting that the ladder would take up now were its rest lifted (game-progress.js takeBackRungs, read from a copy of the goal with that one rest cleared, so the shield left for the Nether is not offered in the Nether) is offered in the stall's and the rung's questions, take_up_<rung>, said with why and when it was set aside, the minutes its rest has left, the rung in hand waiting meanwhile, the minutes worked on it, where its work is (the rods: the nearest fortress, how far and up, whether the search has left it) with the pathfinder's half-second survey from here, and what the ledger holds of it; chosen, the rest is lifted and its clock starts afresh (takeBackRung). While the pearls are the rung in hand, every route to them from here is an option or said as not one (src/pearl-routes.js): each known warped forest whose walk rests, taken up again (pearls_forest_N: distance, height, the rest and why, the last walk and how it ended, blocks carried, the survey); the sweep for another forest when it rests and no known forest is open (pearls_search); a barter walk toward a piglin with gold carried and none within thirty-two (pearls_barter); back to the Overworld for its endermen (pearls_overworld: the portal trip, the Overworld's hour on arrival, the line's lava, the survey, the rods carried), held half an hour or until the pearls are carried (goal.pearlRoute): from the Nether the ladder returns, and in the Overworld the pearls come before the Nether again (patrol, a cleric's trade) with the rods short; and from the Overworld on that route, back to the Nether's forests (pearls_nether). A barter with no gold and no bastion known is said in stalled.pearlRoutesNotOffered with why. The walk off's failure is read for working free, and "No path to the goal" is a walk failing. The sweep's staircase is the sweep's phase (warped-pearls.js hands tunnelStep the warped_search step, as obsidian's tunnel is), so the flip watch sees one step, not two trading the turn; each try keeps the walk's and the staircase's why; a heading that came to nothing three times is kept with where it was tried from; every heading come to nothing from within eight blocks is the sweep spent from there, rested half an hour and thrown as the step's failure with each heading's why ("The sweep for a warped forest got nowhere from (-41, 35, 82): every heading came to nothing (south: the walk failed (No path to the goal!), the staircase failed (...no floor to step onto...)); ...; the sweep rests half an hour"), written in the ledger. Shown on the recorded states: the five stillness_detour askings rebuilt from the flight with the goal archived at 05:26 and the options the code offers now added (the probe, with every survey from the ledge answering no path, as its walks had). 05:25:26, recorded none_good 0.75: work_free 5 of 5 (0.37 to 0.44; pearls_overworld 0.28 to 0.33, take_up_obtain_blaze_rods 0.14 to 0.20, none_good 0.05 to 0.06). Before working free was offered, with the surveys: pearls_overworld 4 and the rods 1, none_good 0.20 to 0.24; without the surveys: pearls_overworld 5 of 5, none_good 0.10 to 0.13. 05:21:29 (0.45), 05:23:03 (cross_toward 0.57), 05:23:52 (0.63) and 05:24:33 (0.71), without the surveys: pearls_overworld 19 of 20 and the rods once, none_good 0.09 at most. Added to the replay suite as pearls-stalled-rods-set-aside-25600 (expected: working free, the rods back, or a pearl route; forbidden: none_good and differently), 5 of 5 work_free. Tests: pearl-routes.test.js, from the recorded 05:23:03 goal: the stall's question offers the rods back with 24 minutes left and the fortress 68 blocks off and 10 up and no way found there, both forests with their rests, last walk and surveys, the Overworld with the portal trip, no barter and why, working free once the walk off found no path, and the sweep when it rests; taking the rods back lifts their rest and the ladder is on the rods; a forest taken up is walked to; the Overworld chosen returns from the Nether and hunts pearls in the Overworld with the rods short, and pearls_nether drops it. warped-pearls.test.js: from the ledge, every walk "No path" and every staircase "no floor", the staircase is handed the sweep's step, twelve tries (three a heading) end in the sweep resting and the step's failure with each heading's why, in the ledger; elsewhere the sweep is afresh. Each fails without the change. Changed test: "a walk that fails at once is not a leg" now has its staircase make ground, since a spot every heading has failed from is now the sweep spent. npm test 1883 of 1883, the same under JEV_ARBITER=shadow; replay suite 47 of 48 with the known gap. Still open: why the upkeep question (none_good 0.57 at 05:22:52) found its nearest netherrack 13 blocks off across a drop while the bot stood on a netherrack ledge: the floor cells are refused as drops and the ledge has no walls, so it may be true, but it is not shown here. Whether working free gets off the ledge (unstuck.js, move by move) is not tried here. The barter walk and the Overworld route have not run live, and none of this has run live.
586. *mid-242-ae (25598) and mid-242-ac-nether-3 (25591): the work had the turn with a mob at its reach or a shooter by a drop, and Jev was never asked; with mid-242-af-nether-3's ghast, and two more spear piglins (mid-242-ac-nether-3-fortress-2, mid-242-af-nether-1).* mid-242-ae, 04:47:53 to 04:47:58: a golden-spear piglin had taken it from 20 to 7.5; the fight's run at it (the piglin four blocks off, two below, past a two-block drop) went nowhere, and the charge set it aside for twenty seconds as out of reach (bot._unreachable). Retreat found no way, eat was chosen and eaten at 04:47:56. The eat stance holds its mobs for its own 1.6 seconds (stanceMobs), and after it nothing claimed the piglin: immediateThreat left it be on the charge's word ("out of reach both ways", trial 66), though the charge's failing says only that the bot had no way to the piglin, and walk-reach, which judges the piglin's way to the bot, was never the one that set it aside. With survival claiming nothing, the arbiter gave the work the turn alone ("held for survival: missed one look", then "gave work cross_toward (single)", "gave work recover_before_combat (single)"): combat_kit, upkeep and a craft ran, and the watch saw nothing, since its mob alert at arm's length is only at six health or under (7.7 here) and the piglin was no newcomer. It came up to 1.3 across and 1.6 below and speared the bot twice, 7.7 to 1 to 0. mid-242-ac-nether-3, 05:05:39 to 05:07:32: turn_priority gave the work the turn over the night hunt at 10.6 health, and the work's step was a rest's hold (holdForRest, "holding step:return_to_portal until its rest ends"), one turn 57 seconds long on a span with a nineteen-block drop beside it; a crossbow piglin came on from 28.5 blocks to 6.4 in that time, and nothing looks at the claims inside a layer's run: the arbiter rules only between runs, and the hold's own check came every five seconds (its sleep). The arrow landed at 05:07:30 (10.6 to 7.5) and the push put the bot over, 56 to 37, dead. The crossbow piglin was counted (a shooter in sight within sixteen is a threat); what was missing was anyone looking. mid-242-af-nether-3 (the coordinator's third, 05:37:30): keep_working was answered at 05:36:53 for a ghast 34 blocks off, the work's rest hold kept the turn past its fifteen seconds, and the ghast's fireball from 41 blocks put the bot ten blocks down into lava. And the spear itself, from the 26.1.2 server jar: a spear's AttackRange is 2 to 4.5 in a player's hand and half that in a mob's (mob factor 0.5), and a mob's blow lands where its own box widened sideways by its reach, not up or down, touches the target (Mob.isWithinMeleeAttackRange): 2.25 past the box, about 2.8 blocks straight ahead and 4 on the diagonal. A spear piglin fights by PiglinAi's SpearApproach (to within ten), SpearAttack (runs in with the spear raised until within two, landing its hit on the way, then walks to a spot six or seven off) and SpearRetreat, and comes again. Spear damage is not in bypasses_shield and a spear's Weapon component disables no shield: a shield raised facing it takes the hit. Its hits in these deaths were the piglin's own blow, a golden spear adding the material's bonus, none: seven hits, 5.7 to 6.7 through eight armour points (eight before), where every stance said "12.2 a blow" (SPEAR_HIT, thirteen, from a zombie villager's iron spear, note 179) and "2 blows end the bot from 17.8"; three did. mid-242-ac-nether-3-fortress-2 fought one a block below at 3.0 to 3.3 blocks: two swings in 3.5 seconds, the stand between them facing it with the shield down, three hits, 17.8 to none. mid-242-af-nether-1 answered none of these twice (0.44, 0.48) at one by a three-block edge into lava: no stance met a spear holder as a player does, and its one hit that landed knocked the bot 0.7 of a block, over the edge. Now the rule is one: a mob come to its reach of the bot, or something that can push it with a deadly drop beside it, is asked about whenever the turn was given without it. danger.js: atItsReach (the jar's geometry for a spear holder, arm's length for the rest), atReach (those in sight or within two, not the hunt's claimed kind), deadlyDropBeside and pushOverDrop (pushersAbout, note 541, with a deadly drop; those Jev chose to leave be wait out his fifteen seconds). The arbiter's ruling and the holder's turn record what they were given against (reach, push); a held ruling for any layer but survival is asked again when either comes ("a mob came within its reach of the bot", "something that can push the bot is about, a deadly drop beside it"), and the watch, four times a second, stops a holder that is not survival's for one its turn was given without, once (the holder then counts it). Survival claims both: the mob at its reach (atArm now by atItsReach, a spear's reach too), and a pusher by a deadly drop that is no threat by the counts (a ghast or blaze in sight past its sixteen or twenty-two), each claim saying the drop (facts.edge, said on turn_priority). A walker a charge could not reach is no longer left be on that word: its way to the bot is walk-reach's (cannotGetToTheBot); the charge's set-aside stays for the mobs walk-reach does not judge (a spider climbs). walk-reach touches from a spear's reach when a walker holds one, and says a spear piglin's reach, not "it hits at arm's length only". holdForRest waits a quarter second at a time with the task's check between, so a preemption stops it within 250 ms, not five seconds. The spear's fight: combat-estimate prices a piglin's spear at the eight seen (SPEAR_SEEN) and says its way (SPEAR_WAYS); a new stance, shield_the_charge, offered where a spear holder that can get to the bot is in sight within sixteen and a shield is carried: face it with the shield up, strike it as it comes within the sword's reach on its run in, the shield's down moments said and left out of the figure, the blocks said as the game's rules not yet seen in the bot's own fights; every stance that leaves the bot open by a deadly drop says the spear's knock over it (spearOverEdge), the rail that its knock stops at the wall, the shield stance that a blocked hit knocks it nowhere; the fight's hold facing a mob out of reach raises the shield. Probed against the live model, 5 each: the 05:32:22 stance as now asked, shield_the_charge 5 of 5 (0.93 to 0.94; recorded none of these 0.44, taking the fight); turn_priority at 04:47:56.9 as now asked (the piglin survival's claim beside the work), survival 5 of 5 (0.95 to 0.96; recorded: never asked, the work given the turn alone); both added to the replay suite.
